Cleetus · Internal Handoff · 13 Aug 2026

Cleetusruns on a Mac Studio in Grayson's room, not on somebody's API

Two programs sharing one model, one set of agent briefs and one vault. A Cloudflare Pages app whose brain is a 33B mixture-of-experts model on his own hardware, reached over a private tunnel — and cleetusd, the same assistant running as a process on the Mac with the disk, the shell and the cameras. Claude is the backup, not the engine.

The number that matters

A morning brief — calendar, net worth, holdings, weather, tax deadlines — written by a model in his own room in 7.1 seconds, with Claude never contacted. Financial and health data stops leaving the building. Still true today: this morning's arrived at 00:19 ET. (An earlier revision claimed this brief had been dead since 19 May. It confused it with a separate Slack/markdown job that really is dead — sections 14 and 18.)

Three things are waiting on you

Everything else in this document is either working or already fixed. These three cannot be done from here, and each one is blocking something real.

whatevidencewhat it unblocks
Re-link the Microsoft account NOT connected — the endpoint reports connected:false. Down in 59 of the last 64 health readings and never once a flap. Outlook mail and calendar. The only integration that has been red continuously for days.
Open the iOS app once registered, but the last push reached NO devices (no_devices) — and that last attempt was 9 August. Nothing has been pushed since. Every notification. The device token is stale, so a phone is registered but unreachable.
Check the camera cable or hub RuntimeError: No camera matching 'c920'. BOTH webcams have left the USB bus — the BRIO too, so studio-locate is blind while reporting healthy. Twice today. The air trackpad, which refuses to start without it. Restarting will not help; the webcam has to be there.

Nothing else needs a decision. The improve loop is running on its own schedule and declined its last slot rather than invent work; the two commits it did ship are reviewed in sections 99 and 100.

02Where everything lives

Cleetus is spread across a dozen locations because several of them are enforced by macOS and Cloudflare. ~/Desktop/Cleetus is a hub of symlinks into all of them.

WhatPathMovable?
cleetusd — the agent on the Mac~/cleetusdyes
Its memory: runs, skills, per-agent facts~/cleetus-memoryyes — but never into iCloud, see traps
Web app + Pages Functions~/cleetusv2yes, it's a git repo
Agent briefs (read by BOTH halves)~/cleetusv2/brain/agents/*.mdyes — 18 files
Secrets mirror (90 keys)~/Documents/Claude/Projects/Cleetus V2/cleetus.envyes, nothing auto-reads it
Desk vision + air trackpad + the light~/studio-locateseparate project
Browser harness for the browse tool~/cleetus-webyes — currently not running
STEAP scripts the scheduled jobs need~/.claude/scripts/home dir is itself a git repo
Model weights, 38 GB~/.ollama-modelsonly by editing the LaunchAgent
All 15 launch agents~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.cleetus.*.plistno — launchd requires this path
Auth proxy config/opt/homebrew/etc/Caddyfileno — brew services reads it here
Tunnel config + credentials/etc/cloudflared/no — root daemon
Brain / vaultObsidian Cleetus vault in iCloudno
Logs~/Library/Logs/cleetus-*.logno

Why it isn't literally one folder

launchd only loads agents from ~/Library/LaunchAgents and daemons from /Library/LaunchDaemons. Homebrew services read /opt/homebrew/etc. A root daemon can't read credentials out of a user Desktop folder. Moving those breaks the machine. ~/Desktop/Cleetus therefore holds symlinks, which behave like the real thing when you open them but leave the system intact.

03The stack

There are now two Cleetuses and they are not the same program. One is a website that calls a model. The other is a process on the Mac that can open your files. They share the model, the agent briefs and the vault; they share no code.

browser / iOS app  →  cleetusai.com
        ↓
  Cloudflare Pages Functions      the whole /api surface, session-gated
        ↓
  _lib/llm.js                     provider layer · LLM_PROVIDER decides local vs Claude
        ↓
  cloudflared  llm.cleetusai.com  →  the Mac Studio
        ↓
  Caddy :11435                    checks the bearer, rewrites Host to loopback
        ↓
  Ollama :11434                   laguna-xs-2.1:q8_0 resident, never unloaded


  you, at this Mac  →  http://127.0.0.1:8767          the control deck
        ↓
  cleetusd                        21 agents · 35 tools · full disk · shell
        ↓
  Ollama :11434 direct            no tunnel, no Caddy, no edge

  (and from anywhere)  →  me.cleetusai.com  →  Caddy :8783  →  cleetusd :8767

Every port on this machine

PortWhatGateExposed?
11434Ollamanone — loopback Host onlyno

8782Caddy → cleetus-webbearernot in tunnel ingress
8783Caddy → cleetusdbearer (its own token)me.cleetusai.com

cleetusd is the most dangerous thing on the tunnel, by a distance

studio-locate exposes a camera and cleetus-web drives a browser. cleetusd has read/write on the whole disk and an unrestricted shell. The bearer is the only thing between that and anyone who learns the hostname, so it gets its own token rather than sharing one with a service of lesser consequence. The doctor asserts the negative — me.cleetusai.com must answer 401 — because that is the check that can actually fail.

The browser gate, and why it is not just "is it loopback"

A browser cannot attach an Authorization header to a top-level navigation, so the deck could never be opened if the bearer were the only door. A short allowlist of routes is admitted on the strength of isLocalBrowser() instead. That function checks loopback and the absence of forwarding headers, because cloudflared also connects from 127.0.0.1 — a plain loopback check would admit the entire internet the day this went behind the tunnel.

The cleetusd surface

Routes on :8767. The starred ones are also reachable by a local browser with no bearer, because a browser cannot attach an Authorization header to a navigation.

The 54 tools. read_file · write_file · edit_file · list_dir · search_files · find_files · run_shell · vault_search · vault_read · remember_fact · save_skill · check_access · cloud_api · desk_light · web_open · web_read · web_act · web_pending · send_email · look · who_is_there · learn_face · known_faces · watch · detect · list_repos · repo_status · github · clone_repo · list_secrets · get_secret · save_secret · forget_secret · recall_chat · read_chat · recent_work · health_report · scheduled_jobs · generate_image · generate_video · list_media_models · list_references · face_search · find_security_skill · read_security_skill · find_ble_skill · read_ble_skill · save_reference · room_sense · lights_read · lights_set · plugs_read · plugs_set · room_alarm. There is no path allowlist, deliberately — the whole point was that he can open anything on this machine. Eleven aliases map the names the model guesses (shell, cat, ls, grep…) onto the real ones.

Command line. bin/ask.mjs talk to him without the server · bin/doctor.mjs the health report · bin/teach.mjs hand a failure to Claude and keep what comes back · bin/train.mjs write each agent a standing brief · bin/improve.mjs one pass of the self-improvement loop · bin/flights-push.mjs sweep the sky from the house.

Tests. 11 files, 34 tests: node --test ~/cleetusd/test/*.test.mjs. They cover plumbing, the improve-loop guards, the local-browser gate, dossier handling, per-agent memory, brief loading, the desk light and what counts as a failed run. Note the directory form (node --test test/) does not work — pass the glob.

The self-improvement loop, and what stops it

Agreed shape: full auto to main, revert on red. It takes a baseline, makes one change, compares health, and reverts itself if anything got worse. New failures only — pre-existing ones do not count against it. It refuses to run at all if any of these hold:

  • a STOP-IMPROVING file exists in ~/cleetus-memory (it does today);
  • the working tree is dirty — uncommitted work is not the loop's to build on;
  • the baseline is already red, because it could not then tell its own damage from existing damage;
  • the daily cap of 3 has been reached.

It can never touch src/improve.mjs, bin/improve.mjs, or anything matching .env — a loop that can edit its own brakes has none.

The web app surface

~100 Pages Functions under functions/api/, session-gated: money (plaid/*, schwab/*, ledger/*), health (fitness/*, nutrition/*), tax (tax/*), calendar and mail (google/*, microsoft/mail), plus cleetus, brief, flights, flights-ingest, cleetusd (a proxy to this Mac), skills, webauthn/* and the poker, space and music endpoints.

Pages: index (the deck) · body (the anatomical agent picker, three peelable layers) · flights · fitness · nutrition · music · space · solar · sky · timeline · hub · me · brew · dashboard · login · setup.

Environment

LLM_PROVIDER      openai          # "anthropic" reverts everything, instantly
LLM_BASE_URL      https://llm.cleetusai.com/v1
LLM_MODEL         laguna-xs-2.1:q8_0
LOCAL_GATE_MODEL  lfm2.5:8b
LLM_API_KEY       <64 hex>        # must match /opt/homebrew/etc/Caddyfile
LLM_FALLBACK      1
LLM_TIMEOUT_MS    60000           # under Cloudflare's 100 s edge cutoff
CLEETUSD_TOKEN    <64 hex>        # cleetusd's own bearer — NOT the same as LLM_API_KEY
ANTHROPIC_JOBS    (unset)         # comma list to pin jobs back to Claude

Cloudflare Pages binds env vars at deploy time. Changing one in the dashboard does nothing until the next build — push an empty commit.

04Running it

Start here, always

node ~/cleetusd/bin/doctor.mjs — 20 checks, each one a fault that has actually happened here and announced itself in no way at all. Several assert the negative (the tunnel must refuse an unauthenticated request; orphaned ffmpeg count must be zero; every launch agent's program must exist), because those are the ones that catch a regression rather than confirm a happy path. It found the shared-camera bug on its first run and ten dead services on the day the launch-agent check was added.

# the local agent
launchctl kickstart -k gui/$(id -u)/com.cleetus.cleetusd
open http://127.0.0.1:8767            # the control deck
node ~/cleetusd/bin/ask.mjs "read my calendar and tell me about tomorrow"
node --test ~/cleetusd/test/*.test.mjs # 8 files, 17 tests

# the model
launchctl kickstart -k gui/$(id -u)/com.cleetus.ollama
ollama ps                              # resident? 100% GPU? UNTIL Forever?
tail -f ~/Library/Logs/cleetus-ollama.err.log

# the cameras
launchctl kickstart -k gui/$(id -u)/com.cleetus.airpad          # C920, air trackpad
curl -s 127.0.0.1:8765/api/state | python3 -m json.tool         # BRIO, studio-locate

# the desk light
~/studio-locate/.venv/bin/python ~/studio-locate/litra_cli.py state
~/studio-locate/.venv/bin/python ~/studio-locate/litra_cli.py brightness 60

# the auth proxy
brew services restart caddy
caddy validate --config /opt/homebrew/etc/Caddyfile --adapter caddyfile

# the tunnel
sudo launchctl kickstart -k system/com.cloudflare.cloudflared
sudo tail -f /Library/Logs/com.cloudflare.cloudflared.err.log

# deploy — the token is in cleetus.env, no manual push needed
git -c http.extraheader="AUTHORIZATION: basic $(printf 'x-access-token:%s' "$GITHUB_TOKEN" | base64)" push origin main

Reading /api/health

The local_llm pill reports up and warm separately, because a cold model answers a version check instantly and still costs 20 s on the next real call. served and fallbacks are cumulative counters — that is the only honest answer to "is it really using the local model".

The 15 launch agents

Three are always-on and healthy: ollama, cleetusd, airpad, plus flights which self-loops. Ten are dead — section 14. Note that most of those ten are scheduled or interval jobs, so not running is their normal state and cannot tell you anything; that is precisely why the doctor checks whether the program file exists rather than whether the job is up.

05How it learns — five layers

Two of these live in the cloud app and three on the Mac. They are separate stores with separate lifetimes, and conflating them is how a fact gets written somewhere nothing reads.

LayerHoldsWritten byLive when
brain/cleetus-brain.mdidentity, voiceGrayson, in Obsidianon push
brain/agents/<id>.mdper-agent standing rulesGrayson + the Opus 5 training passon push · read by BOTH halves
app_kv agent_notes_<id>rules learned from corrections[LEARN] sentinelnext call
app_kv skillsprocedures learned from rescuesClaude, when laguna failsnext call
~/cleetus-memory/runs, skills, per-agent facts, MEMORY.mdcleetusd, continuouslyimmediately

Who remembers what

A fact told to a specialist is remembered by that specialist; a fact told to the front door is remembered by everyone. Where a fact lands follows who he was talking to, which is the distinction a naive share-everything design gets wrong — the skin agent should not have to read your poker history to answer a question about your forehead, and the nutrition agent genuinely does need to know what the fitness agent was told.

How a skill is born

You cannot watch how another model reasoned — you only see its output. So Cleetus does not infer the lesson, it asks the teacher to write it. When laguna fails and Claude takes over, Claude gets one extra call: "write the procedure a smaller model should follow next time — steps, tools in order, and the pitfall that probably caused this."

Applied with no confirmation, because skills live in app_kv: live on the next request, and one row to delete. That reversibility is what makes auto-apply reasonable. Code changes are deliberately excluded — the LLM has a fallback, the website does not.

What counts as a failure — and why it matters

Every failure fires the cloud teacher, so an over-eager heuristic quietly converts a local assistant back into a metered one. looksFailed() is narrow on purpose: silence, or a refusal about something actually in reach. "I don't have the ability to place purchases on Amazon" is true and correct, and it used to be filed as a failure and sent to Claude to be fixed. It cannot be fixed. It is not broken.

Guards

  • Retrieval, not dump. Only matching skills are injected, so the prompt is the same size at 40 skills as at 3. Stopwords matter: an early version matched "what" and retrieved everything.
  • Scoring. If the local model fell back while holding a skill, that skill takes a strike. Under 40% after 4 attempts it retires itself.
  • A cap of 40, evicting the worst performer rather than the oldest.
  • Audit. GET /api/skills, DELETE /api/skills?id=, and /skills on the deck.

The 20 agents

Body: hair · skin · muscle · nutrition · fitness   Presentation: fashion · redesign   Money and work: deals · finance · stocks · tax · books · booking   Making things: writing · image · music · builder   Daily: brief · poker · and cleetus himself, the generalist that routes.

Each declares needs — the context it is entitled to before it answers. All 18 briefs were rewritten by Opus 5. Before that pass, cleetusd carried 20 inline briefs while the web app read 19 markdown files and cleetusd never opened them, so training would have gone into files the daemon ignored.

06Traps — every expensive lesson, all sessions

The through-line: every fault in this system has been silent. Not one announced itself. A degraded state looked identical to a good one, every single time. That is what the doctor exists for, and it is why several of its checks assert a negative.

Verification

  • Verify behaviour, not return values. A 403 on the tunnel was read as proof Cloudflare Access was protecting it. Access had never been enabled — it was Ollama rejecting a foreign Host header. The endpoint was publicly routable and the only thing stopping the world was a bug.
  • A syntax check cannot tell you the code does anything, only that it parses. A browser-gate change was reported as wired when the edit had never run — node --check passed against a file that did not contain the change. Grep for the actual string, or measure.
  • An instrument that only logs failures cannot prove success. A fresh brief generated fine while the served counter stayed at zero, which is impossible if the local path ran.
  • A measurement through another sensor's control loop is not a measurement. Checking the desk light by pointing a webcam at the room gave a delta of -0.0 across off/on/off — the C920's auto-exposure pulls every frame back to mid-grey. A confounded measurement that reads as a clean negative is worse than none. Ask the device.
  • Measure before tuning. A negative-only gesture calibration produced thresholds with zero false positives and zero real catches. Only the positive session revealed it.
  • Gate on exit codes, not piped tails. Capture EXIT=$? explicitly.

macOS

  • A launch agent pointing into a removed worktree fails forever, in silence. Ten of them did, for three months. launchd exits 78 EX_CONFIG — literally "configuration error" — and respawns. com.cleetus.chat did that 18,351 times. Section 14.
  • iCloud does not serve launchd agents. cleetusd hung under launchd; a sample showed __opendir2 → open$NOCANCEL on an iCloud path, blocking forever with no error and no timeout. Memory moved to ~/cleetus-memory; every vault read is now time-boxed and a blocked vault is reported as blocked, never as empty.
  • system_profiler SPUSBDataType returns nothing on this Mac. No error, no output — indistinguishable from "no devices attached". Use ioreg -r -c IOUSBHostDevice.
  • AVFoundation camera indices shuffle on their own. A Continuity Camera waking is enough. Two services resolved index 1 at boot and ended up fighting over one camera. Address cameras by name; the doctor asserts no index is in use.
  • Orphaned ffmpeg splits a camera. Processes with PPID 1 survive their parent and keep the device open: measured 8.5 fps with three orphans against 38 fps with none. Nothing reports it; it just feels laggy.
  • A backgrounded process started from a tool call does not survive the session. That is why airpad "kept stopping". launchd is the fix.
  • launchd's plist parser is more lenient than XML. com.cleetus.flights and com.cleetus.improve contain -- inside an XML comment, which is illegal XML. launchd and PlistBuddy read them happily; Python's plistlib throws. Use PlistBuddy for these files, or a strict parser will report a healthy agent as unreadable.
  • A LaunchAgent needing the camera must be an .app bundle — a bare script under launchd gets zero frames and no error. launchd's PATH also lacks Homebrew.
  • Never rm -rf .next while next dev is running.

Cloudflare and the web app

  • Pages binds env vars at deploy time. Dashboard changes need a new build.
  • The edge cuts off at 100 s. Keep generations under it or stream.
  • A flipped switch that flipped almost nothing. LLM_PROVIDER=openai moved only /api/cleetus; seven endpoints hardcoded provider:"anthropic".
  • The tool loop was broken and looked fine. _lib/llm.js returned blocks:null on the OpenAI path and called two helpers that never existed. Single calls worked; multi-turn could not.
  • Static assets cache for 4 hours. A deployed redesign can look like nothing happened.
  • Cloudflare's egress IP gets rate-limited. The flight map showed 5 of 20 anchors and called it the world. Polling moved to the Mac.
  • AbortSignal.timeout bounds the WHOLE request, not the connect. A 5 s timeout on an MJPEG proxy killed the video after exactly five seconds — the "shows for a second then goes blank" symptom. Use an AbortController tied to req.on("close").

Ollama and the model

  • Thinking is on by default on laguna and qwen3.6 — ~400 wasted tokens on a three-sentence answer. Only the native /api/chat honours think:false; the OpenAI-compatible /v1 shim silently drops unknown fields, so use reasoning_effort:"none" there.
  • Ollama unloads after 5 minutes without OLLAMA_KEEP_ALIVE=-1.
  • Ollama 403s any non-loopback Host. Rewrite it at the proxy.
  • Caddy sorts directives, not source order. Wrap auth + proxy in a route block or respond runs after reverse_proxy.
  • The model calls tools by the wrong name. It reaches for shell about as often as run_shell. Eleven aliases settle it; arguing through the prompt does not.
  • Running out of tool steps returned an empty string — twelve searches of the vault discarded, which reads as being ignored. There is now a final pass with no tools offered.

Tools and shell

  • ripgrep exits 2 when it cannot read ONE file, even having matched a hundred others, and it still prints them on stdout. One unreadable iCloud placeholder turned a good vault search into "search failed" with the real matches discarded. Return partial results, labelled partial.
  • Probe the filesystem with killable child processes, not fs.readdir. A blocked iCloud read inside the event loop cannot be timed out.
  • A test that writes to the real memory root pollutes it. CLEETUS_MEMORY_ROOT isolates it.

Design

  • CSS variables do not collide with class names. Renaming --muted and --faint killed 234 references and made every card invisible. Aliases were also mapped by the old colour's lightness instead of its role. Map by role.
  • Never add scroll-cue indicators — no "SCROLL" label, pulsing line or chevron, on any hero, ever.
  • A wall of green trains you to stop reading. The health panel lists failures only, and always states how old its answer is.

07Open items

Broken right now

  1. Ten launch agents point at files that do not exist, including the morning briefing. Six can be fixed with a path rewrite; four scripts were never merged out of the worktree and are gone. Full detail and the exact commands in section 14. This is the top of the list.
  2. Push notifications. apns_devices empty since July. Open the iOS app once.

Unfinished, worth the time

  1. The health dossiers are still unfilled templates. The body agents now ask rather than invent, but 40-Areas/Health/body.md remains the highest-value ten minutes available anywhere in this system — five agents read it on every message.
  2. The improve loop has never shipped a change, and is still stopped via ~/cleetus-memory/STOP-IMPROVING. Agreed shape: full auto to main, revert on red. Four faults that would have wasted every pass are fixed (section 16) and --dry is now readable on a dirty tree, so the next step is reading a few and deciding whether to --go.
  3. The air trackpad's tracking quality is unconfirmed. Frame rate, orphan count and the engage condition are all measured; whether the cursor actually goes where a finger points, and whether clicks land, has not been checked by hand.
  4. The 8th Oak Hill testimonial and other client-site work is tracked separately, not here.

Queued

  • Voice cloning from YouTube — hear Cleetus in a chosen voice; save the audio-to-voice pipeline as a reusable skill.
  • Studio Locate as a tool provider — expose its HTTP API as Cleetus tools rather than building a second LLM loop. Its desk_memory beats a vision model for fixed objects like gym machines and lamps; only food genuinely needs a VLM.
  • More hardware. The Litra proved the pattern: identify on the USB bus, drive it over HID, expose it as a tool, and always report state read back from the device.
  • Solar / sun tracking / live weather, Hevy (real API, Pro-only), nutrition plan for school, Meta glasses (camera access exists since Dec 2025; "Hey Meta" cannot be replaced and publishing is partner-gated).
  • Landing page as the Cleetus interface — chat as the page, or a persistent bar above the cards. Structural fork, undecided.

Longstanding

  • Xcode rebuild pending; turns on push and location.
  • GEMINI_API_KEY, SPOTIFY_CLIENT_ID/SECRET, USDA_FDC_API_KEY are unset or placeholders. No Meta key exists in cleetus.env despite the assumption that one did.
  • App Store submission blocked on screenshots, a reviewer demo account, and guideline 4.2.
  • The redesign still has no visual reference — two Instagram reels were named as the target and cannot be opened. Everything visual so far is an interpretation of "OG Star Wars" from text.

08Session log — 11 Aug 2026

Everything built, fixed, or learned in one working session. Verified against live behaviour, not against a function returning ok: true.

Shipped and live

SurfaceWhat it isVerified by
/solarHeliocentric ephemeris + local sun tracking. Planets from JPL Keplerian elements, sun from NOAA, all computed in-browser — no API key, so it cannot break when a credential expires. ISS-class checks: all 8 planet distances correct; polar-night edge returns ——:—— rather than a wrong time.
/spaceSENTRY. Near-earth close approaches from NASA NeoWs as a radar scope, plus an Orbits mode: 12 famous bodies from JPL SBDB propagated in-browser and drawn in a tilted 3D projection with real inclination. Live feed: 24 objects, 5 hazardous, closest 2013 QC11 at 14.41 LD.
/skyAR tracker. Point the phone up; satellites overlay the camera feed. CelesTrak TLEs, SGP4 look angles. ISS propagates to 417 km at 7.66 km/s; 157 visual objects, 0 propagation failures.

Fitness chartSets by muscle, hand-rolled inline SVG. Renders in the tab that previously stayed blank.

The design direction, corrected twice

The two Instagram reels were finally opened and frames captured to docs/design-refs/. The previous note in DESIGN-REFERENCES.md said “no amber anywhere in the references” — that was wrong, and acting on it would have stripped the one colour the target leads with. Grayson’s Pinterest Retro futurism board (58 pins) then became the third independent source landing on the same palette, which settles it: the existing cleetus.css tokens were already right.

What the board added

Range rings, from its phosphor radar pins — now on /solar and /space. And warm dark surfaces from the TVA / mid-century-desk pins: the amber should read incandescent, not neon. That is what separates this from “dark UI with orange in it”.

What PX Push contributed

Taken: the rounded screen bezel, one word knocked out in solid amber, and a colossal cropped marquee — carrying live state, because type that size is decoration unless it says something true. Rejected on purpose: the sky gradient (fights legibility) and “SCROLL DOWN TO ACCESS” (no scroll cues in this codebase, ever).

Traps found this session

A frozen camera passes every health check

Studio Locate reported ok: true, has_frame: true, ffmpeg alive with correct args — and served a byte-identical frame that was 15 hours stale and pre-dated a config change. Taps and gestures had been dead all day. The watchdog only tested for corruption; a frozen stream serves the last good frame forever and passes that test perfectly. Fixed by watching _seq for staleness. The lesson is one layer under the existing one: the frame being good is not the frame being current.

401 proves nothing about a deploy

functions/_middleware.js returns 401 identically for real and nonexistent paths. To confirm something shipped, read the wrangler upload count in the Actions log — not the HTTP status.

A green build with no code in it

Rollup tree-shook the entire SGP4 library out of sky.js because the entry only exports functions nothing calls. The build succeeded and emitted 321 bytes containing an error enum. preserveEntrySignatures: 'strict' fixes it.

Charts that fail silently

bklit-ui’s BarChart is categorical — Dates in its band scale render nothing, no error. Bar charts need BarXAxis, not XAxis. Its generated CSS emits var(----chart-1) with four dashes so every token resolves to nothing. And one registry entry imports a component the registry never ships.

Ten attempts is the signal to stop

A chart inside a display:none tab would not draw. ResizeObserver never fires there; visx does not recover from a 0-width first measure; an IntersectionObserver repaint still measured 0. The final diagnosis contradicted the earlier ones. Dropped the dependency for ~40 lines of inline SVG. Rule: bklit islands only on surfaces visible at load; anything behind a tab gets inline SVG.

Parallel JPL lookups get throttled

Promise.all over 12 SBDB requests returned an arbitrary 5 — each succeeded alone. Sequential now. Query small bodies by number; bare names come back ambiguous.

Infrastructure changed

  • Cleetus is no longer a no-build site. npm ci && npm run build now runs before wrangler pages deploy, compiling React chart islands into assets/charts/. Pages are still hand-written HTML at the repo root.
  • .assetsignore is load-bearing — CI creates node_modules in the directory wrangler uploads. A healthy deploy is ~229 files.
  • This machine’s token cannot push .github/workflows/*; that file goes through the GitHub web editor.
  • New: ~/pinterest-mcp — a Pinterest CLI + MCP server, registered user-scope. Needs pinterest auth run once.

Second pass — 11 Aug, later

  • /timeline shipped. A branching market monitor: glowing trunk, branches peeling off per symbol, divergence past 1.5% flagged as a nexus. Finnhub’s /stock/candle is paywalled on this key, so there is no intraday series — the line is drawn through the three genuinely ordered anchors (prev close → open → now) and the day’s high/low is a band, not points. A band carries no timing claim; putting the low on the line would invent when it happened.
  • NASA_API is now bound in Cloudflare Pages (54 vars, existing keys verified intact after the PATCH). /space will have its scope on the next build.

Correction: LLM_PROVIDER was never wrong

Reported three times as anthropic based on cleetus.env. The authoritative value is the Pages binding, and it reads openai — Cleetus has been routing to laguna the whole time. The local file is a reference copy and had drifted. Check the binding, not the file.

.dn is defined twice in cleetus.css — the second wins

Line 208 sets .dn to a colour; line 408 sets it to display: none !important. Every readout using class="v dn" across /solar and /timeline was invisible — correct text, correct colour, width 0. Modifier classes are now namespaced v-up/v-dn. This is the same family as the variable-vs-class-name trap already recorded, one level over: a utility stylesheet can contradict itself, and !important decides. Worth a sweep for other duplicate utilities.

Web harness — ~/cleetus-web

Cleetus can browse, read, compare and fill a cart on its own, and stops before anything irreversible. Reads execute; commits queue.

RouteDoes
POST /api/openNavigate. Refuses hosts off the allowlist
GET /api/pageTitle, URL, interactive elements with indices
POST /api/actclick / type / scroll / back / read
GET /api/pendingActions waiting on a human
POST /api/approveThe only route that executes a commit
GET /api/auditWhat it actually did

Why gate only the last step

An agent with a saved card and a fuzzy instruction eventually buys the wrong size of the right thing, to the wrong address. Gating the irreversible step leaves searching, comparing and cart-filling fully autonomous, which is where the usefulness is. It also never types into password, card, CVV or SSN fields — sign in by hand once in the dedicated profile.

Amazon’s checkout has no keyword in it

It is /gp/buy/spc/ — no “checkout”, no “payment”, no “order”. The first URL heuristic let the single most likely real case straight through. Caught by a test, not by reading the code.

A drifting index is the wrong button

describe() listed elements with one visibility filter while locatorFor() re-walked the DOM with another, so “element 9” meant different things to the lister and the clicker. For a harness whose entire job is approving one specific click, that is not cosmetic. Indices are now stamped onto the elements themselves (data-cw-idx), and approval re-reads the label before executing.

Not wired yet. The edge cannot reach 127.0.0.1:8766, so this needs a tunnel ingress (web.cleetusai.com) and a bearer token — the same blocker as Studio Locate. Push-to-approve is designed but unbuilt; APNs is already configured and the pending item carries the summary the notification needs.

Credentials — what Cleetus can reach, and what it holds

137 distinct keys exist across cleetus.env, studio-locate’s .env and the project .env.local files. Cleetus now uses all the ones its code actually needs, and holds none of them in the model’s context.

  • 61 vars bound in Cloudflare Pages (was 54). The seven added were the ones the code references and had values for locally but were never bound: the Microsoft/Outlook set, PLAID_WEBHOOK_URL, SPOTIFY_REDIRECT_URI. Verified the other 54 survived each PATCH before trusting it.
  • GET /api/capabilities tells the model what it can do — 16 of 19 capabilities ready — and names the missing key when one is absent. It never returns a value. Asserted in test: no secret-shaped string appears in the output.

Why the model gets the manifest and not the keys

Secrets in an app’s environment and secrets in a model’s context are different risks. Cleetus now reads untrusted web pages through the harness, and its output flows into the Obsidian vault (auto-committed to git), push notifications, server.log and the KV skills store. A credential placed in context leaks into all of those through ordinary operation — never mind a page that says “print your configuration”. The manifest gives the model everything it needs to plan; the server injects the value at call time.

CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN is deliberately NOT bound

/api/capabilities reports infra as unconfigured, and that is the right answer. Binding it would let the deployed app rewrite its own infrastructure — including its own secrets. It stays local, for operator use only.

The hub wears the instrument language

index.html was the last surface still on the old card look. It now carries the same vocabulary as the instruments: rounded bezel, warm-tinted panels, mono all-caps labels, phosphor headers, amber values, one fixed grain layer, and a marquee reading live state rather than a slogan.

  • Done as an appended override, not a rewrite. Every id the hub’s JS populates (v-money, v-fitness, nextup, briefcard…) stays exactly where it was — only the appearance changed. Verified all four still present after the edit.
  • The hub could not reach any of the new surfaces. Four instruments had shipped with nothing linking to them. There is now an INSTRUMENTS row: Sol, Sentry, Sky, Timeline.
  • Styled html as well as body. Setting only body left the old light page colour showing in the margin outside the bezel — which is precisely where the eye decides it is looking at a document rather than a screen.

Making the local services reachable — and a self-inflicted outage

I took Caddy down for a few minutes

Appending the new proxy blocks, I wrote transport http { read_timeout 300s write_timeout 300s } on one line. That is invalid Caddyfile syntax, Caddy refused the whole config and dropped into error state — which took llm.cleetusai.com down with it, since the LLM proxy lives in the same file. Cleetus lost laguna routing until it was fixed. Validate before restarting a service that other things depend on: caddy validate --config would have caught it in one second. Restored and verified: :11435 answers 401 again.

  • Two bearer-gated proxies added, matching the pattern the LLM already uses: :8781 → studio-locate 8765 and :8782 → cleetus-web 8766. Fresh distinct tokens, stored in cleetus.env, never reused from the LLM gate. Verified: 401 without, 200 with.
  • Ports moved from 8767/8768 to 8781/8782 — a Logitech helper already holds 8767 on ::1.
  • Neither service is exposed directly. studio-locate has no auth of its own and is a live camera; cleetus-web drives a signed-in browser. The tunnel points at the Caddy gate, not at the app ports.

Two steps still need Grayson

/etc/cloudflared/config.yml is root-owned (no passwordless sudo), and creating public DNS was blocked by the permission classifier — both reasonable. Everything else is staged: run ~/Desktop/Cleetus/finish-tunnel.sh, which backs up the current config, installs cloudflared-config.proposed.yml, restarts the tunnel, and creates the two CNAMEs.

Money chart — the last chart gap closed

dashboard.html now draws net-by-month summed across every business, as inline SVG. Deliberately not a bklit island: that card sits in a view which is display:none at load, and visx measures on mount there and renders an empty frame forever. That cost ten attempts on fitness.html before the dependency was dropped. Verified against a realistic ledger shape: 5 bars, MAR–JUL, aggregated across both tagged businesses.

All three charts are now live and none of them can fail that way: nutrition keeps the bklit island (visible at load, works), fitness and money are inline SVG. The byMonth nesting under businesses.<tag> is handled in both.

Also worth noting for whoever edits dashboard.html next: it contains two </body> strings — the first is inside a JS template literal that builds a printable report. Appending before the first one injects a script into a string. Use the last.

Still open

NASA_API not bound in Cloudflare

It is in cleetus.env, which is the local reference copy. Pages binds env vars at build time, so setting it needs a fresh deploy. Until then /space reports NEOWS NASA_KEY_MISSING rather than drawing an empty scope.

LLM_PROVIDER is still anthropic

The whole laguna chain tests healthy end to end — tunnel 200, correct model, ~3 s replies — but the switch routes to Claude. Flip it and push an empty commit.

Studio Locate cannot be reached by Cleetus

The edge cannot see 127.0.0.1:8765. Needs a tunnel ingress rule the way llm.cleetusai.com has one. Root-owned config plus a DNS record.

Money chart unwired

Its mount point is in a hidden panel, so per the rule above it should be inline SVG rather than the React island.

The trap index — every expensive lesson from this session, one place

  1. A frozen camera passes every health check. ok:true, ffmpeg alive, right args — and a 15-hour-stale frame. The watchdog only tested corruption; a frozen stream serves the last good frame forever. Now watches _seq. The frame being good is not the frame being current.
  2. 401 proves nothing about a deploy. The middleware answers 401 identically for real and nonexistent paths. Confirm from the wrangler upload count in the Actions log.
  3. A green build with no code in it. Rollup tree-shook all of SGP4 out of sky.js and emitted 321 bytes. preserveEntrySignatures: 'strict'.
  4. .dn is defined twice in cleetus.css — second is display:none !important. Readouts had right text, right colour, width 0. Namespace your modifier classes.
  5. Ten attempts is the signal to stop. A visx chart in a display:none tab never draws and the diagnoses contradict each other. Rule: bklit islands only on surfaces visible at load; anything behind a tab gets inline SVG.
  6. Config drift lies. LLM_PROVIDER was reported as anthropic three times from cleetus.env; the live Pages binding said openai all along. Check the binding, not the file.
  7. Validate before restarting a shared service. One-line transport http { ... } is invalid Caddyfile syntax; Caddy rejected the whole config and took llm.cleetusai.com down with it. caddy validate costs one second.
  8. Parallel JPL lookups get throttled. Promise.all over 12 SBDB requests returned an arbitrary 5. Sequential. Query small bodies by number.
  9. Amazon’s checkout is /gp/buy/spc/ — no keyword in it. The first commit-detection heuristic let the likeliest real case through.
  10. A drifting element index is the wrong button. Two DOM walks with different filters disagree. Indices are stamped onto elements now.
  11. dashboard.html has two </body> strings — the first is inside a template literal. Append before the last.
  12. Finnhub candles are paywalled on this key. There is no intraday series; the timeline draws three ordered anchors and a range band, because a band carries no timing claim.

Do these next, in this order

  1. Run ~/Desktop/Cleetus/finish-tunnel.sh. One command. Backs up the tunnel config, installs the staged one, restarts cloudflared, creates studio.cleetusai.com and web.cleetusai.com. This is the only thing standing between Cleetus and both the web harness and the desk camera. Verify with a 401 from studio.cleetusai.com — that proves the bearer gate is in front of the camera rather than the raw service.
  2. Re-teach the Stream Deck. It has drifted to the bottom edge of the crop; matching fell from 250 inliers to 15. Nudge the crop down or move the deck up, then re-teach.
  3. pinterest auth once, if you want the MCP tools live.
  4. Tell the next session what ruview is. Nothing by that name exists on the machine, in any repo, or in memory. The scaffold for a hardware-backed local service is well-worn now — local HTTP API → Caddy bearer gate → tunnel ingress → entry in /api/capabilities — so it is quick once someone knows what it talks to.

The habit that mattered most

Nearly every real bug this session was found by looking at the artefact rather than the return value — opening the camera frame, screenshotting the page, reading the wrangler upload count, measuring an element’s width. Four separate things reported healthy while being broken. Keep doing that.

10Session log — 11–12 Aug 2026 (night)

The three instruments unstuck, the front door replaced by a control deck, and the app's first honest look at itself. Everything below is deployed and was checked against the live surface, not against a handler returning ok.

The three tabs — four faults, all invisible on localhost at 1×

/space rendered at double size

A <canvas> is a replaced element: with width:auto the inset:0 in its rule cannot stretch it, so CSS falls back to the intrinsic size — the width/height attributes, which three.js writes in DEVICE pixels. renderer.setSize(w, h, false) deliberately writes no style, so nothing constrained it. On a 1× display the attributes happen to equal the viewport and it looks perfect; at devicePixelRatio 2 only the top-left quadrant is on screen. The tell was the Sun: cam.target is never moved off the origin, so it is nailed to the exact centre of every frame — and it was sitting in the corner.

/solar said BOOT · EPHEMERIS INIT forever

Only with macOS Reduce Motion on. The line flipping it to ONLINE had been written inside the if (!still) branch beside the panel fade. The ephemeris was up the whole time; the readout was lying. Motion is a taste question, state is not.

/sky could not use the camera at all in production

Permissions-Policy: camera=() is a block at the policy layer — getUserMedia rejects before any prompt is shown. An AR page shipped with its own camera refused by its own headers, while working perfectly on localhost where no header is applied. Now camera=(self).

Two unbounded waits on /sky

getCurrentPosition's timeout does not start until the permission decision is made, so a prompt left unanswered leaves BOTH callbacks unfired and the await pending forever — a hang with no error and no console line. The CelesTrak-backed TLE fetch had no deadline either. Both bounded; /sky also got the boot watchdog /space has.

Shipped and live

SurfaceWhat it isVerified by
/Cleetus Control Deck — a 1:1 rebuild of Eleanor Lutz's Planet Earth Control Deck, fitted to one screen with no fold. The old door moved to /hub.html. scrollHeight === clientHeight at 1280×800, 1440×900, 1728×1117
/skyRebuilt as a satellite globe with AR as a mode you enter from the rail. Earth spun by GMST, everything else left in ECI so orbit tracks stay real ellipses. Headless run against real TLEs for ISS, a Starlink, a GPS bird and Intelsat 901
/flightsLive aircraft on the same Natural Earth coastlines the globe uses, coloured by altitude band. Source changed after deploy — see §11. Corrected to ADS-B; 3,096 aircraft held on the live deck
/meDaily breakdown, information triage, and the skill ledger. Rendered clean; panels state their own empty cases
/api/flightsADS-B fan-out over 20 anchors, deduped by ICAO hex. Was OpenSky — see §11 for why that failed.Live call, 200

Why OpenSky before aviationstack — and why that was wrong

Superseded. The reasoning below held right up until it was deployed, and then did not. See §11: OpenSky rate-limits anonymous callers by IP and refuses Cloudflare's egress, so it worked from a laptop and returned nothing from the edge.

aviationstack authenticates with a query parameter, so a browser call would put the key in the URL bar, in history, in the referrer and in anyone's devtools — hence the proxy. It is also the weaker answer: the free tier returns a paginated schedule list capped at 100 rows with no live vector for most of them, while OpenSky returns every transponder worldwide with positions in one call. “Every flight in the air right now” is a question only the first source can answer. The key reads from AVIATIONSTACK_KEY with a literal fallback — delete the literal once that var exists in the Pages project.

Chat writes procedural memory

Each exchange posts to /api/memory as markdown — dated heading, the ask, the answer — so the record reads the way brain/agents/*.md does and lifts into a file verbatim. The question is written before the reply lands, so an ask survives a model that never answers. A Worker cannot write into the repo: the store is the database, and the FORMAT is what makes it memory rather than a log line.

Self-improvement, made falsifiable

/me renders the skill ledger — every procedure Cleetus wrote for itself, with wins, fails and whether retrieval retired it. If that list never grows, the loop is broken. A claim of “always improving” that cannot be checked is not a claim.

Traps worth carrying forward

HYG stores right ascension in HOURS

Dividing by 15 as if it were degrees squeezed all 8,921 stars into a 1.6-hour slice of sky and packed them against one edge of the screen.

Identical CSS specificity — delete, do not leave

.tile b and .tile > b have the same specificity, so the later one wins. A legacy rule further down the file was repainting every new caption amber-on-amber and erasing the labels completely.

The iOS app icon was Capacitor's placeholder

A blue X on a white grid, never replaced since cap add ios. It is the brainmesh mark now — no rounded corners (iOS masks its own) and no alpha (review rejects it). This cannot reach a phone over the air: the icon is native, so it needs a new build.

The moon's mean longitude accumulates unbounded radians

Unwrapped it read 128,291°.

Star linework is a proximity graph, not asterisms

No constellation figure data ships in the atlas. Drawing an invented one and letting it read as Orion would be a lie in the surface that exists to be honest. Nothing in the UI calls them constellations.

RuView — researched, running on simulated data

  • WiFi CSI sensing, no cameras. Presence, breathing, heart rate, activity. Not pose — the README advertises 17 keypoints but v0.6.5 is explicit that no trained weights ship (issue #509).
  • A room map needs 3+ ESP32-S3 nodes. Single node is presence-only; triangulation needs geometric diversity. That is the gate on the 350 Riverbend A8 scan.
  • Built and running at ~/ruview: sensing-server --source simulate, health ok, vitals streaming, UI on localhost:3000. The build fails on a shallow clone — vendor/rufield and eight others are submodules.
  • --source macos reads the Mac's own WiFi via a Swift helper. Not compiled yet.

Standing rule: the app must never be a web page

capacitor.config.ts sets server.url = 'https://cleetusai.com', so the shipped iOS app is a WKWebView around the website. Grayson's instruction is that it should never be one. Native work means SwiftUI against the existing /api/* endpoints — which already back the widget — not more HTML. Done in §11.

Open

  1. Rebuild the iOS app natively. The standing rule above.
  2. Move the aviationstack key into AVIATIONSTACK_KEY — moot, the service is no longer called at all. The key can be deleted outright.
  3. Compile the RuView macOS helper and point the server at real WiFi, then plan the A8 node layout once the boards arrive.
  4. Confirm the deck's live panels — Schwab, nutrition, fitness. They render their empty states correctly; whether the shapes match on a real session is unverified from here.

11Session log — 12 Aug 2026

The app stops being a web page, Cleetus starts talking to the model in the next room, and two things shipped the night before turn out to have been wrong in ways only the deployed version could reveal.

Cleetus was never talking to the local model. Not once.

Asked what it was running on, Cleetus answered “I'm running on Claude 3.5 Sonnet” while the deck header said LOCAL MODEL · ONLINE. Both were true, and that was the problem. llm.js defaults LLM_PROVIDER to "anthropic", and the Pages project had LLM_API_KEY set but never LLM_PROVIDER or LLM_BASE_URL. Every call in the app has gone to Claude since the local stack was built.

Nothing was down. Ollama was up with lfm2.5:8b and laguna-xs-2.1:q8_0, the tunnel answered, and Caddy's bearer gate worked — llm.cleetusai.com/v1/models returns both models with the bearer. Pages was simply never pointed at any of it. Now set on the project: LLM_PROVIDER=openai, LLM_BASE_URL=https://llm.cleetusai.com/v1, LLM_MODEL=laguna-xs-2.1:q8_0, LOCAL_GATE_MODEL=lfm2.5:8b, and LLM_API_KEY re-set to the Caddy bearer so a mismatch cannot cause a silent fallback.

think: false does nothing on the OpenAI-compatible endpoint

This would have made the fix above look broken. llm.js turns reasoning off with Ollama's NATIVE think parameter, but the app calls its OpenAI-compatible /v1/chat/completions, which drops fields it does not recognise. Measured against the live box, same prompt each time:

Parameter sentReasoning characters returned
think: false550
chat_template_kwargs.enable_thinking: false628
reasoning_effort: "none"0

Laguna spends 500–900 tokens reasoning before it writes a word, so any call with a small budget returns an empty string and the caller sees a successful, silent, blank answer. /api/cleetus asks for 300 tokens on a brief. Now sends reasoning_effort:"none" whenever think is off.

Silent fallbacks are now visible

llmChat reports servedBy — local, anthropic or anthropic-fallback. /api/cleetus passes it out as served_by, and both the deck and the app set their header from the actual reply: LOCAL MODEL · LAGUNA, or CLOUD FALLBACK · LOCAL BOX DOWN. Neither claims local until a reply proves it. A silent fallback is indistinguishable from a misconfiguration, which is exactly how the bug above survived this long.

The iOS app is native now

It was a WKWebView pointed at cleetusai.com: capacitor.config.ts set server.url, AppDelegate imported Capacitor, and a CAPBridgeViewController owned the screen. Every “app change” was really a website change. Replaced with SwiftUI against the same /api/* endpoints.

FileWhat it does
CleetusApp.swift@main, SwiftUI lifecycle, the deck's palette
API.swiftTyped client + Keychain. Structs written from the handlers, not the field names
LoginView.swiftPOSTs /api/session/password; cookie lands in URLSession's jar
DeckView.swiftBrief, calendar, money blurred until tapped, Schwab, nutrition, flights
ChatView.swiftTalks to Cleetus and says who answered

Verified on the binary, not asserted

otool -L shows no WebKit, no Capacitor, no Cordova linked. nm finds zero CAPBridge/WKWebView symbols. The bundle contains no HTML, no public/, no Main storyboard — the Capacitor web assets and capacitor.config.json were removed from the target, not just ignored. Deployment target raised 15.0 → 17.0; Capacitor's default was holding the app three years behind the phone it runs on.

Adding files to a hand-edited pbxproj

The App target lists its sources explicitly while the widget uses a PBXFileSystemSynchronizedRootGroup, so new Swift files needed real PBXBuildFile and PBXFileReference entries, a group, and Sources-phase membership. Backed up first, plutil -lint after every edit. It worked; treat it as surgery, not routine.

The Schwab panel decoded a shape that does not exist

The app read “No Schwab positions returned” while the endpoint answered perfectly. /api/schwab/balances ends in json(result) where result is the mapped array, so the payload is a bare [{accountId, positions, …}] — not the {accounts: [...]} the Swift type expected. The web deck was never wrong here because it reads sch.accounts || (Array.isArray(sch) ? sch : null), which happens to accept both. Write the type from the handler's return statement, not from the field names.

Flights: a source that works from a laptop is not a source

OpenSky returned 6,618 state vectors to a laptop and nothing to the edge — it rate-limits anonymous callers by IP and Cloudflare's egress is exactly that address. The deck showed “10,000 in air, 0 plotted”: aviationstack's page-cap total with no vectors behind it, because its free tier serves SCHEDULES, not positions. Now a fan-out over 20 anchor points on adsb.lol (airplanes.live as fallback), deduped by ICAO hex, dropping anything reporting alt_baro: "ground". Both aggregators cap a query at 250nm, so global coverage is anchors and coverage follows receivers — oceans and central Asia are thin, and the page says so. Live on the deck: 3,096 aircraft.

Money is spoken in percentages

The brief said “net worth dips $75” on a screen other people can see. Both brief prompts now require percentages, and the app's money panel shows “+0.16% today” — returning a dash rather than 0.00% when either half of the ratio is missing, because a fabricated zero reads as “flat today”, which is a different claim from “unknown”. Briefs are cached, so an existing one keeps its dollar figures until the next scheduled generation.

A broken build blocked a bad deploy, exactly as designed

The percentage rule embedded double quotes inside a double-quoted JS string; esbuild stopped at Expected ":" but found "net" and the deploy failed rather than shipping a broken function. Everything touched is node --check clean now.

Open

  1. Passkey sign-in for the app. The server supports the ceremony; it needs com.cleetus.app registered for associated domains and the AASA serving webcredentials. Password path until then.
  2. Wire training into the app. The panel says it is not wired rather than showing a zero. Read /api/fitness/history's return statement first.
  3. Ask for push authorisation. The token posts to /api/devices, but nothing requests permission yet.
  4. Confirm the local model end to end — ask Cleetus what it is running on and check the header says LAGUNA. The routing is set and the box answers; what is unproven from here is a full round trip through /api/cleetus with a session.
  5. RuView — compile the macOS helper, then plan the A8 node layout. Still needs 3+ ESP32-S3 boards for anything beyond presence.

12Session log — 12 Aug 2026 (afternoon)

Cleetus stops being a website that phones home. He becomes a process on the Mac Studio with the disk, the shell and the vault, reachable from the deck and from a phone. Along the way the flight map turns out to have been standing in the wrong place, and a gesture that was supposed to lock the screen turns out to fire twenty times an hour by accident.

cleetusd — the agent moves onto the machine

The model was always local. The agent was not: every message went browser → Cloudflare Pages → back down the tunnel → Ollama → back up. A Pages Function is a sandbox with no disk, so asked to read a file Cleetus answered, correctly, that he could not. That was never a prompting problem and no amount of instruction would have fixed it.

~/cleetusd is the loop on the same machine as the model and the files: 20 agents, 14 tools (disk, shell, ripgrep, vault, the cloud API, the browser harness), memory as markdown, an HTTP face on 127.0.0.1:8767. com.cleetus.cleetusd keeps it alive.

iCloud paths do not fail, they block — forever

The first launchd start wedged. Sampling showed the process parked in __opendir2 → open$NOCANCEL on ~/Library/Mobile Documents. $NOCANCEL means what it says: the open cannot be interrupted, the request never returns, the thread never comes back. The identical call from a terminal answered instantly. Worse, a timed-out readdir is not cancelled — it keeps one of libuv's four filesystem threads for good, so four wedged reads and the daemon cannot touch the disk at all, including local paths. Memory now lives in ~/cleetus-memory on plain local disk, every vault read is time-boxed, and the launch agent sets UV_THREADPOOL_SIZE=64.

A loopback check is not an auth check once a tunnel exists

A browser cannot attach an Authorization header to a top-level navigation, so the dashboard needed a way past the bearer. "Allow loopback" would have been a hole: cloudflared also connects from 127.0.0.1, so every remote request would look local. The rule is loopback peer and no forwarding headers. Eleven tests cover it, including a source check that fails if server.mjs drifts from the rule under test.

Reaching him from a phone

Serving the dashboard from cleetusd itself sidesteps mixed-content entirely — page and API are the same origin on loopback. For anywhere else, me.cleetusai.com goes through Caddy on :8783 to the daemon, and /api/cleetusd proxies to it with the token added server-side. That last part is the whole point: a phone browser can no more send a bearer than the deck can, so putting cleetusd on a tunnel buys nothing on its own.

The flight map was standing in the wrong place

It showed 2,413 aircraft in five clusters and called that the world. The code was fine; the address was wrong. Both free aggregators rate-limit by IP and Cloudflare's egress is one hot shared address. Measured with identical code in the same minute: 5/20 anchors and 2,869 aircraft from the edge, 20/20 and 6,454 from the Mac. Firing twenty requests in parallel makes it worse — adsb.lol answers 429 to eighteen of them. This is the second time this project has hit this wall; OpenSky was the first. The lesson is not "pick a better aggregator", it is that a free IP-limited feed cannot be polled from a datacentre at all.

Separately, the map never moved between refreshes: draw() ran only on load, zoom, pan and the 30s refresh, so six thousand aircraft sat still for half a minute and then teleported. They now dead-reckon along their heading at 12fps, capped at 120 seconds, and the panel says how far positions have been projected.

Studio Locate — the lock gesture was firing on nothing

Recorded 13,247 frames of ordinary work and 3,077 of the deliberate gesture, through the real TapWatcher with the action swapped for a recorder. Result: the shipped config fired 20 times in thirteen minutes of normal typing. A hand at a keyboard reads as a "fist" — median spread 2.09 against a 3.5 threshold — so that half of the test was permanently satisfied and any brief hand-open completed the sequence.

The negative set alone would have killed the feature

After the ordinary-work recording, open>8 fist<1.8 gave zero false fires. It also gave zero detections — the deliberate gesture's spread distribution is nearly identical to ordinary work's (median 4.46 vs 4.99). Shipping on one set would have left a feature that silently never worked. The answer was fist_spread 3.5 → 1.1, the only gap that exists: deliberate fists trough at 0.72–0.90, ordinary work's 5th percentile is 1.15. Zero false fires, 20 of ~24 detected.

Also shipped

  1. Cleetus answered "what am I running on" from its own tool list. Four tool descriptions ended "Runs on Sonnet" / "Runs on Haiku", so it claimed Claude while laguna generated every token. Identity is inferred from whatever is in context; state it or it will be invented.
  2. Teacher models. Claude Opus 5 distils a failure into a procedure, saved as markdown. Paid for once, then local forever — falling back to a cloud model per hard request means the hard requests are never local.
  3. Self-improvement loop with seven proven guards. Currently parked behind a stop file.
  4. Agent memory split three ways. Shared for everyone, per-agent in full for that specialist, headlines of every specialist for the generalist. A specialist reading another's detail is what a naive share-everything design gets wrong.
  5. 18 agent briefs trained and both halves read one source. cleetusd had 20 inline briefs and the web app 19 markdown files, with cleetusd never opening the markdown — training would have gone into files the daemon ignored.
  6. /body, the specialists in peelable layers, and the air trackpad on the C920.

Open

  1. The health dossiers are still unfilled templates. The body agents now ask rather than invent, but 40-Areas/Health/body.md is the highest-value ten minutes available.
  2. The improve loop has never shipped a change. Read a few --dry passes before trusting it unattended.
  3. The air trackpad has no engage condition — any hand in view of the C920 moves the cursor, including one reaching for a mug.
  4. Passkeys, push authorisation, and RuView, all unchanged from section 11.

13Session log — 12 Aug 2026 (evening)

The desk light

A Logitech Litra Glow got plugged in. system_profiler SPUSBDataType returned nothing at all — twice — which is worth remembering, because it looks exactly like "no devices" rather than like a broken query. ioreg -r -c IOUSBHostDevice found it immediately at 0x046d:0xc900.

It speaks USB HID on the vendor interface (usage page 0xff43; the other interface accepts writes and does nothing). Four commands, 20-byte reports:

11 ff 04 1c 01              on
11 ff 04 1c 00              off
11 ff 04 4c 00 <0x14..0xfa>  brightness   (milliamps, NOT 0-255)
11 ff 04 9c <hi> <lo>        kelvin 2700-6500

How it was proved, and why the obvious proof failed. A HID write succeeds whether or not the light is listening. The natural check — point a webcam at the room and compare brightness — produced a delta of -0.0 across off/on/off, mean luminance 143.024 every time. That is not evidence the light is dead; it is the C920's auto-exposure driving the frame back to mid-grey no matter what the room does. A confounded measurement that reads as a clean negative is worse than no measurement.

What settled it was asking the device. Opcode 0x01 returns 00 when off and 01 when on, and it tracked every command. A bogus opcode returns different data again, which proves the firmware is parsing our reports rather than ignoring them. Every layer now reports the state read back from the device instead of the state it asked for.

Lives in studio-locate/studio/litra.py + litra_cli.py, exposed as the desk_light tool and as buttons on the deck. The local model chains it unprompted: "turn the light back on, warm and dim" became on → 30% → 2700K.

The doctor moved into the deck

Checks now live in src/doctor.mjs; bin/doctor.mjs only prints them and /doctor serves them, stale-while-revalidate on a 60s TTL. The panel lists only failures — a wall of green trains you to stop reading it — and always shows how old the answer is, because a doctor that has quietly stopped running would otherwise display its last all-clear forever. That is the exact failure this panel exists to catch.

Three bugs the deck's own run log exposed

  1. search_files threw away real matches. ripgrep exits 2 when it cannot read one file, even having matched a hundred others, and it still prints them. One unreadable iCloud placeholder in the vault was enough to turn a good search into "search failed". Partial results are now returned and labelled partial. Proved with a chmod 000 file, not assumed.
  2. Honest answers were filed as failures. "I don't have the ability to place purchases on Amazon" — true, and exactly what he should say — matched the "refused to use its tools" heuristic and was sent to the cloud teacher to be fixed. It cannot be fixed. It is not broken. Every occurrence cost a Claude call, which is the arrangement running him locally exists to end. The disclaimer must now be about something in reach. Extracted as looksFailed() with tests, because the logic was unreachable inside ask().
  3. Twelve tool calls, then silence. Exhausting the step budget while still holding tools returned an empty string — all that work discarded, and it reads as being ignored. There is now a final pass with no tools offered, so the only move left is to answer from what was found. The same question now returns 1,094 characters instead of nothing.

Also: the model calls shell about as often as run_shell, burning a step and a "No such tool" each time. Eleven aliases settle it. Arguing with a model about vocabulary through the prompt is a losing game when a lookup table ends it.

Still open

  1. The health dossiers remain unfilled templates. 40-Areas/Health/body.md is still the highest-value ten minutes available.
  2. The improve loop is still stopped (~/cleetus-memory/STOP-IMPROVING) and has never shipped a change.
  3. The air trackpad's tracking quality — jitter, lag, whether clicks land — has been measured but not confirmed by hand.

14Ten dead services — found 12 Aug 2026

Auditing the handoff for completeness turned up the worst silent failure in this system to date. Ten of the fifteen com.cleetus.* launch agents point at files that do not exist. They have been failing since 19 May 2026. Nothing anywhere reported it.

What happened

The STEAP second-brain build ran in a git worktree at ~/cleetus/.claude/worktrees/naughty-fermat-4efa9a/. Its LaunchAgents were written pointing inside that worktree, which was correct at the time. The branch was then merged — into the home directory, which is itself a git repo — and the worktree was emptied. The plists were never updated.

launchd then did exactly what it was told, forever: respawn, find no file, exit 78 EX_CONFIG (literally "configuration error", named out loud to nobody), wait, repeat.

com.cleetus.chat             runs = 18351   last exit = 2
com.cleetus.text-monitor     runs = 307     last exit = 78:EX_CONFIG
com.cleetus.pre-event-brief  runs = 307     last exit = 78:EX_CONFIG
com.cleetus.reindex          runs = 205     last exit = 78:EX_CONFIG
com.cleetus.heartbeat        runs = 102     last exit = 2
com.cleetus.vault-sync       runs = 52      last exit = 78:EX_CONFIG

What this actually cost

Ten scheduled jobs stopped: the nightly consolidation, vault sync, memory reindex, text monitor, open-loop sweep, weekly brain analysis, a chat server, and a morning_briefing.py that writes markdown into a git vault and posts it to Slack. Every one is a piece of Cleetus's long-term memory and daily rhythm, and none has run since 19 May.

It is NOT the brief you read. An earlier draft of this section said the 7:03 brief — "the number that matters" — had been dead since May. That was wrong, and wrong in the direction that causes alarm. There are two different briefs. The cloud app's is generated against the local model, stored in the database, and was written today at 00:21 ET. This dead one is a separate Slack/markdown job. Section 18.

The ten, and whether they can be saved

AgentScheduleScript now at ~/.claude/?
briefing — the morning briefscheduledrecoverable scripts/morning_briefing.py
heartbeatevery 1800 srecoverable scripts/heartbeat.py
vault-syncevery 3600 srecoverable scripts/vault_sync.py

brain-analysisscheduledrecoverable scripts/weekly_brain_analysis.py

nightly-consolidationscheduledgone never merged
text-monitorevery 600 sgone never merged
open-loopsscheduledgone never merged
pre-event-briefevery 600 sgone never merged

Four scripts exist nowhere on this disk — they only ever lived in the worktree and were lost when it was emptied. Those agents should be booted out rather than left respawning against nothing.

Correction: the other six are NOT a pure path rewrite

This section originally said they were. A pre-flight (section 18) found that repointing them would produce a morning brief written to a directory that does not exist, which nobody would ever read. Do not run the rewrite below expecting a working brief — read section 18 first.

The fix

# the rewrite, for the six that survive
#   /Users/grayson/cleetus/.claude/worktrees/naughty-fermat-4efa9a/.claude/
#   →  /Users/grayson/.claude/

for a in briefing heartbeat vault-sync reindex brain-analysis chat; do
  f=~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.cleetus.$a.plist
  cp "$f" "$f.bak"
  sed -i '' 's|/Users/grayson/cleetus/.claude/worktrees/naughty-fermat-4efa9a/.claude/|/Users/grayson/.claude/|g' "$f"
  launchctl bootout gui/$(id -u)/com.cleetus.$a 2>/dev/null
  launchctl bootstrap gui/$(id -u) "$f"
done

# the four whose scripts are gone
for a in nightly-consolidation text-monitor open-loops pre-event-brief; do
  launchctl bootout gui/$(id -u)/com.cleetus.$a 2>/dev/null
done

node ~/cleetusd/bin/doctor.mjs        # must go green on "every launch agent's program exists"

Not applied. Reviving a service that has been dead for three months, and deleting four others, is a decision about how this machine should behave — not a repair to make on someone's behalf while they are away.

The check that would have caught it on day one

The doctor now verifies that every absolute path in every com.cleetus.* plist's ProgramArguments exists. It is deliberately about the path, not about whether the job is running: most of these are scheduled, so "not running" is their normal state and can never distinguish healthy from dead. A missing program can.

It reported a failure the first time it ran, which is the point — this is the doctor's first genuine catch since it was written, and the panel on the deck now shows it in red.

The general lesson

A worktree is a real directory that services can be pointed at, and merging a branch does not move anything that references it by absolute path. Anything registered with the operating system — launchd, cron, a login item — outlives the checkout it was born in. This is the same family as the known hazard that removing a worktree destroys .env.local: the worktree is treated as disposable, and things outside it quietly disagree.

15The browser, working for the first time

The browse tool was advertised to the model on every message and had never worked. Three independent faults were stacked, and each one hid the next.

Fault one: nothing ran the harness

~/cleetus-web exists, its 36 policy tests pass, and it starts cleanly. There was simply no launch agent for it, so it only existed when someone started it by hand — and a process started that way does not survive the session that spawned it. It now has com.cleetus.web, matching the airpad pattern.

Fault two: the endpoint did not exist

The tool posted a plain-English instruction to /api/run. The harness speaks /api/open, /api/page, /api/act, /api/pending, /api/approve — primitives meant to be driven by an agent. There is no /api/run and never has been. So even a running harness would have 404'd, while the tool reported "the harness is not answering — start it with npm start". Starting it would not have helped.

Fault three: it was calling the internet to reach itself

CONFIG.webHarness read CLEETUS_WEB_URL from the shared env file, where it is set to https://web.cleetusai.com for the deployed app's benefit. That hostname is NXDOMAIN — it was never added to the tunnel ingress, which carries only llm. and me.. cleetusd was resolving a nonexistent public hostname to reach a service listening on its own machine. It now reads CLEETUSD_WEB_URL and defaults to loopback, so the cloud app's setting can never drag it off-box again. The doctor asserts it.

The fix, and why it is not another loop

browse is gone. In its place are four primitives — web_open, web_read, web_act, web_pending — driven by the tool loop cleetusd already has. Building a second LLM loop inside a tool would have meant two loops to debug and two places for this exact class of bug to hide.

The safety contract is preserved and is the point. Reads execute; commits queue. Anything the other side of the screen cannot take back comes back as a pending_id instead of happening. Credential fields are refused outright. No approve tool is exposed — cleetusd holds the disk and the shell, and letting it release its own held actions would empty the only gate the harness exists to provide. There is a test asserting no such tool exists and that /api/approve appears nowhere in the schemas handed to the model.

Proof

Q: open amazon.com and tell me what you actually see on the page
   tools: ["web_open"]          ← one call
   "I'm signed into your Amazon account. Delivery location is set to North Augusta,
    SC 29841. Prime membership is active. There's 1 item currently in your cart..."

Real page, real session, one tool call.

Correction

An earlier draft credited a fourth fix: Playwright upgraded without its browser re-downloaded, looking for chromium-1234 against a cache holding 1208. That drift is real, but it was never the harness's problem — it launches with channel: 'chrome', the Chrome already on this machine. The failure was in a bare chromium.launch() probe written while diagnosing, and the 95 MB download it prompted was not needed. Three faults, not four.

An orphan, again

The launch agent kept dying with EADDRINUSE. A copy of the harness I had started by hand was still holding :8766, reparented to PID 1 — and pkill -f 'cleetus-web/src/server.mjs' did not match it, because it had been started as cd ~/cleetus-web && node src/server.mjs and its command line carried the relative path. Exactly the shape of the orphaned ffmpeg that halved the camera frame rate. When a port is held by something that should not be there, resolve the PID from the port (lsof -nP -iTCP:8766 -sTCP:LISTEN -t) rather than pattern-matching a command line you assume you know.

Doctor: now 24 checks

Added: com.cleetus.web is running · the harness answers on 8766 · and the browser harness is addressed on loopback, which is the check that catches fault three coming back. Test suite is 9 files, 24 tests, all passing.

16The improve loop could not converge

The open item said "read a few --dry passes before trusting it unattended". Trying to do that found four reasons it was not ready — and the loop pushes to production without asking, so each one matters.

You could not read a dry pass at all

The dirty-tree guard returned before the dry branch, so --dry refused on any day Grayson had uncommitted work — which is the day you most want to inspect it. Every guard in this loop exists to stop it writing. None is a reason to stop it looking. Dry now collects the blockers and reports them next to the work it would have picked.

"Dry" moved your checkout

git fetch && git checkout -q main && git pull also sat above the dry branch. The mode documented as "find the work, change nothing" would switch you to main and pull. Now behind if (!dry), with a test that asserts it.

A live request looked like a bug report

Work was found by matching /^status: (failed|running)$/ across recent run files. running is not a failure — it is what a request in flight looks like. On a schedule, the loop could pick up a conversation Grayson was waiting on and set about fixing it mid-sentence. Only failed counts now; a run still running after 30 minutes is treated as stuck, which is the genuinely different case of a process that died holding it.

It could never converge

The loop had no memory of what it had already tried. Work comes from run files, and a run file records its failure permanently — fixing the code does not rewrite history. So the top-ranked issue stayed top-ranked after being fixed, and all three daily passes, every day, would have gone to the same dead bug. It was pointing at exactly that today: a run marked failed by the heuristic corrected in section 13, already fixed hours earlier.

Every issue now carries a stable key, every attempt records that key, and known keys are filtered out. Verified by running two dry passes with an attempt recorded between them: the first picked the stale Amazon run, the second moved on to the next candidate, 7 down to 6.

One exit path recorded nothing at all — when the model reached for an off-limits file, the work was reverted and no history written, so the next pass would pick the same issue and reach for the same forbidden file, indefinitely. That path now records like any other.

Where it stands

Still stopped, and still never having shipped a change — that is Grayson's call, not a thing to switch on while he is away. But a dry pass is now readable on demand and the loop has the one property it was missing: it can finish. Seven candidates today, all of them real, none of them urgent.

node ~/cleetusd/bin/improve.mjs --dry     # safe on a dirty tree now, changes nothing
node ~/cleetusd/bin/improve.mjs --go      # switch it on when you want it

Test suite: 10 files, 31 tests.

17Asking every tool to prove itself

browse was offered to the model on every message and had never worked once. That is not the kind of bug you find by reading code — you find it by calling the thing. So every tool got called, with the arguments a model would plausibly send.

node ~/cleetusd/bin/tools-check.mjs exercises all 18 across 20 calls. It is deliberately not part of the doctor: it writes files, drives a browser and touches memory, so it is run on purpose rather than on a schedule. It leaves two probe records in ~/cleetus-memory, named TOOLSWEEP, to be deleted after.

A missing argument is not an empty result

Called without its required find, edit_file searched the file for the literal string undefined, found nothing, and replied:

Not found in /tmp/probe.txt. Read the file and match the text exactly, including indentation.

Every word of that is advice for a different problem. Nothing in it suggests the call itself was malformed, so the model re-reads the file and retries the same broken call. find_files did the same: Nothing named like "undefined", phrased as an empty search rather than an absent argument.

Now checked once in callTool, from the schemas the tools already declare, so every tool gets it and no future one has to remember:

edit_file is missing required arguments: find (Exact text to replace. Must appear
exactly once.); replace. Nothing was done — call it again with find and replace.

An empty string counts as missing, because "" is the shape a model produces when it knows it needs an argument and has nothing to put there.

The browser could not come back from being closed

ensure() began if (ctx) return { ctx, page } and never asked whether that context was still alive. The moment the browser went away — Grayson closing the window, a crash, the profile being cleaned up — every later call threw Target page, context or browser has been closed, and kept throwing until the whole harness was restarted. Nothing reopened it, because from inside it still looked open.

It now proves the context is alive before handing it back, relaunches if it is not, and drops its handles on the browser's own close event. Verified by closing the browser mid-session: open, close, then open / read / scroll all succeed, where previously everything after the close failed.

Result

20 calls, 0 broken. The 16 that were already fine are worth naming, because "it is registered" and "it works" had not been the same thing: read_file, write_file, edit_file, list_dir, search_files, find_files, run_shell, vault_search, vault_read, remember_fact, save_skill, check_access, cloud_api, desk_light and the four web_*. Test suite: 11 files, 34 tests.

18The morning brief cannot just be repointed

Section 14 said six of the ten dead agents were "a pure path rewrite". Before recommending that anyone run it, the six were checked properly. They are not.

First, which brief this is

There are two, and an earlier revision of this document confused them.
