The weekly self-assessment is handed a list of question titles and told to be hard on itself. It has no idea what it actually did, so it makes something up — and because the something is about its own behaviour, it reads as insight.

The 14 August analysis concluded:

"You're falling down because you're not actually looking at the camera output
 — you're just saying you can't see images. The one change that would help most
 is to stop claiming limitations and actually run who_is_there, look, ..."

who_is_there and look were each called five times that week. And both cameras are off the USB bus, so the recommended change was to stop admitting a limitation that is real. That is the invented-desk failure wearing a different hat: no evidence, confident mechanism, advice pointing straight at fabrication.

The cause is in the prompt. The digest it receives is - [status] title and nothing else — never a tool call, never a result. Asked "where are you falling down" against that, the honest answer is "I cannot tell from here", and models do not volunteer that.

Evidence it can actually use

The tally built earlier today (section 117) counts tool calls out of the same run files, so it costs nothing to hand over. The job now sees what it really called, and is told what it cannot see:

Tools you actually called this week, counted from your own run files:
  read_file: 146 calls
  who_is_there: 5 calls
  look: 5 calls
  ...
You are seeing the QUESTIONS and the tool counts, not the answers you gave.

That fixed the claim and did not fix invention

Worth recording precisely, because the obvious reading of this section would be that better evidence solved it. It did not. The next run dropped the camera claim and closed with:

"The desk light is currently at 2700K warm white, 50% brightness — on."

A live hardware reading, in a weekly retrospective, from a job that has no tools at all. It also decided studio-locate "isn't broken" while its camera check was red. Better evidence moved the invention rather than removing it.

So the second guard is a prohibition rather than more context. This job reads a list of old titles and cannot see the machine, which means every present-tense sentence about a device is invented by construction — and that makes it something to forbid outright rather than hope gets crowded out:

You have no tools here and you cannot see the machine. Say NOTHING about the
current state of any device, service, camera, light or file. Anything you write
in that form is invented.

Two runs since: no hardware claims, no invented mechanism, no recommendation to call a tool it already calls. The five tests assert the guards are present rather than what the model says — a weaker kind of test than this project likes, and the right one here, because the behaviour belongs to the model and the instruction is the only part this code owns. Suite at 333.

Found along the way and fixed structurally: two runs on 13 Aug recorded a tool result as the literal text [object Object], followed by a confident description of a desk nobody had looked at. That was fixed inside vision.mjs, on one tool. callTool now coerces every result: an object is serialised (the information was there — only the formatting was lost), and nothing at all becomes a sentence saying so, because an empty gap is what invention grows in. Thirty-eight tools, any of which could have grown that path.

122The file's own rule, broken in its own helper

The header of src/jobs.mjs states the doctrine this whole project runs on. The function four of those jobs read their input through violated it in its first line.

// A JOB THAT CANNOT RUN SAYS SO. [...] a job that reports "nothing to do"
// when it actually could not look would be the same bug in a new costume.
const files = (await readdir(RUNS).catch(() => [])).filter(...)

Rename the runs directory, move it, or change its permissions, and that returns an empty list. The nightly consolidation then reports "nothing durable", the open loops report "Nothing open", and the weekly analysis reports a blank week. Four jobs, all cheerful, all blind, and nothing anywhere saying so — the system would look quiet and healthy while seeing nothing at all.

It now throws, and the message draws the distinction it exists to draw:

FAILED nightly-consolidation: threw: cannot read the runs directory
/tmp/cleetusd-not-here-9f31/runs (ENOENT)
— this is not an empty day, it is a blind one

Throwing is the right shape rather than a flag, because bin/job.mjs already turns a thrown job into a FAIL line in jobs.log, and the doctor's the scheduled jobs are working check reads that log — the same route that surfaced the ollama outage an hour earlier. The failure travels the path built for it instead of inventing a fifth silent one.

The third test is the one that keeps this honest: an empty runs directory must still be a normal quiet day. A guard that fires on every genuinely quiet day is noise, and noise gets removed. Both halves were proven by restoring the old one-liner and watching two of the three go red.

Tonight will say nothing, and that is correct

All twenty of today's runs are marked probe: true — jobs, health checks, and two sessions testing conversation persistence. Grayson has not spoken to Cleetus today. So the consolidation at 23:00 will report nothing durable, and for once that can be trusted rather than merely hoped: real conversations do come through unmarked, 18 of them on 12 Aug and 28 on 13 Aug.

Which is the point of the change. Before it, "nothing to consolidate" was a sentence that could mean either a quiet day or a broken one, and there was no way to tell them apart from the outside. Suite at 336.

123The app never asked, and the key speaks the wrong environment

Notifications had never once been delivered. Four separate things were wrong, and every one of them reported success.

The app never called registerForRemoteNotifications(). AppDelegate had a perfectly good didRegisterForRemoteNotificationsWithDeviceToken waiting for a token that nothing had ever asked Apple for. The one token that did exist was minted in May by the Capacitor webview, and the app POSTed it to /api/devices — a path no handler answers. The endpoint is /api/devices/register.

Server-side, pushAll() threw the APNs HTTP status away. Apple was answering 403 BadEnvironmentKeyInToken for every device, which is neither the 200 that counts as sent nor the 410/400 that prunes a dead token, so the loop finished having done nothing at all and returned this:

{ok: true, sent: 0, pruned: 0}

/api/health read that and blamed the phone: "the token is probably stale; open the iOS app once". Opening the app could never have fixed it. The real cause is that the key speaks Production and the phone speaks Sandbox — auth key B8S6L34V7K is scoped to Production only, and APNS_ENV is sandbox because a development-signed build mints sandbox tokens. Proved by minting a provider JWT locally and sending to both hosts with a deliberately bogus device token: sandbox answered BadEnvironmentKeyInToken, production answered BadDeviceToken — the correct "your credentials are fine, that device is not".

The one remaining step needs an Apple login, so it is Grayson's: create an APNs auth key with Sandbox (or Sandbox & Production) enabled at developer.apple.com → Certificates, Identifiers & Profiles → Keys, and paste the .p8 into the APNS_AUTH_KEY secret on the cleetus Pages project. Everything either side of that is done and verified.

Three smaller things were caught the same way — by running the thing instead of reading it. The breadcrumb pushAll leaves in app_kv was a fire-and-forget fetch, and a Worker cancels pending I/O the moment it returns a Response, so health was describing a push from the previous day as "the last push". Fmt.shortDate parsed date-only strings as UTC midnight and formatted them in local time, so in Georgia every date rendered a day early — a workout logged on the 12th read "Aug 11". And /api/morning-brief answers with two different shapes: the first call of the day writes the brief and returns a snapshot, every later call is served from cache with no snapshot key at all.

The app itself is now native the whole way down. CapApp-SPM, the Capacitor plugin schemes and config.xml are out of the Xcode project, and otool -L on the built binary links no WebKit at all. Five tabs — deck, money, markets, inbox, more — over the same /api/* the website draws, with every decodable written against a real captured response rather than a reading of the handler. That distinction had already cost this app once: SchwabResponse expected {accounts: […]} when the endpoint returns a bare array, and the panel said "COULD NOT LOAD" while the endpoint answered perfectly.

124The message that never sent was a loop that never ended

Four complaints, one afternoon: messages that go nowhere, a chat box that forgets, one conversation at a time, and pictures that look like pictures a computer made. Only the first looked like a mystery, and it was not one — the answer was sitting in a run file the whole time.

"I can't send him messages" was never about the connection. The run file for the request that appeared to vanish is on disk and says so plainly: 2026-08-18-2051-make-a-website-for-a-farmers-market-apple-stand-.md, started 20:51, still marked status: running at 21:26. Thirty-five minutes, and it had not failed. It was going round.

The website agent had set itself a check it could never pass. It ran

pkill vite; npm run dev & sleep 8; curl localhost:5173

and read the output to decide whether the page looked right. A Vite dev server returns <div id="root"></div> and React renders in the browser, so curl could not see the page however many times it asked. Every pass looked like a failure, so it tried again.

The step ceiling did not save it and could not. Twenty steps growing to a hundred and twenty sounds like a bound; at thirty to sixty seconds a turn on a 33B model it is an hour and a half. Steps are not the unit the person waiting is counting in. There is a wall clock now — CONFIG.turnDeadlineMs, eight minutes, checked inside the loop — and hitting it goes through the same salvage pass as running out of steps, so the files it wrote are still written and the answer says which limit stopped it. improve and the scheduled jobs pass deadlineMs: 0, because nobody is waiting on those.

A first pass at this blamed the browser, measuring fetch('http://127.0.0.1:8767/health') from https://cleetusai.com and getting TypeError: Failed to fetch. That test ran on /login, which does not get the CSP carve-out; /reach has its own, naming the loopback port under connect-src, and it still works. Wrong page, wrong conclusion. Worth writing down because the symptom was real and the explanation was not.

From the phone there is a hard ceiling, and it is measured: POST https://me.cleetusai.com/chat answers 524 after 125.2s. Cloudflare cuts an origin request at about two minutes, so every slow answer came back as a failure even when the Mac went on to finish it. cleetusd writes the answer into the thread before it writes it to the socket, so the page now watches the conversation as well as the socket: a 524 became a slow answer instead of a lost one.

/reach is served from cleetusd itself now, at http://127.0.0.1:8767/reach — the same file, read off disk per request like /editor, with the site's Touch ID script swapped for a marker the page reads. On the Mac that is same-origin: no CORS, no probe, no Cloudflare, no ceiling. The cross-origin path still works and is still there for the phone; it is just no longer the only way in, and it no longer depends on a CSP file in another repo staying correct forever.

And /chat logged nothing when it failed. It is the route the tunnel uses, so every message from the phone came through the one path in the daemon that could die without leaving a line anywhere.

One conversation per agent, all of them running at once

The page had a single #log that was emptied and repainted on every agent switch, and a single disabled flag standing in for "is something in flight". That is the same assumption twice — that you talk to one agent at a time — and it is wrong in an obvious way: a picture takes a minute on this GPU and there is no reason to watch it render instead of asking the poker agent something.

An agent owns a pane now: its transcript, its thread, its own busy flag. Panes are never destroyed, so a reply that arrives for an agent you are not looking at is written into that agent's pane and is simply there when you go back. The rail shows which agents are still thinking. Verified with two agents fired seconds apart: both marked working, both answers landed in their own transcripts, neither in the other's.

Clear chat empties the box and keeps the conversation. It sets a cleared flag that list() honours and search() does not, so the thread leaves the rail and the screen while recall_chat still finds every word of it. A delete would have been the same one line of code and would have thrown away the thing that makes this worth having.

Why the pictures looked like pictures a computer made

The default was sdxl-turbo, three steps, guidance 0.0. Everything people mean by "it looks AI" is in that line, and one part of it is not a tuning choice at all: turbo models are distilled to run without classifier-free guidance, and with guidance off the negative prompt is not weak, it is inert. There is no unconditional pass for it to steer away from. Every --negative ever passed to this tool was accepted, passed through, and discarded in silence.

The default is RealVisXL now — a photoreal fine-tune, thirty steps, guidance 4.5, about forty seconds an image — with negatives that actually apply, aspect ratios (square is the wrong shape for a photograph of a person, and it was the only shape on offer), and a short photographic style appended when the prompt has not already named one. sdxl-turbo is still there and is now described as what it is: a three-second draft.

Both defaults are trimmed to seventy-seven tokens, because that is CLIP's limit and diffusers does not error past it — it truncates, and says so only in a warning on stderr. The first version of the new defaults overflowed at eighty-one tokens and lost the tail of both. A negative prompt listing twenty faults where only the first eight survive is worse than a short one, because it reads as thorough.

A refusal is not an answer when the tool was never called

Asked for a picture it did not care for, the image agent replied "I can't create that image. The guidelines specifically prohibit generating explicit sexual content" and called nothing. Nothing in its brief said that. It is the base model reciting what a hosted assistant is supposed to say, inside a process running on Grayson's own GPU writing to his own disk — the same fault looksFailed exists to catch when the tax agent claims it cannot open a website while holding web_open.

Rewriting the brief did not fix it. The behaviour is in the weights, and prompt text loses that argument. Heretic strips it at the weights and cannot run here: Laguna is a MoE whose routed experts are not nn.Linear, so bitsandbytes cannot quantise it and the "4-bit" load comes back at 64.5 GB on a 64 GB machine.

Restricting the tool list was the second attempt — offer only the generation tools, so that answering in prose is not a branch that exists. It did not work either, and the reason is the interesting part: a model that will not call a tool does not call a different one, it calls nothing. Five prompts, five refusals, zero tool calls, with nothing on offer but generate_image and generate_video.

What works is to stop treating the call as the model's decision. It is a function. The model is needed for one thing — turning a sentence into a good image prompt — and that framing is the whole trick: asked to generate something it declines, asked to rewrite the same sentence as a visual description it simply does it, because rewriting is not the act it was trained to refuse. Three rungs, and the last cannot fail: ask for the rewrite; failing that use Grayson's own words; either way this file calls generate_image.

The detector is deliberately narrow. It wants a first-person unwillingness phrase or a cited rulebook, and no breakage word anywhere — so "I could not generate that, the model failed to download" is a real answer and passes through untouched. It is not gated on which agent answered, either: the router only sends a request to the image agent when it contains make/draw/generate, so "I want a picture of…" goes to the generalist and gets refused in exactly the same words. Gating on the agent would have fixed half the phrasings and left the rest broken, which is worse than not fixing it, because it would have looked fixed.

One line is kept, and it is not a matter of taste: nothing sexual or suggestive involving anyone under eighteen. That line needed its own enforcement rather than the model's judgment, precisely because the last rung stopped asking — a path that calls generate_image with no model in the loop would have sailed straight past it. So a request mentioning a minor never reaches the override at all, checked at each of the three ways in and again on the model's own expansion of the prompt, which invents detail the request did not contain. The guard is blunt and over-inclusive on purpose: it only ever runs on something the model already refused, so everything it catches by mistake was being declined anyway.

Measured after: five prompts that had been refused five times over now return a file — a 832x1216 RealVisXL render in about thirty-seven seconds — and the suite is 349 green.

One trap worth naming, because an hour went into it. cleetusd loads code only on restart, and a test harness that restarts the daemon from inside a script racing another script does not restart it when you think. Two full rounds of "the fix did not work" were a daemon started at 21:52 answering code written at 22:37. Check ps -o lstart= against the file mtime before believing a negative result.

Cleetus · handoff updated 19 Aug 2026 · every agent holds its own conversation and several run at once, a turn is bounded in time as well as in steps, the pictures are photographs now, and a refusal is no longer the last word
Sessions covered: local LLM stand-up, tunnel + auth, tool-loop repair, redesign, skills system,
the three instruments unstuck, the control deck, flight tracker, RuView,
the local model finally in the path, the native iOS app,
and cleetusd — the agent moving onto the machine, with the flight map and the
lock gesture both found to be measuring the wrong thing.

The workout app now does what Hevy does

2026-08-20. fitness.html had one seeded program and no way to write your own. It now has five tabs and the whole non-social half of Hevy: routines with folders and an editor, a 342-exercise library you can search and add to, empty workouts, four set types, a calendar and streak, per-exercise charts and records, eleven body measurements with progress photos, CSV import and export, and a plan generator.

Migrations 020, 021 and 022 are applied to prod (joradwbgaqzpkrjicxyv). Nine new tables; nothing existing was touched. The code shipped in the deploy on commit d4b66dc.

The two things worth remembering

  • Volume on bodyweight work follows Hevy's rules, not weight × reps. A pull-up moves bodyweight + belt; an assisted pull-up moves bodyweight − assistance. exercises.counts_bodyweight marks the twelve movements that use all of it. Custom exercises never count bodyweight, which is also Hevy's rule.
  • prKindsFor() in _lib/fitness.js decides which records an exercise can hold. It exists because the old code awarded a heaviest-weight PR on assisted exercises — where less weight is the better performance, so the award fired backwards. Weighted bodyweight gets no 1RM. Assisted gets rep records only.

The generator is an algorithm, deliberately

/api/fitness/trainer picks from gym_exercises, so it can only ever prescribe a movement that exists and that the room can load. Hevy describes theirs the same way. It reports the slots it could not fill rather than quietly handing over a shorter programme — which is how the missing home-gym leg extension was found.

docs/hevy-parity.md is the 97-item checklist and says what was checked rather than assumed. docs/hevy-audit-helpcentre.md records what a second pass through Hevy's own help centre proved wrong the first time.

The room-sensing night: three WiFi nodes, one dead end, and the trigger that actually works

2026-08-20. RuView went from nothing to three provisioned, named, positioned CSI nodes with a server that survives reboot — and then to a measured, unambiguous verdict that it cannot detect whether you are in the room. The desk trigger you wanted got built on something else and works. Read the RuView section before touching any of that hardware again; it will save you a night.

Running right now

  • com.cleetus.ruview — sensing server, LaunchAgent, KeepAlive verified by kill -9 (back in ~2s). REST on 3000, WebSocket on 3001 (separate listeners — the page pointed at 3000 and could never have connected), UDP 5005. --udp-bind 0.0.0.0 is load-bearing: the default is loopback, which silently drops every frame from a LAN node while logging nothing.
  • com.cleetus.desk-trigger — desk presence from HID idle time. ~/desk-trigger/state.json, hooks in ~/desk-trigger/hooks/. python3 desk_trigger.py --self-test → 9/9.
  • com.cleetus.airpad — C920 → Dell (display 5). It was found driving the LG at runtime despite config saying 5; retargeted and persisted.
  • cleetusai.com/protocols — live, editable, nav button on the deck. Stored in app_kv, changes need no deploy. No Run button on purpose: the orchestrator does not exist.
  • Mac Wi-Fi IP pinned to 192.168.1.134 (networksetup -setmanualwithdhcprouter). Every node targets that address from NVS; a DHCP change would silently blind all three. Revert with networksetup -setdhcp "Wi-Fi".

RuView: why it cannot see you, and the one thing that would change that

STATION-MODE CSI IS THE WHOLE STORY. The firmware never calls esp_wifi_set_promiscuous — the only promiscuous call is esp_ieee802154_set_promiscuous, which is the Zigbee radio used for time sync. So a node can sense exactly one path: the link between itself and the AP it is associated with. Nothing else.

Grayson's router is downstairs. So every sensed path arrives up through the floor as scattered multipath, and a body seated above boards at 0.75 m never intersects it. That is the entire failure, and it is geometry, not configuration.

Measured, do not re-derive: empty room vs seated, 75 s per condition, MAC filter active, clean boot-time thresholds, verified geometry → AUC 0.473 / 0.474 / 0.444 on nodes 2/1/3. 0.5 is a coin flip; a usable trigger needs >0.9. There is no threshold to find. Raw data in ~/Desktop/Cleetus/empty-filtered.json and seated-filtered.json.

Dead ends already paid for:

  • Two calibrations exist. The server field model needs 12,000 frames (not the 600 DEFAULT_MIN_FRAMES implies) and refuses to finalise below target. Each node separately learns its presence threshold from its first EDGE_CALIB_FRAMES = 1200 frames after boot, as mean + 3σ. Boot a node with a person in the room and it learns "ambient = a human" and never fires again. This was real, was fixed, and did not fix occupancy.
  • --filter-mac was genuinely necessary: unfiltered, a node accepts CSI from every transmitter in range, so motion_energy (a variance) measures transmitter diversity, not motion. Fixing it dropped the empty-room floor 6.74 → 4.99 and false presence 14% → 3%. It did not create separation.
  • Peer-to-peer sensing (point one node's filter at another node's MAC) captures CSI but at 0.1 frames/second — see station mode above. Forcing traffic by pinging the peer reaches 2–7 pps; 20 parallel pingers (~200 ICMP/s) knocked two nodes offline. Firmware also gates CSI at ~50 Hz.
  • Multistatic fusion is unusable: timestamp spread across nodes is median 984 ms, p90 2.26 s. Do not keep raising WDP_GUARD_INTERVAL_US; a guard wide enough to admit those frames is fusing captures seconds apart.
  • /api/v1/pose/current returns 17-keypoint skeletons with every keypoint at confidence 0.0 — no trained model ships. It also claimed 4 people in an empty room.

The only next step worth taking: put a 2.4 GHz access point in the room (extender or mesh node, ~$25, same SSID). The nodes associate to the strongest AP, so the sensed path becomes an in-room one at body height that a chair can actually block. Then re-provision all three --filter-mac to the new BSSID and re-run the identical AUC test. If it does not move, the honest conclusion is that this hardware cannot do occupancy here.

The fleet

nodenameposition (m)MACIP
1guitar-stand0.000, 0.000, 0.75e8:f6:0a:8f:1e:fc192.168.1.171
2desk1.742, 2.184, 0.753c:0f:02:ca:c3:38192.168.1.214
3turtle-shell2.642, 0.000, 0.753c:0f:02:ca:cb:c4192.168.1.170

Seat measured at (1.471, 1.438), solved from two distances and checked against an independent third — 3.2 cm residual. Full geometry, provenance and every verdict: ~/Desktop/Cleetus/room-geometry.json. AP BSSID (2.4 GHz) is 38:17:b1:34:e7:5e — note the router's LAN MAC is ...:5a, four different; guessing it from ARP would filter on a transmitter that never sends.

Node 3 is sick. Measured in the same spot as the others: −71.5 dBm and 11.7 pps against node 1's −51.5 dBm and 30.4 pps. A 20 dB deficit is ~100× less power. Its WS2812 stays dark while a solid red power LED is on, which means the firmware LED task is not running — consistent with brownout resets. Swap its cable and PSU before assuming the board is dead; the desk node had the identical symptom and the Mac's USB fixed it.

Hue: unblocked, and the audit is stale about it

The Rev 1.5 audit lists Phase 0 as BLOCKED on buying a Hue Bridge. There has been one on the network the whole time: BSB002 at 192.168.1.70, bridgeid C42996FFFE689ADE, API 1.78.0. arp alone will not find it (cache only holds recently contacted hosts, and its OUI is c4:29:96, not the 00:17:88 everyone greps for) — use dns-sd -t 4 -B _hue._tcp or curl https://discovery.meethue.com.

Next action, needs a human: press the bridge's link button, then POST {"devicetype":"cleetus#mac"} to https://192.168.1.70/api within ~30 s to mint an API key. Then, per audit finding A6, resolve groups by UUID not name: GET /clip/v2/resource/room, match metadata.name, read the services[] entry with rtype: grouped_light, and cache it. Hue bulbs are Zigbee and can never be RuView transmitters.

Known broken

  • Every Cleetus SMS alert is silently failing. Twilio error 30032 — toll-free number never verified. APNs push works; SMS does not. This affects all alerts, not one.
  • The Brio 101 delivers zero video frames in every pixel format including none. Ruled out by experiment: not permissions, not contention (the C920 delivered 10 frames from the same shell while AirPad was using it). Its AirPad instance is configured but not loaded: config.brio.json + com.cleetus.airpad-brio.plist. Try a direct port rather than the hub chain.
  • Node 3, above.

Open, in the order I would do them

  1. Press the Hue link button and let the lighting layer of /protocols become executable. Cheapest win available.
  2. Put real commands in ~/desk-trigger/hooks/on-arrive. The trigger fires reliably today and does nothing but write to a log.
  3. Re-calibrate AirPad at http://127.0.0.1:8768. The pointer now follows the thumb/index midpoint (pointer_anchor: pinch_mid) so pinching no longer drags the cursor — but calibration fits from that same raw point, so the aim is off by ~half a thumb span until refitted. Then try lowering click_lead from 0.12 toward 0; it exists to rewind drift that no longer happens.
  4. Buy the $25 2.4 GHz extender if RuView is still wanted.
  5. Apple Home: there is no macOS API for a daemon to control HomeKit. The route is Shortcuts.app + shortcuts run "Name" against an allowlist. Nothing is wired yet.
  6. The /room 3D view was designed but not built — needs a LiDAR scan (Scaniverse/Polycam, export GLB). three.js is vendored at /assets/vendor/three.module.min.js; no GLTF loader vendored yet.

Traps that cost time tonight

  • launchctl kickstart -k does not re-read an edited plist. It restarts the process with launchd's cached job definition. A flag added to the plist was simply absent from argv and nothing warned. Use unload then load -w, then verify with ps -p <pid> -o command=.
  • launchd's PATH omits /usr/sbin, so ioreg was not found and the desk trigger reported unknown forever. Use absolute paths in agents.
  • SSIDs are case-sensitive. "Not A Meth Lab" fails; it is Not a meth lab. Symptom is Haven't to connect to a suitable AP now! with rssi=0. networksetup -listpreferredwirelessnetworks en1 gives the true case; system_profiler redacts SSIDs without Location permission.
  • provision.py's real flag is --tdm-total, not the --tdm-nodes the web docs and DeepWiki both claim. Read --help.
  • Malformed --node-positions entries are silently skipped, which shifts every later node into the wrong slot. Always confirm the log says Configured 3 node positions.

The alarm that had to be told its first stage does not work

2026-08-21. Grayson asked for a room alarm with the two sensors in conjunction — RuView raising the first alert and the camera picking it up once RuView has fired. That is now built, running under launchd and proved end to end: a real push landed on his phone, the Hue lamps flashed, and a confirmed person was suppressed by name. It also came with a measurement that says the first stage does not earn its place, and the honest version of that finding is written into the baseline file rather than into a comment nobody reads.

Three other things came loose on the way: the Hue bridge is unblocked, 23 secrets were being read with their own documentation glued to the end, and the RuView plist has been unparseable XML for as long as it has existed.

Hue: minted, wired, and answering

Grayson pressed the link button; the key was minted inside the 30-second window and lives in cleetus.env as HUE_APP_KEY. Bridge BSB002 at 192.168.1.70, one room (Bedroom), four Essential lamps. src/hue.mjs is the library and lights_read / lights_set are the tools — asked "are the lights on in my room right now", Cleetus called lights_read and answered correctly. That is the lighting layer of /protocols becoming executable.

A group brightness write is not reversible by restoring the lamps that were on

The first blink set the group to 100% and then put back only the lamps that had been lit. That silently overwrote the stored brightness of the two lamps that were OFF, from 33% and 39% to 100% — so the next person to touch the wall switch gets full brightness with nothing to explain it. Snapshot and restore are per-LIGHT in hue.mjs now, and they include brightness for lamps that are off. Groups are addressed by UUID, never by name (audit finding A6): a rename in the Hue app would otherwise retarget whatever "bedroom" meant.

RuView cannot gate the camera. This is the second feature to fail, and it was measured properly

The 2026-08-20 verdict (AUC 0.473/0.474/0.444) was measured on motion_energy from /api/v1/edge-vitals. That is not the only number the server produces. motion_band_power, a per-node CSI feature carried on the sensing WebSocket, had never been tested — and it is a much richer signal, so it was the one thing that might have made a first stage real.

It is not. Labelled by the camera across 92 s with nothing moving and 48 s of real movement, combined AUC is 0.60. But AUC is the wrong question for a gate. The rule an alarm actually runs — three consecutive frames over a threshold — has no operating point that is both quiet and catching:

thresholdfalse camera wakes / hour, still roomcatches / minute of movement
6726,418139.5
8863,20917.4
9426267.5
9533136.2
983390

The reason is worse than noise: the still room's own peaks run HIGHER than the moving room's (p99 941.6 against 893.4 on node 1; max 1026 against 978). Thresholding the top end — which is the only thing an alarm can do — is therefore worse than a coin flip. Stage one runs anyway, at the quietest point that still catches anything (313 wakes/hour, 6.2 catches per minute of movement), because a false positive costs one 1.5-second camera probe that the camera then vetoes and nobody is told about. But the camera heartbeat is what is actually guarding the room, and roomwatch-baseline.json says so in verdict rather than leaving it to be rediscovered.

Everything above re-derives itself: roomwatch baseline recomputes the table and rewrites the verdict, so the day a 2.4 GHz AP goes in the room and the geometry changes, one command says whether it worked.

The first baseline believed the filenames, and they were wrong

Version one trusted that empty.jsonl held the empty room. It produced a baseline in which the empty room moved MORE than the occupied one — not a sensor fault. A person asked to "move around for four minutes" sits still after ninety seconds, and a person asked to leave takes a moment to go and comes back before the timer ends. Auto-exposure was ruled out by measurement (0.43 grey levels of drift), which left only the labels.

Labels now come from something that was actually looking: every camera probe records what fraction of the frame changed, each RF frame is labelled by the probe covering it, and the evidence frames are saved to roomwatch/clips/. The verified empty stretch reads changed_pct of exactly 0.00 for forty consecutive probes, bracketed by 98.6% and 93.6% as he walked out and back — and the picture at the quiet point shows an empty room, which is how the label was confirmed rather than argued.

A mean pixel difference cannot tell a person from a light switch

A body crossing a room changes 10% of pixels a lot; a brightness shift changes 100% of them a little, and the mean is the same. The metric is now the fraction of pixels that move by more than 12 grey levels after each frame's mean is removed. A still room scores exactly 0.00%; a person moving scores 3–98%. That is why the camera holds the veto and RuView does not.

The same helper now detects a frozen stream. A stalled capture keeps answering 200 with identical bytes, and every difference goes to zero — which the old code would have reported as "nothing is moving", forever, in exactly the situation where an alarm most needs to speak. Real sensor noise never produces a byte-identical pair.

Two false alarms, both fixed, both worth keeping in mind

The first armed run raised a full alarm — push, lights, the lot — on Grayson, who was in the room the whole time and had turned away from the lens. A face recogniser needs a FACE; motion does not wait for one, and most real movement in a room shows a back or an elbow. Two changes:

  • Recognition is a fact about the room with a lifetime, not about the current frame. Seeing a known face within the last two minutes suppresses the alarm. An intruder in an empty house never produces a known face at all, so this costs nothing against the case that matters. The watcher also seeds this at startup, or it treats whoever just launched it as an intruder for its first two minutes.
  • A second look four seconds later. One glimpse is a curtain or a phone screen waking; two is a person.
  • An exit delay on arm (default 60 s). Arming from inside the room and having it fire while you walk to the door is the oldest false alarm there is.

23 secrets were being read with their own comments attached

cleetus.env documents itself inline — APNS_KEY_ID=M54J9L88Y9  # Key ID of the APNs .p8. Not secret. — and config.mjs handed back the key ID with that sentence glued to it. The failure named the wrong thing: the push reported a missing file at a path ending Not secret..p8, which reads as a missing .p8 rather than a parser bug. PLAID_SECRET, AUTH_SECRET, ENCRYPTION_KEY, MS_WEB_CLIENT_SECRET and every APNS_* are written the same way — and the doctor has been reporting plaid and outlook down. Only unquoted values are trimmed, only at whitespace before a hash; every one of the file's keys was checked, and exactly 23 values changed, all of them by removing a trailing comment.

Push has never worked, and now it does

The note that APNs key B8S6L34V7K is Production-only is stale. A replacement, M54J9L88Y9, was installed on 18 Aug and speaks both environments — proved without notifying anyone by posting a 64-zero device token to each host and getting BadDeviceToken (credentials fine, device fake) from both, rather than BadEnvironmentKeyInToken from either. One iOS device is registered. roomwatch test-alert then got a real 200 from api.sandbox.push.apple.com.

roomwatch talks to Apple directly from the Mac, not through the cloud endpoint. It is an alarm: it has to work when the tunnel is down, and /api/notify also fans out to Twilio, whose toll-free number has never been verified (error 30032) so every SMS it attempts fails silently.

Running now

  • com.cleetus.roomwatch — loaded, running, disarmed. KeepAlive verified by kill -9 (back in under 40 s). It restarts only on a crash, never on the clean non-zero exit that means "not calibrated" — a crash-looping alarm that pages nobody is worse than one that is plainly off.
  • bin/roomwatch — sample, baseline, status, arm [seconds], disarm, once, test-alert, run, selftest (8/8).
  • bin/midictl — rebuilt with its source at src/swift/midictl.swift; the previous binary had none. New stream mode reads a script on stdin through one open CoreMIDI client, because 128 separate invocations take seven seconds and cannot time a test a human is watching.
  • Two new tools, 49 total. The doctor's tool-list check is green again.

Traps that cost time tonight

  • The sensing WebSocket path is /ws/sensing, and it is served on BOTH 3000 and 3001. The earlier note that these are separate listeners and only 3001 works is half right — the PATH is the part that matters. A bare ws://127.0.0.1:3001 is refused with a non-101 status and no explanation.
  • zsh aborts a whole command line when a glob matches nothing. An rm -f a b clips/cal-* deleted none of the three because the last pattern had no match, so a fresh recording appended to a stale one and the sample count silently disagreed with the file. Separated afterwards by timestamp; use setopt null_glob or avoid the glob.
  • plutil -lint says the RuView plist is fine and plistlib throws. launchd's parser is lenient about a double hyphen inside an XML comment, which that file had because it documented its own --udp-bind flags. Rewritten without the leading hyphens; argv verified byte-identical to the running process afterwards.
  • Node 22 has a built-in WebSocket global, so no ws package is needed — and there isn't one installed.

Open, in the order worth doing them

  1. Decide whether to arm it. roomwatch arm gives 60 s to leave. Armed means "nobody should be here", so confirmed motion alarms; known-face suppression is a safety net, not the primary logic.
  2. The $25 2.4 GHz access point is still the only thing that would change the RuView verdict, and now there is a one-command test for it.
  3. The Brio 101 still delivers zero frames, so the room has one eye. A second camera covering the angle the C920 misses is the cheapest real improvement to the alarm.
  4. com.cleetus.studio is loaded but not running — port 8765 is already held by another instance started outside launchd. Two copies, one supervised.
  5. Put real commands in ~/desk-trigger/hooks/on-arrive; it still only writes to a log, and the lights are now something it could actually turn on.

The app had the whole Mac one tunnel away and never asked it anything

2026-08-21. Grayson asked to be able to talk to Cleetus about any file on his computer from his phone. That was never a missing endpoint. /api/cleetusd has existed for weeks, is session-gated, carries the bearer token a phone cannot hold, and reaches the process on the Mac with the filesystem, the shell and the cameras. ChatView simply called /api/cleetus instead — which runs inside a Cloudflare worker with no disk, so asked about a file it answered, correctly, that it could not see one. The app has been talking to the wrong Cleetus.

Fixed, built, and installed on the paired iPhone 15. Proved through the phone's exact path: POST /api/cleetusd → served_by: mac-studio, has_filesystem: true, used: ["room_alarm"].

  • The Mac is the default, with a segmented control to switch to the cloud.
  • The 30-second request timeout was the second bug. Right for a panel, far too short for a tool loop that lists directories and reads files before it has a first word to say. 180 s on the Mac path — the old ceiling turned "he is thinking" into "could not reach Cleetus".
  • Falling back to the cloud is labelled, never silent. An unmarked downgrade is exactly how "he is local" survived months of Claude answering every message.
  • The header shows which brain answered and which tools it used, so "there are 21 files in bin" is visibly a list_dir result rather than a guess.

Arming the alarm from the door

You arm an alarm at the moment you are not at the Mac, so room_alarm (tool 50) reads roomwatch and arms or disarms it — "arm the room" typed into the app on the way out now works, with a 60-second exit delay.

It is a tool and deliberately not a route on /api/reach. That proxy is read-only by exact name and refuses anything that changes state on the machine; widening it for one feature would trade a carefully drawn boundary for a convenience. The phone already had an authenticated path. Status also reports whether the camera can currently see — armed and able to see are different facts, and an alarm with a frozen stream is the failure nobody notices — and it carries the measured caveat about the WiFi stage so the model cannot describe RuView as working.

Feature parity: what the web has and the app does not

Computed from the two trees rather than from memory. Twenty native views cover deck, money, markets, inbox, brief, body, peptides, supplements, taxes, books, advisor, music and chat. Thirteen web surfaces have no native screen at all:

surfacewhy it matterssize
fitnessthe Hevy-parity workout app — five tabs, routines, 342-exercise library, charts, measurementslarge
nutritiondiary, meals, photo portions, targets — pairs with fitnesslarge
reachthe Mac's own dashboard; the closest thing to "my computer, from my phone"medium
ruviewthe room, and now the alarm's own viewmedium
protocolsthe room chart; now genuinely executable since the lights answersmall
timeline, hub, studioread-mostly surfacesmedium
flights, sky, solar, spacefour related sky surfaces, one screen with tabsmedium
brewthe De'Longhi bridgesmall

This is a project, not a session. The order worth taking it in: reach and ruview/alarm first (they are what a phone is actually for — the machine you are away from), then fitness and nutrition together since they share a data model, then the four sky surfaces as one tabbed screen, then the read-mostly rest. Nothing here needs new backend: every one of these already has JSON endpoints under functions/api/, which is why the widget works.

The keyboards do not take host LED control

Answered by experiment rather than by manual. Six staged bursts — KeyLab DAW notes, DAW CCs, Arturia LED SysEx on both the MIDI and DAW ports, and MPK88 pad Note-Ons across both ports and two channels — and nothing lit on either keyboard. Both answer a Device Inquiry (MPK88 → 47, KeyLab → 00 20 6B), so the channel is real and the LEDs are simply not on it. Arturia's documented LED SysEx is for the KeyLab MkII, a different product line.

So "cut the lights on the keyboards" comes back to power, and the answer is now cheaper than it was: there is a working Hue bridge. A Hue smart plug on the strip feeding the keyboards would be controllable through the same lights_set tool that already works, with no new integration at all. No per-port USB power control exists on macOS, and uhubctl needs a PPPS-capable hub, which these are not.

Cleetus Fit, and why the first design looked generated

2026-08-21. Training and food are their own iOS app now — com.cleetus.fit, five tabs, at ~/cleetusv2/cleetus-fit. The website is untouched. Both apps build and run in the simulator; the phone was off WiFi, so a watcher is waiting to install to it the moment it comes back.

The split is not tidiness. A workout app is used with one hand, mid-set, with the phone balanced on a bench, and it was competing for the same thumb as a bank balance and an inbox. Removed from the main app: BodyView, its More row, and the deck's nutrition and training panels. The two widgets were deliberately left alone — deleting a widget already placed on a home screen breaks it silently, and that is Grayson's call to make.

It runs on the endpoints that already existed

There is no new backend and deliberately isn't one. Every screen is a native front end over /api/fitness/* and /api/nutrition/*, so the PR rules and the volume maths stay in one place and the site cannot drift from the app. That is what made splitting it out cheap.

The first design looked AI-generated, and here is the list

Written down because it is a checklist, not a mood: floating rounded cards at a uniform 14pt radius; a centred stacked wordmark in a silver gradient; a soft radial glow behind everything; four identical progress rings in a row; perfect symmetry throughout. Each of those is the default an AI reaches for. Together they are a template, and no amount of polish rescues them.

The rebuild commits to something specific: gym equipment. 2pt corners because racks and plates are cut, not moulded. Full-bleed modules separated by hairlines like a spec sheet — nothing floats, nothing has a drop shadow. Tabular monospaced numerals flush left against a fixed 62pt label gutter, so the number is the content and never competes with a ring drawn around it. Barbell knurling — the 45° cross-hatch, the one texture that belongs to this sport — on a 4pt strip that marks a live module and nothing else. Four identical rings became four stacked load bars, which can actually be compared against each other. And one signal colour, hazard amber, spent only on a personal record.

Two bugs the screenshots caught that the build never would

  • preview on a routine is an array of plain STRINGS, not objects. Modelling it as a struct failed the entire RoutinesReply decode, so the Train tab was empty while the rest of the screen loaded perfectly. 31 routines, invisible, no error anywhere. The file's own header comment says to build models against a real response; this one was built against an assumption.
  • A session left open overnight is normal — this account's had been running for two days — and the elapsed clock rendered it 4241:01. Now 2d 22h.

Neither is visible to a compiler, and both were obvious the moment a screenshot was actually looked at. Building it and reading the log is not the same as looking at it.

Getting a screen without touching the phone

DEBUG-only, matching the main app's -startTab convention: -screen train|food|body|library|workout, plus CLEETUS_FIT_PASSWORD in the process environment (via SIMCTL_CHILD_…) so any screen can be reached for a screenshot without driving the UI by hand. The password lives for one launch and is written nowhere.

cleetus-fit/install-to-phone.sh builds and installs both apps; it needs only that the phone be on the same WiFi. Signing is automatic against team UU8F9AT8MU and no Apple login is involved at any point.

The Pi panel shows the room now, not just the guess about it

The RuView dashboard displayed WiFi traces — the channel that was measured, twice, not to separate an occupied room from an empty one. It now carries the C920 live above them: the same camera roomwatch confirms motion with, so the panel shows the exact frames the alarm decides on rather than a second opinion from a different sensor. The traces sit below it, because that is what they are worth and the layout should say so.

  • /camera.mjpg is proxied by the service, which holds the bearer. An <img> cannot attach one, and this box serves the whole tailnet, so a token in the markup would be a token given away.
  • Backpressure is waited on explicitly. The panel is slower than a 125 KB/s MJPEG stream, and writing without waiting for drain buffers the difference in the process until it is killed.
  • Headers are guarded against a second write — that exact bug took cleetusd down once.
  • An MJPEG <img> that dies does not fire a second error; it simply stops updating, which on a wall panel is indistinguishable from a very still room. A 12-second watchdog re-arms the source.

Verified by screenshotting the panel, not by systemctl is-active, which has lied on this box before. The first shot showed node 3 clipped off the bottom; the camera pane was resized to fit all three.

The Pi dashboard existed only on an SD card

It is now mirrored, tracked, at ~/cleetusd/pi/ruview-dash/ with its deploy and verification steps. There was also an untracked ~/ruview-dash on the Mac that had drifted stale against what was actually running — the worst state for a directory to be in, because it looks authoritative and is not. Re-synced from the Pi, with a WHERE-THIS-LIVES.md pointing at the tracked copy.

Still open

  1. Feature parity is a project, not a session. Eleven web surfaces still have no native screen anywhere: reach, ruview, protocols, timeline, hub, studio, flights, sky, solar, space, brew. All already have JSON endpoints. Order worth taking them in: reach and ruview first, then the four sky surfaces as one tabbed screen.
  2. Cleetus Fit has no app icon — the asset catalogue has an empty AppIcon slot, so it ships with the default grey tile.
  3. The two fitness widgets still point at the main app. Moving them to Cleetus Fit means a widget extension target and re-placing them on the home screen.
  4. Targets are unset on the nutrition account, so the load bars show intake only. The app says so rather than drawing a bar against a number nobody chose.

What RuView actually is, measured three ways, and the number that would have lied

2026-08-22. Grayson asked for RuView fixed until it "works every time and scans be at my desk". It is not fixed, and this section is the evidence for why, because the honest answer took three independent measurements and one of them nearly came out the other way.

Two claims in this document were wrong. Both are corrected

  • "The firmware never calls esp_wifi_set_promiscuous." It does — csi_collector.c:530, with a promiscuous RX callback beside it. Station mode was never the whole story.
  • The MGMT-only filter is not a limitation left on by accident. It is a deliberate workaround: DATA frames cause 100–500 interrupts/sec that crash Core 0 in wDev_ProcessFiq. Boards without a display already upgrade to MGMT+DATA (main.c:500), and this fleet's CSI rates — 30/54/40 Hz against the ~20 Hz MGMT-only produces — say these boards are already in the better mode. There is no free win left in the radio configuration.

The thing nobody had tried: fitting a model

Every previous verdict, including both from the night before, thresholded one feature — motion_energy (AUC 0.47), then motion_band_power (0.60). A single-feature threshold is the weakest classifier that exists, and its failure says one projection of the signal is uninformative, not that the signal is.

What made a real test possible is a trustworthy label: com.cleetus.desk-trigger reads HID idle time, which measures a person using the desk directly. bin/ruview-collect.mjs now samples every feature the server emits at 1 Hz and stamps it with that label; bin/ruview-train.py fits windowed mean/std/min/max/slope over 27 raw features.

A random split claimed 0.990. The honest number was 0.454

That 0.990 is the number that would have been shipped as "RuView works". It is leakage: rows one second apart are near-duplicates, so a random k-fold trains on the test row's own neighbours. Cross-validation here uses contiguous time blocks, and the trainer deliberately prints both figures side by side so the gap can never be missed again.

Blocked CV over the first hour: 0.454 — worse than a coin flip, with per-block scores swinging 0.852 / 0.363 / 0.147. That instability is itself the tell: with one class-change in the data, each block is nearly all one class, so the model learns which hour it is looking at rather than whether anyone is there.

So ask it a sharper question

bin/ruview-transitions.py controls for drift by construction: around each moment he sat down or got up, compare the four minutes before with the four minutes after. Both sides are minutes apart, so the environment has not moved, and it is the question that actually matters — does the channel change when he arrives?

transitionsmean best-of-21 AUCchance baseline
30.699~0.60
70.706~0.60

Stable at roughly 0.10 above chance — and the baseline is ~0.6 rather than 0.5 because the score is the BEST of 21 features picked per transition. Worse for the hypothesis than the number itself: a different feature wins every time (node 2 motion_band_power, node 3 dominant_freq, node 3 rssi, node 2 rssi). A body really perturbing the channel would move the same feature the same way. Different winners each time is what noise looks like.

Standing verdict: three independent measurements, on two features and one fitted model, all say this deployment cannot tell an occupied room from an empty one. The collector is still running (capped at 200 MB, ~4 days) so the answer keeps improving on its own; re-run the two scripts any time. The only remaining move is physical — a 2.4 GHz AP in the room so the sensed path crosses a body at desk height instead of running to a router downstairs.

Meanwhile, the thing he actually wanted already works

The panel now reads GRAYSON · at the desk. It was showing OCCUPANCY · CANNOT TELL while a camera two panes above it knew the person's name.

/presence on cleetusd answers WHO from the sensor that can. Identity comes from the camera and face recogniser, cached 8 s because the recogniser spawns a Python process. A name persists two minutes with its age — a recogniser needs a FACE, and looking down at the desk is not the room emptying. A frozen stream reports as frozen, never as "nobody".

The C920 cannot see the desk, and the answer was already on the machine

It points across the room at the door — which is right for the alarm — so someone at the desk sits behind it, facing away, with the chair back in shot. The Brio that does face the desk still delivers zero frames.

com.cleetus.desk-trigger had been answering this correctly the entire time from HID idle, and nothing was reading it. Keyboard and mouse activity is a direct measurement of a person using a desk, so it outranks a camera that cannot see the chair. Footer rows relabelled WIFI FUSE / WIFI OCC, because two answers about one room without a source on each label reads as the panel contradicting itself.

The fusion guard, and the size of what it bought

WDP_GUARD_INTERVAL_US 300 → 500 ms. The panel went FAILING → LOCKED and the doctor check went from pinned at the 6/min rate-limit ceiling to 4/min.

The log cannot give you the distribution — it records only spreads that EXCEED the guard, and is rate-limited to one line per 10 s. That is why it read "5/min" when the true rate was 297/min. Sampling the live fleet instead (the gap between freshest and stalest node IS what the fuser sees, since it timestamps on arrival) gave p50 68 · p90 222 · p95 302 · p99 488 · max 683 ms. 300 cleared 95.0% of healthy cycles; 500 clears 99.1%.

And a near-miss worth remembering: one 60 s window at each setting showed a 21× improvement. Eight 30 s windows at each — an actual measurement — showed median 111 → 103 and worst-case 344 → 124. The between-window variance is larger than the effect. Do not raise it further: after the change the exceedances re-clustered just above the NEW guard (median 514 against 500), exactly as they had at 300. The distribution is heavy-tailed and there is always a population just over whatever line is drawn; the 9.9 s tail is a node being DOWN, and a guard wide enough to admit that is fusing captures seconds apart.

Nutrition, and an icon

Targets are live: 3,400 kcal · 185 g protein · 462 g carbs · 90 g fat, goal bulk. Two profile errors fixed on the way: height read 6'3" (now 6'2") and the goal was recomp.

Targets could not compute at all before this because they are derived from a 7-day bodyweight average and there were zero bodyweight entries — hence missing: ["bodyweight"]. 185 lb is logged as a placeholder and should be replaced by real morning weigh-ins; the trend is what calibrates the number, not the arithmetic.

The server's own suggestion was 3,160 kcal on a hardcoded 1.5 activity multiplier. Six hour-long lifts a week plus sport and campus walking is nearer 1.7 (TDEE ~3,250). The set value sits deliberately between the two estimates, because the multiplier is the least reliable term and starting low costs two weeks while starting high costs fat.

The real constraint is not calories, it is meal structure: a yogurt plus one big meal cannot hold 185 g of protein. One shake between classes closes most of the gap.

Cleetus Fit has an icon now — a machined bumper plate, drawn in cleetus-fit/icon.py. The first render put a hard straight chord across the plate where a pieslice shaded the lower rim; at icon size that reads as a rendering bug, not shading.

Another session moved this checkout out from under the work

Deploying found ~/cleetusv2 switched from the feature branch to main, and all nine Cleetus Fit source files gone from disk — they exist only on the branch, so checking out main removed them. Nothing was lost because it was all committed, but building without looking would have shipped an app with no Fit source in it. Check git status and git branch --show-current before building in a shared checkout; see also the shared-worktree note earlier in this document.

Shipped

  • cleetusv2 → main, pushed (6d654da..61c0da8). iOS-only, so the website is functionally unchanged.
  • CLEETUS → pushed (833cc7e..6f71243).
  • Both apps installed on the iPhone — Cleetus Fit 1.0 and Cleetus 1.3.0 (8), the Fit build carrying the icon (verified in Assets.car, not from the build log).
  • 23 com.cleetus.* agents loaded; new since yesterday: roomwatch and ruview-collect.

Open

  1. The $25 2.4 GHz access point is now the ONLY remaining move on RuView, and there is a one-command test ready for it: re-provision the three boards' --filter-mac to the new BSSID (USB only) and re-run ruview-transitions.py.
  2. Log real morning bodyweights. The targets recalculate off a 7-day average and currently rest on one placeholder.
  3. Eleven web surfaces still have no native screen: reach, ruview, protocols, timeline, hub, studio, flights, sky, solar, space, brew. All have JSON endpoints already.
  4. The two fitness widgets still point at the main Cleetus app.
  5. com.cleetus.studio is loaded but not running — port 8765 is held by an instance started outside launchd. Two copies, one supervised.
  6. The Brio 101 still delivers zero frames, which is why the desk has no camera of its own.

Books: the ledger that could not answer what he owns

2026-08-22. Grayson is turning "The Magnolia Company" into a parent LLC with three trade names, and asked for QuickBooks recreated inside Cleetus: personal and business sides, the business side rolling into one ball, the whole tax structure set up ahead of the businesses that will use it, and the cheapest possible way to watch money move.

What was actually there

ledger_transactions: one row per bank transaction, a business_tag, and a free-text category. 342 rows, 330 of them untagged, last synced 4 August. That is a cash spreadsheet with extra steps. It can say what was spent. It cannot say what he owns, what he owes, who owes him, or whether its own numbers agree with each other, because a single row has no other side. No balance sheet is derivable from it at any level of effort.

What replaced it

Migrations 023 to 027. Real double-entry: book_entities (nine books), one shared coa_accounts chart (77 accounts, each carrying its Schedule C line), journal_entries and journal_lines, periods, audit log, receivables, payables, bank feed, reconciliation, budgets, and the tax engine. 31 tables.

The load-bearing object is a DEFERRABLE INITIALLY DEFERRED constraint trigger that refuses any entry whose debits do not equal its credits at COMMIT. Deferred because a plain AFTER trigger fires on the first line and would reject every multi-line entry ever written.

Entity is a dimension on the journal line, not a separate ledger. That is the whole answer to "roll up into one neat ball": adding Magnolia Booking later is an INSERT, not a migration, not a second QuickBooks subscription, and not a second reconciliation.

Posting lives in plpgsql, not in the Pages Function. A document that exists without its journal entry is the worst failure an accounting system has, and an HTTP handler dying between two fetch() calls produces exactly that. Every posting function is idempotent: it unposts before it reposts, so editing a sent invoice cannot double-book the revenue.

Two corrections to the phase-one plan, both material

  1. Separate Schedule Cs. The plan said all three Magnolia lines combine into one Schedule C. The Schedule C instructions require one per trade or business, and the IRS reads that as the ACTIVITY, not the name on the invoice. Web design, event booking and managed IT are three activities. One LLC, one EIN, one bank account, one ledger, and three Schedule Cs on one 1040. The roll-up happens on Schedule SE, which is where they legitimately become one number. This is why book_entities.separate_schedule_c is a stated flag rather than inferred from kind.
  2. Two entities were missing entirely. Good Life Music LLC is a partnership he part-owns: Form 1065, K-1, and box 14a joins the Schedule Cs on Schedule SE. Higher Ways is a Georgia nonprofit corporation with no IRS exemption yet, which means a 27-month clock on Form 1023 and a Georgia C-100 registration required BEFORE soliciting.

The sign convention, which is the dangerous one

ledger_transactions used Plaid's convention: positive means money OUT. bank_transactions uses the bank's and SimpleFIN's: positive means money IN. The importer flips all 342 rows and the migration test asserts the flip in both directions. An inverted sign here crashes nothing. It turns every expense into income and produces a beautiful, completely wrong P&L. Proof the flip was right: money-in and money-out totals computed under the two opposite conventions agree exactly at 17,833.39 and 27,310.43.

The Plaid charges were not random

Plaid bills Balance and Liabilities per request. Neither plaid/accounts.js nor plaid/credit.js cached anything, and eight callers fanned out to them: the dashboard Money tab, the iOS MoneyView, the iOS home-screen widget, /api/health, /api/advisor, /api/stocks, /api/snapshots, /api/morning-brief. The widget refreshes unattended on iOS's schedule, forever. Not one runaway job: eight callers each ringing a meter. Both endpoints now cache (60 min and 6 h) and only a COMPLETE answer is cached, so a partial total is never pinned. Replacement chosen: SimpleFIN Bridge, $15/year flat, read-only, 25 institutions, adapter built.

Two real bugs the two-pass migration test caught

  • Mileage was claimed on every Schedule C. The line 9 query had no entity filter, so three businesses that drove nowhere each showed a $253.75 deduction and a matching phantom loss. mileage_log now carries a real entity_id.
  • The tax calendar re-seeded on every run. UNIQUE(entity_id, tax_year, title) with a NULL entity_id never conflicts in Postgres, so household-wide rows duplicated: 15 became 22 on the second pass. Now a unique index over COALESCE(entity_id, sentinel).

Honesty machinery

  • tax_rule_sources stores every tax constant with its citation and a verified flag. Georgia's 2026 standard deduction is genuinely disputed between sources ($12,000 vs $15,000) and dor.georgia.gov's own page stops at 2024, so it ships unverified and amber rather than looking settled. About $150 of Georgia tax.
  • The bank inbox does not auto-file. Rules propose; a human accepts. Only a rule explicitly promoted to auto_post writes the ledger unattended, because a rule applied across 300 rows produces a categorised, confident, wrong P&L that nobody goes looking for.
  • The legacy business_tag carried across as a suggestion only. It says which book a row belongs to, never which line of Schedule C, and untagged rows got no guess.

Verification

pglite, all five migrations applied TWICE, then a full quote to invoice to partial payment to bill to vendor payment to year-end close cycle, asserting the accounting equation holds at every step. Each guard was proved to actually fire: unbalanced entry, single-sided entry, P&L on the unassigned book, posting into a closed period, overapplied cash, reconciling with a difference. Applied live; all 14 report RPCs and the whole /api/books surface exercised against the real database, including a negative control confirming a bad call 404s rather than returning empty.

Open

  1. 342 transactions are uncategorised. Nothing appears in any report until they are filed. The two big ones are traps: Schwab MoneyLink (~$8,270) and Capital One payments (~$5,310) are transfers. Filed as expenses they inflate income and spending by ~$13,000 each way.
  2. No SimpleFIN connection yet. Needs a setup token from beta-bridge.simplefin.org pasted into Books > Setup. It can only be claimed once.
  3. No bank account is mapped to a chart-of-accounts account, and an unmapped account cannot post: a feed row has no cash side without it.
  4. Confirm Georgia's 2026 standard deduction and the 2026 mileage rate; both ship unverified.
  5. Higher Ways: fill in incorporated_on and the EIN, or the 27-month exemption clock cannot be computed.
  6. Plaid is cached but not yet removed. It comes out once SimpleFIN is live and reconciled against a month of overlap.
  7. Not built, deliberately: payroll runs, e-filing, and merchant processing. Each needs a licensed provider, not code.

Ten rounds of trying to break the Books

2026-08-22. Grayson asked for Books to be fire-tested until it provably could not be corrupted, with each round deployed live. Ten rounds ran. 324 checks across eighteen suites, and about forty real defects found and fixed. Everything is in test/books/, runnable with npm run test:books.

The pattern behind most of the ledger bugs

A BEFORE INSERT trigger validates a row when it is written and never sees the SECOND write that invalidates it, nor a write to a DIFFERENT table that changes what the first one meant. That one sentence explains: moving an entry's date into a closed year, deleting rows out of a closed year, flipping a posted account from expense to income, shrinking a payment below what was already applied from it, and shrinking an invoice below what had been paid against it. All were fixed in migration 028.

The worst finds, in order of how quietly they went wrong

  1. Euros were being added to dollars. The currency columns existed since 024 and nothing read them. A 100 EUR expense reported as "100.00" next to dollars. Fixed as a WALL, not a feature: the ledger is USD and anything else is refused with a reason, because half-built multi-currency is worse than none.
  2. The 110% safe harbour was never implemented. Prior-year AGI over $150,000 raises the estimated-tax safe harbour from 100% to 110%. RULES carried that as a comment on a constant that never changed. On a $12,000 prior-year tax the engine said 12,000 where the rule requires 13,200, and that $1,200 is exactly what an underpayment penalty is charged on.
  3. The filing packet disagreed with the estimate. filing.js kept its own copy of the rate constants, missing the additional 0.9% Medicare tax. At $300,000 of gross it understated self-employment tax by $693.45 against the number the same app showed on the next tab. It now imports one source, and the suite asserts the two agree at four income levels.
  4. A closed year's profit and loss read as all zeros, because the closing reversals counted as activity. Last year became unreadable the day it was finalised.
  5. report_pnl_by_entity put its filters in a LEFT JOIN's ON clause, so they never applied at all. Every entity netted to precisely zero after a close.
  6. Sales tax could be collected and never remitted. 2200 only ever grew. That is the state's money.
  7. PostgREST parameter injection on three endpoints. ?status=x&limit=99999&select=* appended real parameters. Note that encodeURIComponent alone would NOT have fixed it: PostgREST parses the decoded value, so a bare ) still escapes a filter group.
  8. The payables system was half built. bill_payments existed since 024 and nothing could write it. A bill could be recorded and never paid.
  9. Account 6130 said "written by the depreciation run" and there was no depreciation run. Migration 031 built it.
  10. One bad row from the bank failed a whole 300-row batch, because a non-numeric amount became NaN, which JSON.stringify writes as null, which violates NOT NULL.

The discipline that actually found things

  • Prove the check can fail. Three assertions passed for the wrong reason: with fetch mocked every call "failed", so ok === false validated nothing. The XSS suite was proved by neutering esc(): 16 handlers executed with it broken, 0 with it intact.
  • Derive expected values independently. The tax suites write the arithmetic longhand in a comment above each assertion. That caught the 110% harbour and also caught two of my own slips, which is the point.
  • Assert on the query, not the response. The API suite replaces global fetch and inspects the URL the router built.
  • Apply migrations TWICE. Caught a UNIQUE over a nullable column that never conflicts, and a CREATE OR REPLACE that cannot widen a return type.
  • The dash gate was itself a false pass for one round: it used grep -P, which BSD grep lacks, so it exited 2, the if read that as "no matches", and it printed ok while checking nothing.

Open

  1. 342 transactions are still uncategorised. Nothing appears in any report until they are filed. Schwab MoneyLink (~$8,270) and Capital One payments (~$5,310) are TRANSFERS; filed as expenses they swing the numbers ~$13,000 each way.
  2. No SimpleFIN connection yet, and no bank account is mapped to a chart-of-accounts account. An unmapped account cannot post.
  3. book_attachments has no upload path. It needs object storage and a signed URL. Named rather than stubbed.
  4. The UI does not expose the newer routes: bill payments, fixed assets, depreciation, budgets, recurring, audit, sales tax. All are live on the API.
  5. Georgia's 2026 standard deduction and the 2026 mileage rate still ship UNVERIFIED.
  6. Plaid is cached but not removed. It comes out once SimpleFIN is live and reconciled against a month of overlap.

A tape measures distances, not coordinates

RuView runs on a room layout that was trilaterated on paper in August from three tape readings. That was correct once and wrong the moment a board moved, and nothing could notice: the server takes --node-positions on faith and reports confident coordinates from whatever it was handed.

cleetusai.com/geometry takes the measurements you can actually make with a tape and solves the layout by least squares. Click empty space to add a named point, shift-click a second one to record the distance between them, in 8'8" or 104in or 2.64m or 264cm. Press Solve.

The residual is the whole point

A drawing cannot tell you it is wrong. A fit can. Fed the six August readings the layout fits to 0.8 cm with the worst measurement 1.3 cm out, and each row shows its own error, so a mistyped tape reading is visible rather than absorbed.

Three things it cannot know, all shown rather than hidden. A point with fewer than two distances cannot be placed. Two anchors are needed to pin a frame, so a single hand-placed point pins nothing and is reported as a plan rather than a fact. And a distance graph fits its own mirror equally well, hence the Flip button.

The finding that matters: six measurements are not enough

Six distances place four points, which leaves exactly one spare measurement. Planting a 30 cm error in one of the August readings gives a residual of 0.47 cm: the solver just moves a point until the mistake fits, the fit looks excellent, and the room is wrong. Add three more distances and the same error surfaces at 6.35 cm with the guilty pair ranked first.

So the page reports the spare count and refuses to call a barely-determined layout trustworthy. A good residual over too few measurements is not evidence. When the room gets remeasured, measure more pairs than the minimum.

What it is actually for

The sensing question is not where things are, it is which radio paths pass through a seated body. So the seat carries a radius and a height range, and for every transmitter-to-node pair the page reports the span, how far the path passes from you, and the height at which it crosses. Knees and chest are not the same measurement, because breathing lives in the chest.

Seeded with the measured studio and the planned pocket router. It reproduces the by-hand answer: 2.24 m from the mango to the desk node with the seat 13 cm off the line, plus two clean 1.32 m controls to the turtle and guitar-stand nodes.

Reading it back

~/cleetusd/bin/ruview-geometry.mjs prints the layout, the path analysis and the --node-positions flag from a terminal, and compares that flag against the one the LaunchAgent is actually running, because a plist edited and not reloaded is the failure this project has already paid for twice. --write regenerates room-geometry.json; --plist rewrites the flag and prints the unload and load pair, since kickstart -k will not re-read an edited plist.

The plist currently disagrees, and that is fine. The solved frame came out rotated from the August one. Every pairwise distance is the same to within 1.3 cm, so the room has not moved and the running server needs no change. Fusion works on distances; the frame is arbitrary.

Left open

  1. The mango does not exist yet. Its position is the plan (halfway between the turtle and guitar-stand nodes), carried rigidly with the room so it keeps meaning across re-solves, but nothing can check it. Tape it to all three nodes once it is on the shelf.
  2. Heights are all assumed 0.75 m. The path analysis reports crossing height, so if a board sits materially higher or lower it is worth entering.
  3. Only the horizontal layout is solved. The z values are typed, not fitted.

A filter is not an association

The WiFi sensing has never detected anybody. The reason recorded for months was that the nodes were unfiltered — pooling every transmitter in range, so motion_energy measured transmitter diversity rather than a body. A GL.iNet pocket router, the "mango", was bought to be a dedicated in-room transmitter, and the fix was written down as one USB trip per board to set --filter-mac.

Every part of that was already done, and none of it worked.

All three nodes had been filtered since 20 August — to 38:17:b1:34:e7:5e, the house router. The record was sitting in ~/.config/wifi-densepose/esp32-provision-state/*.json the whole time. A session earlier that day had concluded "none is filtered" from the fact that their CSI frame rate wanders, which is wrong: filtering to an access point that carries real client traffic still gives a wandering rate. That test only distinguishes a beacon-only AP from everything else.

Flashing node 2 onto the mango produced yield=0pps — zero CSI frames — while the node stayed associated, kept its RSSI, kept answering ESP-NOW, and simply vanished from /api/v1/nodes. That reads as a dead board, not a misconfiguration.

The first cause was a channel. On 27 August the mango's own site survey put the house AP on channel 1, so the mango was pinned to 1. It is on 6 now. A station locks to the channel of the AP it associates with, so node 2 sat on 6 and could not physically hear a transmitter on 1. room-geometry.json had predicted exactly this when it refused to pin --channel: a pin goes silently dead if the router ever moves.

Matching the channel was necessary and not sufficient. Still zero. The answer was in the firmware, in a comment beside a self-ping added under issue #954:

the router's ICMP echo replies are OFDM frames destined to this station, which drive the CSI engine regardless of promiscuous filter state or ambient traffic

ESP32 CSI is driven by frames addressed to the station. A third party's broadcast beacons do not reliably drive it. So "point a node at an in-room beacon-only AP" cannot work by filtering at all — the node has to be a client of that AP, which makes its frames unicast and aims the 50 Hz self-ping at it as the gateway. Re-provisioned with --ssid ruview-tx, node 2 came up in one boot: CSI cb #1: len=256 rssi=-45 ch=6, yield climbing, and a link steadier than the two still on the house router — RSSI standard deviation 0.47 dB against 1.02 and 1.79.

The mango is now in repeater mode, so its own AP follows the house router's channel automatically. That matters more than it sounds: the router has already moved once, and a fixed channel would have re-broken this silently. It also regained a LAN address, so for the first time it can be liveness-checked — a dead transmitter and an empty room produce the same reading, and until now nothing could tell them apart.

Two nodes remain on the house router, deliberately: node 2 is the experiment, they are the control. Its presence threshold is poisoned — it booted and learned from 1200 frames with somebody at the desk — and its old empty-room baseline is void because it is measuring a different transmitter entirely. Nothing scored from it means anything until it boots into an empty room.

The room can be switched from the wall

The Pi panel showed the room. It now runs it: tap a row to switch it, tap EDIT on a lamp for brightness, a warm-to-cool white strip, and colour.

The naming problem had to be solved first. Meross calls both surge protectors "Smart Surge Protector" and every outlet "Switch 1..4"; the Hue bridge calls three of four lamps "Hue Essential lamp N". Neither is addressable by a human standing in front of it. Apple Home already holds the real names — Desk, Nightstand, Helix, Turtle Lamp, Right Main Monitor — so the panel reads them from Home's own database, live, on every call.

Two joins were needed and neither is obvious. For the outlets, HomeKit's provided name ends in the last four hex of the device's MAC (MSS425F-1d8e), which is also the tail of the Meross uuid; serial numbers do not match between the two systems. For the lamps, HomeKit stores the Hue device id as its serial number while the API you write to returns the light-service id — different UUIDs for the same bulb, so a naive match finds nothing. /clip/v2/resource/device carries both.

Reading Home live was a correction, not a design choice. It was first built as a cached JSON file rebuilt by hand after any rename — which is the classic stale-map bug, where everything keeps working while addressing the wrong outlet. Deriving it costs about ten milliseconds against a Meross write measured in seconds. There was never a reason to cache it.

Two writes are refused server-side.
