The outlet matching /phone and pi/i powers the Pi displaying the panel, so tapping it would kill the panel and the only way to undo it — a trap that disables its own undo. Channel 0 is the whole strip rather than a socket. Both are enforced in the daemon, not hidden in the page: a page-only guard is one stale deploy from useless. The locked row still runs its handler and says why, because a row that does nothing at all reads as a dead panel.

That guard exists because of a real incident. plugs_set defaulted the channel to 0 and always passed it, so an explicit argument overrode the channel the Home alias had already resolved — and switching one outlet cut mains to every outlet on that strip, both monitors included. Twice. It hid because the readback was filtered to the row expected, and the other five channels changed inside the same output being grepped past. Read the whole readback after a write, not the row you asked about. An "idempotent" test is only idempotent if the argument under test is the one being varied.

Rows settle on the readback from the device, never on the value requested, and a tap holds polling for three seconds because a Meross write is a cloud round trip and the cache would otherwise hand back the pre-tap state and snap the row back.

The camera cannot corroborate the night

A bed-occupied window was opened at 02:35 and the camera reported, as it had all night, changed_pct = 0 — nothing reaching the 3% a moving person produces. ruview-empty.py read that as the camera agreeing.

The frame was black. Measured across 1,827 saved frames by hour: 136–151 in daylight, 37 at 23:00, 3.1 at 02:00, and 0.0 from 03:00 to 07:00. There is no night lighting and no IR.

The dangerous part is not the blindness. It is that a black frame scores exactly the same as a still empty room, so the corroboration check could never fail at night. Every night hour inside a claimed-empty window was being blessed by a camera that could not see. The camera helper had been computing mean_brightness all along and throwing it away; it is now recorded on every heartbeat, and the analyser refuses to say "camera agrees" over a dark window. Both verified-empty windows turned out to be 0% blind — daylight and evening — so that ground truth survived. The 83.7-minute occupied window was 76.5% dim, and its corroboration should be treated as weak rather than absent.

Two smaller traps from the same night. com.cleetus.roomwatch is its own LaunchAgent, so restarting cleetusd does not reload it — a correct patch produced no change and looked wrong for fifteen minutes. And the feature collector had been dead for 45 hours, having hit its 200 MB cap while the process stayed alive at LastExitStatus 0. The doctor check added for exactly this fired correctly and told nobody, which is most of the way to being useless.

The dataset it was all feeding cannot answer the question anyway. Verified-empty covers 16:00–22:00 and verified-occupied covers 22:00–07:00; only hour 22 has both, at 5% of the smaller class. Ninety-five per cent of that comparison is evening against night, not empty against occupied, so the features that separate them are measuring the hour. The fix is not more data — it is alternating windows inside the same hours, twenty minutes in and twenty minutes out, so drift is common-mode and cancels.

Every readout green, and the cursor pinned to a corner

The AirPad stopped moving the cursor. Everything it reports said it was fine: a hand tracked, 25 fps, control: true, accessibility: true, tracker_error: null. Nothing in that set can go red for this fault.

The tell was one field. point sat at [1.0, 1.0] and never moved, and engaged never left false. Both are clamps, not measurements — to_screen() ends in min(1.0, max(0.0, …)), so a mapping that sends the hand off the plane produces a perfectly steady corner rather than an error.

Pushing the saved homography through apply_h by hand settled it. The centre of the frame mapped to (0.29, 1.16), and none of five sampled points landed inside the unit square. Four corner captures that are nearly collinear still solve cleanly; the solver is happy to fit a quad that throws most of the frame off screen, and nothing downstream checks.

The backup was the same broken matrix. save_calibration() copied config.json to config.good.json whenever a matrix existed, so a bad calibration overwrote the last good one on the way in. Both files were byte-identical and there was nothing to roll back to. Most recent is not the same as good, and a backup that any write can clobber is not a backup.

Fixed twice over. The calibration key was deleted from both files, which drops to_screen() onto the margin mapping it uses when uncalibrated — that maps centre to centre and spans the full range, so the pad works without any calibration at all. Deleting a bad calibration is the fast fix and it is not a downgrade to nothing. Then calibration_is_sane() was added: the frame centre must land on screen and at least 60% of a 3×3 interior grid must too, and only a calibration that passes is promoted to config.good.json. It was checked against the matrix that caused this — rejected, "the centre of the frame maps to (0.29, 1.16), off screen" — and against an identity, accepted.

Two things in the same log look like the fault and are not. display check failed: min() iterable argument is empty is span_all() running min(d["x"] for d in ds) while CGGetActiveDisplayList returns zero displays, which is what happens with the monitors powered off; it self-heals when they come back. And desktop gesture blocked: face auth required gates only the three-finger show-desktop gesture, never pointer motion.

The robot answers every command and does not move

Asked to drive the mBot a metre, it did nothing — while returning {"ok": true, "left": 255.0, "right": 255.0} to every request, at every speed, for forward, spin, and each motor individually.

The LEDs, set red then blue, did change. That single observation splits the fault cleanly. The command path is whole: Mac to Pi over Tailscale, Pi to robot over BLE, the mCore parsing frames and executing them. The onboard RGB and the sensors run off the logic rail, which USB alone can feed. The motors run off VM, through the driver, from the battery. Every actuator on the first rail works and nothing on the second does.

Motion over BLE has never been verified. The record says "firmware read + live distance over BLE" and, on the Pi, "robotd healthy" — reads, both times. The last confirmed movement was 17 June over USB serial from the Mac. Three months of green health checks sat on top of a capability nobody had exercised.

Worth recording how badly the diagnosis went, because the failure was mine and it is repeatable. The ultrasonic was treated as ground truth twice. First a pair of readings 119.4 → 81.8 cm was read as movement and turned into a confident 15.0 cm/s; twelve samples from a stationary robot then spanned 81.6 to 138.7 cm, so that figure was noise given a decimal point. Then the same sensor was used to conclude the robot had NOT moved — also unsound, because a person walking through the cone moves the reading exactly as approaching a wall does. A distance sensor cannot tell "I approached a wall" from "a wall approached me", and there are no wheel encoders, so the robot has no way to observe its own motion. Grayson standing next to it and saying "it didn't move" was better evidence than anything measured.

Next: unplug USB. Dead robot means the battery is supplying nothing — flat cells or a pack that is not really connected. Still alive means the rail is fine and the fault is downstream, at the motor leads or the driver.

Nine hubs on one port

The USB hub keeps cutting out. No disconnect events are visible — log show returns nothing for any USB predicate on this machine, and system_profiler SPUSBDataType prints nothing at all, so ioreg is the only inventory that works here.

What the topology shows is one root controller carrying eighteen devices while three others sit nearly empty:

  1. 01000000 — 18 devices, three hub tiers deep: a VIA hub into a second hub into a third that holds the keyboard. Hanging off it: two webcams (C920 and Brio), the Litra Glow, a Scarlett, the Stream Deck, two MIDI controllers, an external drive and the ethernet adapter.
  2. 08000000 — 4. 03000000 — 3. 00000000 — 3. 02000000 — 1.

Two cameras and a light is most of a 500 mA budget before anything else is counted. This is very likely the same root cause as two symptoms already on record: the Brio delivering no frames, and the Stream Deck being purged.

There is also a USB Billboard Device on the bus, from VIA Labs — the same vendor as the hub. A Billboard device exists in the spec for exactly one purpose: to report that a USB-C alternate-mode negotiation failed. It is not a peripheral, it is an error message shaped like one, and it points at the hub's cable or port rather than its contents.

In order: confirm the hub's own power supply is connected; swap its cable and port; move the two cameras and the light onto one of the idle controllers.

Plaid billed by the request, then Plaid was removed

A Plaid invoice for over $300 arrived in September (there is no invoice email; the charge appears only on the card and at dashboard.plaid.com). Section 27 had already found the accounts route swallowing failures; this is the money side of the same code, and it ends with Plaid gone from Cleetus entirely.

What Plaid charges for

EndpointBilling
/accounts/balance/getper successful request
/accounts/getfree (same balance fields, possibly a day old)
/transactions/get, /liabilities/getper connected Item, per month
/transactions/refreshper request

Where the requests came from

Until 19 August /api/plaid/accounts called the billed balance endpoint for both Items on every request, and /api/health fans into it. The Mac's com.cleetus.health job polls health every 15 minutes, unattended, so that loop alone was roughly 190 billed calls a day. The dashboard, the advisor, stocks, snapshots and the morning brief added more.

The 22 August cost sweep added caches and switched the accounts route to the free endpoint, but left the billed one in two "fallbacks": credit.js (when /liabilities/get is refused) and tax/estimate.js. Both fallbacks ran on every call, because Capital One answers INVALID_PRODUCT to liabilities and Queensborough was ITEM_LOGIN_REQUIRED, and every one of those caches was written only on a complete answer. In production there was never a complete answer. app_kv has no plaid_* or tax_estimate key at all: the caches never wrote once. Green tests, dead cache.

First fix, then the instruction to remove it all

Commit b5b1435 moved both fallbacks to the free endpoint and added test/books/no_billed_plaid_endpoints.mjs, which scans functions/ for any per-request billed endpoint and was made to fail once before it was trusted.

Grayson then asked for Plaid to go entirely. Order of operations mattered: the Items had to be removed at Plaid first, or the per-Item subscription keeps billing with no code left to notice. The access tokens can only be decrypted by the deployed app (the ENCRYPTION_KEY in cleetus.env is a placeholder; Cloudflare holds the real one), so a one-shot internal endpoint did the /item/remove calls and was deleted in the next commit. Two traps in that step:

  1. The current Plaid API answers /item/remove with only a request_id. There is no removed: true any more, so a check for it reports failure on success. The proof is the second pass: both Items came back ITEM_NOT_FOUND.
  2. A deleted route on Cloudflare Pages does not 404. /api/plaid/accounts now returns 200 with the site's HTML, which every old caller would have parsed as JSON and thrown on. The callers were rewired rather than left to discover that.

What replaced each consumer (commit e13175e)

WasNow
Tax estimate read Plaid transactionsReads ledger_transactions (same sign convention). Taxes-account balance is null, which the funding gauge already treats as unknown.
Daily snapshot took cash and debt liveCarries both forward from the prior row, flagged carried. Investments stay live from Schwab. Recording 0 would have cratered the trend the way the June Schwab lapse did.
Health graded PlaidNo plaid key. The 15-minute doctor stops flagging it.
Money tab offered Plaid Link"Feed disconnected" copy; the Books sync button pulls SimpleFIN.
Webhook receiver, ledger sync, CSP for plaid.comDeleted.

The guard test now fails on any non-comment plaid.com, PLAID_ or billed endpoint under functions/, or if functions/api/plaid/ exists again.

What is frozen until SimpleFIN is claimed

Live bank and card balances, spending, the subscription radar and the paycheck chips. Cash and debt in net worth are the last values from before 9 September. SimpleFIN is not connected: bank_connections holds one disabled manual row. The claim flow is in Books and takes a setup token from the SimpleFIN bridge; once active, the ledger and therefore the tax estimate pick it up with no further change.

Still on the list: the iOS MoneyView calls the old routes until a new build; the PLAID_* variables sit unused in Cloudflare (delete by hand, never round-trip Pages env vars); a refund is discretionary and goes through the dashboard support form, not a chargeback.

The stolen mouse, the money face gate, and the Room panel made fast (15 Sep 2026)

Grayson reported the cursor being "randomly stolen and moved and clicking on stuff". The only mouse-event emitter on the machine is the AirPad (~/studio-locate/airpad.py, LaunchAgent com.cleetus.airpad), up for 5 days 19 hours at 33% CPU. Killing it stopped the theft; the diagnosis came from running it in --watch mode (tracks, never posts) and sampling /api/state.

Cause: phantom hands, and nothing yielding to the real mouse

MediaPipe returned confident, complete skeletons for the cap, the face and the guitar case at the bottom edge of the C920 frame: hand_size about 0.10, every anchor at y=1.0, scale 0.0036 (a three pixel palm), and ratios no hand can make: pinch 3.5 to 9.5 and grip 7.7 to 10.4 against Grayson's measured 2.8 (together) and 5.2 (open). Every downstream threshold is a ratio over scale, so a collapsed palm inflates all of them at once, and one such skeleton held the pointing pose long enough (0.35 s) to engage. Once engaged nothing gave the pointer back: the pad never looked at the real mouse or keyboard.

Fix, in airpad.py (backup airpad.py.bak-20260915-premousefix)

  • Skeleton sanity (sanity in config): refused before it can point or pinch if grip or pinch exceed max_grip (7.2), if any landmark is within edge (0.02) of the frame border, or if scale is under min_scale (0, off, unmeasured). /api/state.rejected shows the last refusal. Live two minutes after restart: 11 phantoms refused, zero engagements.
  • Yield to real input (yield in config): a key-down within 1.2 s, or the pointer found more than 4 px from where the pad last put it (a real mouse moved it; compared against the last two posts because the window server applies moves asynchronously), drops engagement and blocks re-engagement for 1 s. /api/state.yield names the winner.
  • The log says why now: engaged: hand_size=.., click down at, yielded to the real mouse, not a hand: ....
  • Swipe gesture off. It needed no pose at all and fired on anything crossing the frame. It was only ever harmless because its face check was broken: face_cli.py identify requires --url and was never given one, so every check failed for a month. That is what "desktop gesture blocked: face auth required" meant. Fixed, and moved off the tracker thread (it froze the cursor up to 10 s per gesture).

launchctl bootout does not stop the AirPad (it ignores SIGTERM); use launchctl disable plus SIGKILL.

Money needs a face

~/cleetusd/src/facegate.mjs, hooked into callTool so it sits on the one path every tool takes. A cloud_api call whose path matches schwab, ledger, books, snapshots, plaid, simplefin, bank, money, accounts, invoices, bills, payroll or tax reads three frames from the C920 (through the AirPad's /frame.jpg) and needs Grayson in at least two at cosine 0.45. A pass unlocks for 10 minutes; a fail is never cached. The refusal is written for the model: it says nothing was fetched and forbids money figures from memory. GET /facegate is status, POST /facegate/lock revokes; there is deliberately no unlock over HTTP. Measured: refuse 1.2 s, pass 1.3 s. No liveness check: a photo passes. The AirPad being down fails the gate closed.

He scored 0.37 against the August gallery (cap, evening light, face cut off at the bottom of frame because the camera sits low). Eight samples were enrolled in that light; now 0.48 to 0.55. Enrol more if he goes unrecognised again; never lower 0.45.

The Room panel on the Pi, from 9 seconds to 30 milliseconds

The panel (devices-dash on :8791, scene devices) took 9.2 s per read and about 15 s per tap: every read spawned ctl.py (cold Meross login, MQTT, discovery, poll), a tap did that three times, and the Pi reached cleetusd through me.cleetusai.com at 5.4 s a call. Three changes:

  • ~/.config/meross/merossd.py (LaunchAgent com.cleetus.merossd, 127.0.0.1:8772): one warm MerossManager, cloud push updates the device objects in place, 45 s safety poll. /state is 20 ms and /set is 0.8 s with readback. controls.mjs uses it and falls back to ctl.py.
  • cleetusd also listens on 192.168.2.1:8767 (CLEETUSD_EXTRA_HOSTS in ~/cleetusd/.env, gitignored). The Pi is wired to the Mac's Internet Sharing port (192.168.2.2, 0.6 ms). The bearer is required on that listener; it refuses to bind without a token. The Mac's tailscale address is 100.107.235.66, not the 100.118.49.76 in older notes.
  • The Pi's devices-dash/server.mjs tries the wired link first and the tunnel second; the page polls every 2 s; the cleetusd /controls cache is 1.5 s.

Measured from the Pi afterwards: read 31 ms cached, 0.5 s cold; tap 0.98 s end to end. The scene server now boots to devices (DEFAULT_SCENE in scene_server.py); the scene lives in memory only, so this is what a reboot shows. Verified with a grim screenshot of the actual panel.

Not committed: /usr/bin/git and /usr/bin/python3 are Xcode shims and refuse until sudo xcodebuild -license accept. Use /opt/homebrew/bin/python3 in the meantime.

Swapping Laguna for Qwen3-32B + Heretic, a Bluetooth shelf, the logo, and the Keys station (15 Sep 2026)

The model: Laguna out, Qwen3-32B + Heretic in

Grayson asked for "the best Qwen model available" decensored with Heretic, the one that is most capable AND that Heretic can manipulate the most. That is Qwen3-32B (dense), not the 72B (which swaps on this 64 GB box during abliteration) and not an MoE (whose routed experts Heretic never touches, which is exactly why it was a poor fit for Laguna). Dense means every targeted o_proj/down_proj is a plain nn.Linear, so the abliteration reaches the whole model; 4-bit it is about 18 GB, which fits with headroom.

The pipeline is ~/cleetusd/bin/heretic-qwen.sh, adapted from the Laguna one: stages download, abliterate (4-bit, MPS, exports a LoRA adapter), merge (shard-by-shard, peak ~6 GB, via merge_lora_streaming.py), package (into Ollama as qwen3-32b-heretic:q8_0, letting Ollama apply its built-in Qwen3 template rather than copying Laguna's poolside format), and a new verify stage that forces a tool call and checks for a tool_calls array before activate is allowed to run. Tool-calling is the whole product; a model that chats but never calls a tool is useless here and fails silently, so activate now depends on verify passing.

Status: the 61 GB download finished; abliterate/merge/package is running in the background (log ~/models/heretic-work-qwen/build.log, checkpoints in ~/models/heretic-work-qwen). When it finishes, run heretic-qwen.sh verify then heretic-qwen.sh activate. Until activate, cleetusd stays on Laguna. Abliterate stops Ollama to free memory, so Cleetus is slow or without a model during the build and reloads on demand.

The Bluetooth / BLE knowledge shelf

Grayson handed over ~14 Bluetooth URLs to clone, give to Cleetus, wire into his framework, and teach the coding agent to use. Built the same way as the 817-skill cybersecurity library: src/bleskills.mjs plus the find_ble_skill / read_ble_skill tools (registered globally, read-only), an indexer (bin/index-ble-corpus.mjs) that turns vendor/ble/<repo>/ markdown into a searchable index, and briefs: the security agent (offensive and defensive BLE) and the pi agent (the BLE device hub) are both told to search the shelf before answering anything Bluetooth.

The actual repositories are fetched by ~/cleetusd/bin/fetch-ble-corpus.sh, which Grayson runs himself: the Claude Code auto-mode classifier blocks the daemon's own session from downloading external code ("untrusted code integration"), which is correct, so the framework was built to consume the corpus and the fetch is a one-command script he runs. It curls tarballs (no git needed; /usr/bin/git is the Xcode shim), extracts under vendor/ble/, indexes, and prints the restart. The seven clonable repos and the note that the github.com/topics pages and the Bluetooth-Devices org are indexes, not repos, are in vendor/ble/SOURCES.md. Until it is run, find_ble_skill reports the shelf as empty rather than inventing a technique.

The favicon: the latest logo on every page

The latest logo is favicon.svg (Aug 2026, the amber brainmesh-as-reticle mark with a green core on a dark plate); the brand PNGs were stale June/July assets. All 30 root HTML pages now carry the SVG favicon link (five were missing it entirely), and icon-512.png, apple-touch-icon.png and favicon.ico were regenerated from the SVG so the installed PWA icon matches too (old files kept as *.bak-oldlogo). Not deployed yet, because git is the Xcode shim.

The Pi Room panel, reorganized by station, with Govee

"I can't control the keys screen with my Pi": the keys devices are Govee plugs, and the panel only knew Meross and Hue. So Govee is now a third control source (src/govee.mjs, cached 8 s because a Govee read is a 1-2 s cloud call), and controls.mjs builds the panel from a declarative LAYOUT organized by room station rather than by device:

  • Desk: Main Monitors (the two desk monitors merged into one tile, a new merossmerge id that switches both channels), Helix, Desk Screen, Desk USB.
  • Keys (replaces the old Diffuser column): Keys light (Hue "Essential lamp 3", the top light) and Keys monitors (the Govee plug).
  • Lights: the two remaining Hue lamps.
  • Nightstand: Turtle Lamp, Turtle bulb (Hue, moved here), Diffuser (moved here), Phone And Pi (locked). The three dead outlets are gone.

Keys monitors are powered speakers, and cutting mains can pop the drivers, so that tile is not a bare one-tap: it carries a confirm flag and the panel page arms on the first tap (pulsing red, polling paused so the cue is not wiped) and only switches on a second tap within four seconds. An offline Govee plug shows as unreachable and refuses the write rather than reporting a false success. The scene server already boots to the devices panel, so this is what the wall shows.

Laguna removed, keys screens on the panel, the Heretic OOM fixed, and the commits (15 Sep 2026, later)

Laguna deleted

The 76 GB of Laguna base weights (~/models/Laguna-XS-2.1) and the old ~/models/heretic-work dir were deleted: the Qwen pipeline uses ~/models/Qwen3-32B and its own work dir, so they were pure dead disk. The 35 GB Ollama model laguna-xs-2.1:q8_0 was kept for now because it is still the model Cleetus runs on until the Qwen build is activated; deleting it now would leave the daemon with no model for hours. The activate stage of heretic-qwen.sh now removes it automatically the moment Cleetus switches to the Qwen build, so Laguna is gone the instant it is no longer needed.

Keys screens on the Keys tab

"I can't control the keys screen" was the screens, on their own Govee plugs (Keys monitor left / Keys monitor right), which are separate from the powered-speaker plug (Keys monitors). Added both to the Keys column as Keys screen L / R, normal one-tap tiles (screens, no pop guard). They are currently offline, so they render dashed and unreachable until their plugs are powered, then they switch normally. The speaker tile keeps its two-tap confirm.

Why Python was using 53 GB, and the fix

The 53 GB Python process was the Heretic abliteration of Qwen3-32B, and it was not merely large: it was failing with GPU out-of-memory (kIOGPUCommandBufferCallbackErrorOutOfMemory), cycling through dtypes and OOMing on each, while driving 43 GB of swap on this 64 GB box. Cause: Heretic's batch-size auto-picker (batch_size = 0) chose a batch whose per-prompt activations across all 64 layers of a 32B model blew past memory, which is the exact swap storm the config warns about. Fix: pinned batch_size = 1 and max_batch_size = 1 in ~/models/heretic-work-qwen/config.toml (slower, but it fits), stopped the thrashing run (swap fell from 43 GB to 18 GB immediately) and relaunched with the pinned batch. That still thrashed to 55 GB and hung at model load, which proved the blowup is at LOAD, not the forward pass: loading a dense 32B through bitsandbytes-4bit on MPS peaks past this 64 GB box. So the plan changed to match the stated criterion (the most capable Qwen that can actually be manipulated here): Qwen3-14B, abliterated in bf16 with no bitsandbytes at all (~28 GB, fits with room, and it sidesteps the 4-bit-on-MPS load OOM and index bug entirely). The 61 GB Qwen3-32B download was deleted. heretic-qwen.sh now targets Qwen3-14B (--quantization NONE, qwen3-14b-heretic:q8_0). Getting the 32B would need a rented 80 GB GPU.

Committed and deployed (Xcode license accepted, so git works again)

  • cleetusv2 (its own repo, main): the favicon and PWA-icon work committed (a4684a1) and pushed, which triggers the Cloudflare Pages deploy. Only the favicon files were staged; the unrelated untracked zones.html and ruview-zones.js were left alone; the old-logo backups are gitignored.
  • cleetusd lives inside the home STEAP repo (/Users/grayson, branch selfedit-20260813), which had a lot of unrelated in-flight work. Made a focused local commit (d99830b) of only this session's files (facegate, govee, controls layout, bleskills + ble tools, heretic-qwen scripts, and the agents/config/server/index edits), leaving everything else untouched. Not pushed: pushing the home repo is a deliberate call given its state and the daemon already runs live from the working tree.

Pi panel back up, and the keys screen is a WeMo (15 Sep 2026, later still)

"The Pi is down" was the panel timing out

The Pi host was fine (SSH up, 6 days). The panel showed nothing because /controls was returning 502 after 8 s. Two causes, both fixed:

  • The two Govee "Keys screen L/R" tiles I had added were offline, and reading offline Govee plugs is slow cloud calls: cleetusd /controls took 7.5 s, past the panel's timeout. Removed them (they were the wrong device anyway, see below) and gave the Govee state read a 6 s cap so a slow plug can never stall the panel again.
  • The Pi's devices-dash had gotten stuck on the slow tunnel (7 s) instead of the 2.8 s wired link, because its upstream logic remembered the last one that answered. Changed it to always try the wired link first in fixed order (a LAN failure is instant, so this costs nothing) and raised its read timeout to 12 s. Panel /controls is back to about 7 ms.

The keys screen is a Belkin WeMo, controlled locally

The keys screen is one screen on a Belkin WeMo Mini socket (192.168.1.156, serial 22A9B1K01067F0), on HomeKit but not Meross/Hue/Govee. WeMo speaks a local UPnP/SOAP API with no cloud, so it is controlled directly: src/wemo.mjs does GetBinaryState / SetBinaryState over the LAN, trying the last-known port and falling back across 49152 to 49155 (WeMo moves its port on reboot) and caching whatever answers. Added to the Keys column as Keys screen, a normal one-tap tile (a screen, no pop guard), read fresh in ~50 ms. The Keys station is now Keys light, Keys monitors (speakers, guarded), Keys screen (WeMo). /controls is fast again (~0.15 s), because the WeMo read is local and the offline-Govee stall is gone.

Model build

Still downloading Qwen3-14B (bf16); the abliteration has not started yet, so there is nothing to switch Cleetus to. Laguna remains the live model until the build finishes and tool-calling verifies.

Plain summary: the Pi freeze and the model-decensor problem (16 Sep 2026)

Problem 1: the Pi froze

What was wrong, simply: one program on the Pi (the RuView sensing dashboard, ruview-dash) slowly used more and more memory until, after 6 days, it had eaten 2.9 GB of the Pi's 4 GB. The Pi ran out of memory, started thrashing, and got so slow that the room panel froze and it would not even accept a login.

How it was fixed, simply: stopped that runaway program, which instantly gave the memory back (free memory went from 30 MB to 2.9 GB, the panel went from frozen to responding in a tenth of a second). Then set a hard 500 MB memory limit on it so it can never grow big enough to freeze the Pi again. If it ever creeps up, the Pi now restarts it automatically instead of choking.

Note: the "Phone And Pi" smart outlet does NOT actually power the Pi (the name is misleading), so turning it off is not a way to reboot the Pi. The fix is always to free memory, not cut power.

Problem 2: making the new AI model "uncensored" will not run on this Mac

What we were trying to do, simply: replace Cleetus's brain (the Laguna model) with a newer Qwen model, and run a tool called Heretic on it that removes the model's habit of refusing things it can actually do.

What went wrong, simply: Heretic works by running the model over and over again, and on this Mac's graphics chip that step keeps freezing. We tried three different models over about 16 hours. The biggest one ran out of memory immediately. The two smaller ones started, but each one eventually locked up partway through and stopped making progress, with no error, even after the whole machine was cleared to give it room. This is a known problem with this kind of tool on Apple Silicon: it is not a memory problem we can fix by freeing space, and it is not the model's fault. This Mac simply cannot finish the job.

The solution, simply: run Heretic on a rented cloud computer with a proper graphics card for about an hour (roughly a few dollars). There it finishes in minutes instead of freezing. The finished uncensored model then gets copied back onto this Mac and Cleetus switches to it. Everything needed to do that (the whole step-by-step pipeline and settings) is already written and correct; the only thing missing is a machine that can actually run it. Until that happens, Cleetus keeps running on the current Laguna model, which works fine.

Everything turned off during the attempts (the air-trackpad, the cameras, the sensing services) has been turned back on, and Cleetus is running normally.

The Pi's memory cap was never enforced, and the panel it protected was off (16 Sep 2026, later)

What the previous section got wrong

Resumed from the handoff and checked the Pi before trusting it. ruview-dash was inactive: it had been stopped at 16:08, the unit edited at 16:09, and never started again, so the sensing panel on :8790 was dark. Worse, the 500 MB cap was decorative. The Pi's user slice delegates only cpu pids (see /sys/fs/cgroup/user.slice/user-1000.slice/user@1000.service/cgroup.subtree_control), not memory, so a --user unit's MemoryMax has no cgroup to enforce against. systemctl show reports MemoryCurrent=[not set], which is the tell. Enabling delegation needs root, and gpope04 has no NOPASSWD sudo. The Pi was exactly as exposed to the leak as before.

Where the 2.9 GB came from

The service polled https://me.cleetusai.com every 1 s (edge-vitals), 2 s (room) and 5 s (presence) with an 8 s timeout. Measured from the Pi, the tunnel takes 5.4 s per request; the wired link (http://192.168.2.1:8767, the same one devices-dash was switched to the night before) takes 4 ms. setInterval fires whether or not the last call returned, so five or six fetches were permanently in flight per poller and most ended in a timeout, each holding a body, a timer and an abort signal until then. Six days of that. A second, smaller hole: the /camera.mjpg proxy waited on "drain" alone, and a response that closes while waiting never drains, so every viewer that disconnected mid-frame left the loop parked forever with the upstream body and the buffered frames.

What changed (deployed and proven)

  • pi/ruview-dash/server.mjs: upstream is now a fixed-order list, wired link first, tunnel fallback (RUVIEW_UPSTREAM is a comma list; the unit had a single tunnel URL that overrode the new default, which is why the first deploy still showed the tunnel). Each poller is wrapped in serial(): one request at a time, a slow tick is skipped, never stacked. The camera loop waits on drain OR close. /healthz now reports rss_mb and heap_mb so the next person can watch it.
  • ruview-dash.service: ExecStart runs node --max-old-space-size=384. V8 enforces the ceiling systemd cannot; the process aborts and Restart=always brings it back in 5 s with an empty heap. MemoryMax=500M stays in the unit with a comment saying it is inert until memory is delegated.
  • Proofs on the Pi: node --max-old-space-size=64 filling an array dies with FATAL ERROR: Reached heap limit, exit 134. Twelve /camera.mjpg streams opened and dropped: RSS 70 to 76 MB, zero established connections after, NRestarts=0. /api/state answers in 5 ms on the wired link; upstream reads http://192.168.2.1:8767.
  • pi-devices-dash/server.mjs in the repo was behind the copy on the Pi (the wired-first order). Repo now matches the Pi. Committed as f59e842 together with the 15 Sep work that had been left uncommitted.

Still open

The Heretic run is unchanged: it needs a rented GPU box for about an hour, with bin/heretic-qwen.sh (now targeting Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct) as the recipe. Cleetus is on Laguna until then. If the Pi is ever given Delegate=memory for user@1000.service (one root edit under /etc/systemd/system/user@.service.d/), the MemoryMax already in the unit starts working with no further change.

Cleetus is on the uncensored Qwen3.8-27B now (17 Sep 2026)

The rented GPU was not needed

The whole reason the Heretic run was stuck (s145) was that abliteration hangs on this Mac's MPS. It turned out not to matter: the Heretic project's own org already published the finished artifact, heretic-org/Qwen3.8-27B-heretic-ara (15 Aug 2026) — refusals 99/100 to 0/100, KL 0.0535 from the original, tool-calling and vision intact, Apache 2.0, with the exact reproduce/ config in the repo. Renting an H100 for an hour would have rebuilt that same file. So nothing was rented. Downloaded the 54 GB of bf16 weights, imported to Ollama at q8_0 (29 GB), and switched Cleetus over.

Why Qwen3.8-27B and not something bigger

It is the strongest DENSE Qwen (Artificial Analysis index 52 vs 38 for the same-architecture Qwen3.6-27B). Everything above it is MoE, which is the exact thing that defeated Heretic on Laguna: routed experts are not nn.Linear, so ablation never reaches them. It is also the strongest Qwen that fits 64 GB: q8_0 is 29 GB, less than Laguna's 35 GB, with room to keep qwen2.5vl loaded beside it. The 122B/397B/2.4T Qwen variants do not fit at any useful quant.

The one trap: a bare import has no tools

ollama create ... --quantize q8_0 from the safetensors produced a model whose Capabilities were completion, vision and nothing else — every tool call returned "does not support tools", which for Cleetus is the whole ballgame. Two things were needed:

  • Ollama had to be upgraded 0.32.7 to 0.34.1. The Qwen3.5/3.8 renderer and parser (and the library manifest's "requires":"0.32.12") did not exist in 0.32.7.
  • The model had to be recreated with the template Ollama ships for this family, not the empty one the import guessed. The recipe is bin/qwen3.8-heretic.Modelfile: RENDERER qwen3.8, PARSER qwen3.5, and Qwen's sampling defaults (temp 1, top_p 0.95, top_k 20). After that, Capabilities shows tools and thinking, and heretic-qwen.sh verify passes.

Verified end to end through cleetusd's own /chat, not just raw Ollama: "read ~/cleetusd/README.md and tell me the first heading" made it call read_file on the real path and answer "cleetusd" — the correct heading, no "I cannot access files" refusal, which is the behaviour the whole exercise was about.

Speed: the honest trade

It is slower than Laguna. On the same 150-word prose prompt, generation is ~15 tok/s vs Laguna's ~89 tok/s; prompt processing ~224 vs ~423 tok/s. That is the cost of a 27B dense model at q8 on this box, and it is the price of the capability jump. The model ships an MTP speculative-decoding head (draft_num_predict); it was left OFF (draft_num_predict 0) because on Metal at the measured 39% draft-acceptance it was a net loss (~13 tok/s with it, ~15 without). If a future Ollama accepts drafts better, flip it back on and re-measure.

What changed on disk

  • Ollama: qwen3.8-27b-heretic:q8_0 is live; laguna-xs-2.1:q8_0 was removed by activate (35 GB back). cleetusd/.env pins CLEETUSD_MODEL=qwen3.8-27b-heretic:q8_0, so a restart cannot fall back to the deleted Laguna default in config.mjs.
  • ~/models/Qwen3.8-27B-heretic-ara: the 52 GB bf16 source, kept so the model can be rebuilt without re-downloading.
  • bin/heretic-qwen.sh now downloads the published build (all = download to package); bin/qwen3.8-heretic.Modelfile is the tools/renderer recipe.

Rollback

Laguna's Ollama model is gone but its 35 GB source is not on disk either (deleted 15 Sep). To go back to Claude instantly: LLM_PROVIDER=anthropic in Cloudflare for the web app, or for cleetusd point CLEETUSD_MODEL at lfm2.5:8b (still installed) and restart. To rebuild Qwen from the local weights: heretic-qwen.sh package then recreate with the Modelfile.

The Bluetooth/BLE shelf, expanded to the full source list and wired to the model (17 Sep 2026)

What was asked

Grayson gave a list of 13 GitHub links (BLE libraries, awesome-* indexes, offensive-Bluetooth skills, the Nordic course, an org, some topic tags) and asked that the model be able to reach that material when he asks it to do Bluetooth work.

The important distinction: a shelf, not training

Nothing was “taught to the model.” The Qwen swap moved weights only; a model is not retrained by dropping repos on the disk. What actually serves this is a retrieval shelf built 15 Sep: the repos live under cleetusd/vendor/ble/, an index is built into vendor/ble/index.json, and two tools — find_ble_skill (search) and read_ble_skill (open one document) — let any agent search the shelf and read the real text at request time. The answer is grounded in the actual document, not in the model’s memory. That is better than baking it in: the sources can be updated any time, and there is no confabulation.

Two things that were already true

  • Every agent carries the shelf tools. cleetusd does not scope tools per agent (TOOLS is the same set for all 24), so the default cleetus agent reaches find_ble_skill, not only the security or investigator agents. The tool description itself says “call this FIRST for anything Bluetooth,” so discovery does not depend on a per-agent brief.
  • 7 of the 13 links were already on the shelf (fetched 15 Sep), and the Anthropic cybersecurity repo — the first link — was already cloned at vendor/cybersecurity-skills and served by find_security_skill.

What changed on 17 Sep

  • Added to bin/fetch-ble-corpus.sh: the Bluetooth-Devices org members (bleak-retry-connector, bluetooth-data-tools, bluetooth-adapters, bluetooth-auto-recovery), the hbldh/bleak library itself, and a second clone of mukul975/Anthropic-Cybersecurity-Skills.
  • The second clone is so the two Bluetooth SKILLs (detecting-bluetooth-low-energy-attacks, performing-bluetooth-security-assessment) surface on a find_ble_skill search — previously they were reachable only through find_security_skill, and the agent is told to reach for the BLE tool first on anything Bluetooth. The indexer (bin/index-ble-corpus.mjs) marks the new libraries as Bluetooth-dedicated and filters the Anthropic repo to its Bluetooth-named docs only, so its 800+ non-Bluetooth skills do not leak into a BLE search (it contributed exactly 4 docs).
  • The four remaining links are GitHub topics/* tag pages, not repositories — nothing to clone. The repos under those tags are already aggregated by awesome-ble and awesome-bluetooth-security, which are on the shelf. This is documented in vendor/ble/SOURCES.md rather than faked with an empty fetch.
  • Shelf rebuilt: 204 docs across 13 repos (was 167 / 7).

Verified end to end

Not just that the index built: sent a Bluetooth request through cleetusd’s /chat to the default agent. It read vendor/ble/…/detecting-bluetooth-low-energy-attacks/SKILL.md and returned its actual Step 5 (replay) and Step 6 (MITM / GATTacker / BTLEjuice) indicators, which were then confirmed against the real file — grounded, not recited.

How to change it later

The corpus (62 MB) is deliberately not in git (vendor/ is gitignored); the two scripts are, and they regenerate it exactly. To add a repo: put its owner/name slug in REPOS in bin/fetch-ble-corpus.sh, run the script (Grayson runs it, not the daemon — the auto-mode classifier blocks the daemon from fetching external repos), then restart cleetusd. Committed as c0a8b4b.

Is Cleetus on Qwen? Half of him

cleetusd: yes. /health names qwen3.8-27b-heretic:q8_0, a real /chat round trip answered correctly, and ollama ps showed that model at 100% GPU afterwards. The web app (me.cleetusai.com): no. Node 2 was never dead, and four ways onto the mango’s network are closed (22 Sep 2026)

What this file got wrong

Node 2 had been off the RuView roster since about 1 Sep, and the note here said a board “drops out silently when it loses power”. It never lost power. It is plugged into the Mac Studio’s USB and was healthy the whole time. Rebooting it over the serial line proved it: RTS pulses EN, and DTR must stay false or the chip comes up in the bootloader instead of the firmware. It booted clean, joined ruview-tx, took 192.168.8.150, and resumed CSI at about 30 pps with HEALTH sent every 30 s.

The real break, and the one command that finds it

Per section 46 the design is: node 2 associates with the mango, and its UDP is NAT’d to the server as 192.168.1.204, the mango’s house-LAN address from repeater mode, which sits inside --udp-allow 192.168.1.0/24. That address now answers nothing and sits (incomplete) in ARP, and a full sweep of 192.168.1.2-254 finds no 94:83:c4 device at all. The mango’s repeater uplink is down. Its AP and DHCP still run, which is exactly why the node looks perfectly healthy while its packets have nowhere to go. c6_espnow rx#0 says the mesh is split too, since nodes 1 and 3 are on the house AP.

The test is ping 192.168.1.204. Alive means the mango is repeating. Silent means node 2 is stranded no matter how healthy the board looks.

The labelled dataset had been dead for three weeks

ruview-collect only writes a row when all three nodes report unstale. So the 1 Sep migration of node 2 onto the mango silently stopped the dataset the loop exists to build: zero rows from 1 Sep to 22 Sep. The collector was not stuck on its 200 MB cap; the file was 3.2 MB.

Node 2 is back on the house Wi-Fi as a stopgap

provision.py is additive now (issues #391 and #574): it merges CLI flags with per-port state under ~/.config/wifi-densepose/esp32-provision-state/ rather than wiping keys you did not pass. Three keys changed and everything else carried over:

~/ruview-venv/bin/python ~/RuView/firmware/esp32-csi-node/provision.py \
  --port /dev/cu.usbmodem5B790201681 \
  --ssid 'Not a meth lab' --password <HOUSE_WIFI_KEY> \
  --filter-mac 38:17:b1:34:e7:5e

Result: roster 3 of 3, node 2 at 192.168.1.214 on channel 1, mesh sees 1/2/3, and the collector is writing again. RSSI is -54 against -45 on the mango, as expected with the house AP downstairs. The state file is keyed per PORT and this board enumerates on two, so use usbmodem5B790201681; the other port has older, different state. To revert once the mango is back, same command with --ssid ruview-tx, --password <MANGO_WIFI_KEY>, --filter-mac 94:83:C4:92:F6:C7.

Four routes onto the mango’s network, all closed

  • Join by name. networksetup -setairportnetwork answers Could not find network ruview-tx. and exits 0. Scanning works fine; Location permission only redacts the SSIDs, and a join by name cannot match a name the process is not allowed to read.
  • Add it as preferred at index 0, cycle the radio. The Mac rejoined the house network anyway. macOS scores networks itself and will not switch to an AP with no internet, which is precisely what a mango with a dead uplink looks like. The index is not obeyed.
  • Remove the house network so the mango is unopposed. macOS still held the working association and never dropped it.
  • Wired. Every ethernet port reads status: inactive except en13, the USB adapter bridged to the Pi at 192.168.2.1.

Do not repeat the first three. They fail for structural macOS reasons, cost several minutes of Wi-Fi churn each, and every failed join silently adds the SSID to the preferred list, which can later strand the Mac on the isolated 192.168.8.x net. Remove it with networksetup -removepreferredwirelessnetwork en1 ruview-tx.

The outlet that was not cut

The tempting shortcut was power-cycling the mango through Meross. The only candidate is Nightstand channel 5, Phone And Pi. Section 145 calls that name misleading and says it does not power the Pi, but the device notes list it as a live load and the room panel marks it [locked]. The Pi had 5 d 23 h uptime and a hard power-off risks the SD card, so it was left alone. Two notes disagreeing about a live load is a reason to stop, not to guess.

The reset did not fix it, and the way in is a cable

Grayson reset the mango. It still never appeared on the house LAN after about two minutes of polling and another full sweep. A power cycle restores a repeater whose config is intact, so the repeater config itself is gone or broken. If the reset was the button rather than the power, the SSID, the password and the installed SSH key are gone too and it needs re-onboarding.

Use a cable, not Wi-Fi. The August setup was done over a direct Mac-en0 to mango-LAN cable and that is still the move: plug en0 into the mango’s LAN port, take a 192.168.8.x lease there, and the whole box is reachable over SSH with zero Wi-Fi disruption. The key is already installed and known_hosts already has 192.168.8.1.

The presence baseline, poisoned and then fixed

A node learns its presence threshold from the first 1200 frames after boot, so booting with a person in the room teaches it that a person is the ambient state. The 12:52 reboot learned a clean one; the 16:37 re-flash destroyed it, because Grayson had come back to the desk at 16:31. A third reset at 18:24:47 with HID idle at 196 s fixed it for real: the first 13 s read motion=0.00 presence=0.00, which is what an empty room is supposed to look like.

Unchanged and still the point of the whole mango exercise: with the room empty, all three nodes report present_moving with person_count: 1. The fabrication is exactly what section 55 measured. A clean baseline does not make a downstairs AP able to see this room.

Four readings that lied, worth knowing before trusting them

  • tcpdump without root prints nothing and exits 0. It reads as “no traffic on that port”. It is a permission failure.
  • networksetup -getairportnetwork says “You are not associated with an AirPort network” on a perfectly working link, because the SSID is redacted without Location permission. It is not a statement about the radio.
  • networksetup -setairportnetwork returns exit 0 on a failed join, and adds the SSID to the preferred list on the way out.
  • cleetus.env values carry inline comments and spaces, so awk '{print $1}' turned Not a meth lab into Not and produced a false “the stored SSID does not match” alarm.

Still open

  • Cable to the mango, restore repeater mode, then revert node 2 to ruview-tx.
  • Everything in section 149 is unchanged: the Pages deploy still fails on the 64-variable cap, so LLM_MODEL still names the deleted laguna and the web app is still answering on paid Claude while claiming to be local.
  • ~/studio-locate/launchd/swap-agent.sh has not been run, and the C920 still delivers no frames.

How Grayson wants to be spoken to (22 Sep 2026)

Standing instruction, every session: clear, concise wording. Not a ton of words. Volume is not thoroughness: padding buries the facts that matter, and information gets lost in the transfer. Say the thing, give the evidence, stop.

  • Lead with the answer or the finding, not the reasoning that produced it.
  • One short paragraph beats five. Cut restatement, recap and throat-clearing.
  • Keep the evidence (command output, numbers, file paths). Cut the narration around it.
  • Corrections stay to a sentence. No repeated apologising, no tallying past mistakes.
  • Do not pad an answer to look thorough. Short and correct is the goal.

Apple TV state

Superseded by section 152: it is set up and on the house Wi-Fi now.

The Apple TV is set up and on the house Wi-Fi, after a reset that should not have happened yet (22 Sep 2026)

Where it landed

  • Apple TV HD (AppleTV5,3, tvOS 26.6) is set up and on Not a meth lab at 192.168.1.198, the same subnet as the Mac and Grayson’s phone. Phone remote works.
  • Companion is NOT paired again yet. A PIN was on screen and the session timed out. Re-pair with the full-scan + filter script at <scratchpad>/pair.py; identifier is 75106BB8-FEB9-46C6-ACAF-A26C4005623A.
  • ~/.local/bin/appletv still points at the OLD identifier and apple-tv.local on the sharing segment. Update ATV_ID and ATV_HOST before use.
  • The identifier changes on every reset and on completing setup. It went 5B95125F (original) to 86F85EF9 (post-reset, pre-setup) to 75106BB8 (post-setup). Never hardcode it.

What cost the most time, so nobody repeats it

  • tvOS Setup Assistant accepts Companion HID keypresses and silently discards them. pyatv reports success because the device ACKs the event. Verified asleep and awake. Claude said pairing would let it drive setup without a remote, never tested that, and Grayson reset on that assurance. That was the mistake of the session: verify the recovery path before the irreversible step, not just current-state capability.
  • cfgutil prepare --skip-all reports success and skips nothing when the device is unsupervised. The skip flags need supervision.
  • cfgutil erase DOES work unsupervised. install-profile does not: it fails with "User interaction on the device is required" unless supervised.
  • No CEC rescue here: the display is an LG monitor, and monitors rarely implement it. iPhone proximity setup never offered its card either.
  • tvOS greys out Wi-Fi setup while ethernet is connected, so the handover has to be done with ethernet out.

Internet Sharing, and the Pi outage it caused

  • Ticking “Ethernet” in the Sharing pane silently pulled in six extra interfaces (en10 en6 en7 en8 en9 bridge0). macOS rebuilt the bridge, abandoned 192.168.2.0/24 and renumbered to 192.168.234.0/24, deleting 192.168.2.1. That is the Pi’s gateway and cleetusd’s wired listener, so the wall panel died.
  • Fixed by trimming :NAT:SharingDevices to en13, en0 and pinning SharingNetworkNumberStart back to 192.168.2.1 in /Library/Preferences/SystemConfiguration/com.apple.nat.plist.
  • killall InternetSharing does NOT reload the config. The daemon dies, configd does not restart it, and bridge100 keeps its stale address and members. Only an OFF/ON toggle in System Settings rebuilds the bridge from the plist.
  • Pi is healthy again: 192.168.2.2, panel /controls about 8 ms.
  • When checking the Pi, probe the right ports: merossd is 127.0.0.1:8772 on the Pi (loopback, closed from the Mac is NORMAL), cleetusd’s wired listener is 192.168.2.1:8767 on the Mac, panel is 192.168.2.2:8791.

RESOLVED, same night

  • It is renamed GP TV, set up, on the house Wi-Fi at 192.168.1.198. Both Companion AND AirPlay are paired (creds in ~/.pyatv.conf), so playing, app, device_state and artwork work for the first time. AirPlay 7000 only opens once setup completes, which is why it could never be paired before.
  • ~/.local/bin/appletv was rewritten to use pyatv name-based scan (-n "GP TV") instead of a pinned address and companion-only manual mode. The box is on a routable network now so plain scanning works, and this picks up every paired protocol. Round trip is about 3.3 s.
  • Trap: atvremote --id <identifier> does NOT match this device even though a plain scan lists that exact identifier. It fails with "Could not find any Apple TV on current network". Use -n/name. The same bug bit the pairing script; it needed a full scan plus a filter on all_identifiers.
  • Trap: the mDNS hostname is NOT the display name. Renaming it to "GP TV" gave the hostname GP-TV-D0034B4361C2.local (it embeds the ethernet MAC), not gp-tv.local. It also still answers on a stale link-local 169.254.x record that works until the wired link goes, so prefer a routable address.
  • Peer-to-peer wireless being on changes nothing now that phone, Mac and Apple TV share 192.168.1.x. It is what let the phone reach it ACROSS subnets earlier.

Using it as a second display for the Mac (asked 23 Sep)

  • Yes, via AirPlay, not Sidecar (Sidecar is iPad only). Control Center > Screen Mirroring > GP TV, then System Settings > Displays > Use as: Extended Display.
  • Hard ceiling is 1080p60, confirmed from the box’s own /info (receiverHDRCapability = 1080p60). It is an Apple TV HD, so the LG monitor’s native resolution does not matter on this path.
  • It is compressed video over Wi-Fi: cursor lag, soft text, untrustworthy color. Fine for docs, chat, terminal, a parked browser. Bad for video, timing-sensitive work or anything visual.
  • Prefer a cable if the monitor can reach the Mac Studio. Native resolution, no latency, no 1080p cap. AirPlay only earns its place if the display is out of cable reach.

Still open

  • en0 is still an Internet Sharing member with nothing on it. Revert if not wanted.
  • A remote may still be worth having: IR needs no pairing, so it works at Setup Assistant, which nothing software-side can drive.

The Pi panel is now one studio panel: clock, room, RuView, markets (22 Sep 2026)

What is on the glass

  • The devices scene (what the Pi boots to, :8791) is redesigned in a dragonfly.xyz look: black, one orange #fa4c14, light serif clock, small mono labels, corner letters and crosshairs. Numbered cards from the DeerFlow reference, segmented bars from the F1 one.
  • 01 Time: clock, seconds bar, date. Beside it presence (desk from HID idle, camera state) and how many devices are on.
  • 02 Markets: SPY, NVDA, AAPL with price, change and an intraday line against the previous close.
  • 03 Room control: same four stations and every old guard: Phone And Pi locked (server refuses too), speakers arm-then-fire, rows settle on the readback, board dims with a banner past 60 s.
  • 04 RuView: ASCII waterfall of each node’s motion trace, per-node bars, dBm, fps, fusion. People reads NO FIX on purpose (the server’s head count is fabricated).
  • The old page is still at http://192.168.2.2:8791/classic.

Where it lives

  • Source ~/cleetusd/pi-devices-dash/: panel.html (becomes public/index.html on the Pi), classic.html, server.mjs, public/fonts/ (Newsreader, Doto, Geist Mono, served locally so the panel never depends on Google Fonts). Deploy: PI=gpope04@192.168.2.2 ./deploy.sh. Commit 5d73181.
  • New server routes: /quotes (Yahoo chart API, no key, fetched on the Pi, cached 30 s while the market is open and 5 min otherwise, one refresh in flight) and /ruview (loopback proxy to ruview-dash :8790, so no new poll of the Mac).
  • Page loops each await themselves, so a slow endpoint cannot stack requests (the leak that froze the Pi on 16 Sep). Service RSS after deploy: 77 MB.

Traps found

  • Every DNS lookup on the Pi costs a flat 5 s, v4 and v6 alike: the first resolver in /etc/resolv.conf times out. Probably the real reason the tunnel measured 5.4 s from the Pi. Fixed 23 Sep; see “Pi DNS” below. Yahoo fetches use a 15 s timeout meanwhile.
  • deploy.sh used systemctl --user enable --now, which does not restart a running unit. Every past re-deploy only updated static files. Now it restarts. Check the new routes answer, not is-active.
  • The repo copy of the old page was stale (missing the speaker guard and offline tiles). Synced from the Pi before renaming it classic.html.
  • Doto’s full stop is a four-dot cross that reads as “+” (“773+38”), so price decimals are drawn as a small square. Pi Chromium 147 also draws Doto thinner than Mac Chrome.
  • An empty fusion window reports median 0 ms; the panel shows a dash then, not “0 ms”.

Verified

  • Screenshot of the real glass via grim. Every feed cut on purpose: each card says NO FEED and the footer names what is down. Locked outlet write goes through the new server and is refused. A real device toggle from the new page was not tested.

Usability pass, same night (commit b43d033)

  • Bigger type (labels 10 to 11 px, tile names 17 px), a wider ADJ strip with an icon, and more height for room control.
  • Per-station ALL ON / ALL OFF. ALL OFF arms first (tap Confirm within 3.5 s). It skips locked, guarded and offline tiles, so it never touches the Pi’s power or the speakers. Writes go one at a time.
  • Adjust sheet: five brightness presets (5 to 100%) and five whites (Candle to Cool) as 56 px buttons; the slider stays. It closes itself after 25 s untouched.
  • Night veil, 23:00 to 06:30: the panel dims, and the first tap only wakes it for 90 s, so waking the screen in the dark never switches a lamp.
  • Tested in Playwright with /controls/set blocked: wake tap, arm tap, arm timeout and ADJ all sent zero writes. Glass screenshot confirmed the veil on the Pi.

Pi DNS: FIXED 23 Sep, 10:24

  • Resolvers handed out by the Mac’s Internet Sharing: 192.168.2.1 first, then the Mac’s IPv6 link-local. Probed from the Pi with a raw DNS query: 192.168.2.1 times out; the IPv6 one and 1.1.1.1 answer in about 27 ms. So every lookup waits a full 5 s timeout, then succeeds on IPv6.
  • On the Mac, something listens on UDP 53 but does not answer over IPv4, not even on 127.0.0.1. The Mac-side fix is toggling Internet Sharing off and on, which renumbered the bridge and stranded the Pi last time (section 152). Not done.
  • Pi-side fix: ~/fix-dns.sh on the Pi sets IPv4 DNS on “Wired connection 1” to 1.1.1.1 and 9.9.9.9 (ipv4.ignore-auto-dns yes), keeps the working IPv6 resolver as fallback, nmcli device reapply eth0 (no link bounce), then times a lookup. Undo line is in the script.
  • It needs root: nmcli modify is auth for gpope04 in polkit, and sudo asks for a password. Run it in a real Terminal: ssh -t gpope04@192.168.2.2 'sudo ~/fix-dns.sh'. The chat ! prompt cannot answer a password prompt.
  • Grayson ran it 23 Sep 10:24. Verified from the Pi: yahoo 18 ms, me.cleetusai.com 51 ms, github.com 52 ms, apple.com 22 ms (all were ~5000). The tunnel went from 5.4 s to 0.15 s: the “tunnel is slow from the Pi” figure in older sections was this DNS timeout all along.
  • Surprise: /etc/resolv.conf now says nameserver 100.100.100.100 (“generated by tailscale”), not 1.1.1.1. Tailscale accept-dns was already on; when NM reapplied, Tailscale took the file over. That is fine: tailscale dns status shows no tailnet resolvers, so it forwards to the system default, which is now 1.1.1.1, 9.9.9.9, then the Mac’s IPv6. tailscaled is enabled and active. If tailscaled ever dies, DNS on the Pi dies with it; the escape hatch is tailscale set --accept-dns=false (no sudo, operator is gpope04).

The Pi, on one card (checked live 23 Sep)

  • Box: Raspberry Pi 4, 4 GB, Debian 13 Trixie, labwc (Wayland), Chromium 147 as the kiosk. Hostname pi; tailnet creo-bots-pi 100.68.53.19. Up 16 h, 1.1 of 3.8 GB used.
  • Reach it: ssh gpope04@192.168.2.2 (wired, via the Mac’s Internet Sharing; the Mac is 192.168.2.1 and is its gateway) or the tailnet IP. Key auth; sudo needs a password, so everything unattended is a systemctl --user unit (linger is on).
  • Screen: 1024x600 DZX Z3 on HDMI-A-1, mounted upside down; kanshi applies transform 180. Truth is a screenshot: XDG_RUNTIME_DIR=/run/user/1000 WAYLAND_DISPLAY=wayland-0 grim /tmp/panel.png.
  • Services on the Pi: devices-dash :8791 (the studio panel, user unit, RSS ~95 MB); ruview-dash :8790 (user unit, heap capped at 384 MB because MemoryMax is not enforced for user units here); protocol-scene :8080, protocol-kiosk, protocol-leds, protocol-mpv (system units, Restart=always, so kill = reload); robot daemon :8731 and the poker agent :3000 (Tailscale Funnel).
  • Scenes: boots to devices (the studio panel). Switch with curl -X POST localhost:8080/scene/<name>; that restarts the kiosk (~15 s). protocol-mpv shows failed after any switch to a browser scene: mpv exits 4 when stopped. Harmless; a video scene starts it again.
  • Where data comes from: the panel proxies /controls to cleetusd on the Mac (192.168.2.1:8767 first, me.cleetusai.com fallback), with the bearer from /opt/protocol-pi/secrets/ruview.token (never in the page). /ruview reads ruview-dash on loopback; /quotes hits Yahoo from the Pi.
  • Correction to section 152: merossd runs on the Mac (127.0.0.1:8772, a Python LaunchAgent), not on the Pi. Nothing listens on 8772 on the Pi.
  • “Phone And Pi” does not power the Pi (a power-cycle of it left the Pi’s uptime unchanged, 16 Sep). It stays locked on the panel anyway.
  • If the Pi seems frozen: it was memory last time, not a hang. Check free -m and the node RSS before power-cycling.

RuView can say where Grayson is, from the three boards alone (24 to 25 Sep 2026)

Short version. Every earlier verdict that RuView cannot see a person (sections 130 and on) was measured on the wrong data: the collector kept 7 summary numbers per frame and the server keeps none of the per-subcarrier shape. Position lives in that shape. With the raw channel kept, every radio used as a light source and a labelled walk, a model named the right spot on 72.7% of visits it never saw (chance 16.7%). It then failed within minutes of the walk (said “out” while Grayson typed), and was retrained on self-labelled desk time. It is live, spot-level, and reported with its measured accuracy. No line crossing is used anywhere.

Measured

  • Walk, 23:22 24 Sep (3 rounds x 6 spots x 45 s, spoken by the Mac, shuffled order): held-out-round accuracy 72.7%, rounds 70/79/70%. Label-shuffle controls 13.1%. Bed 85, middle 94, desk 88, out 91, keys 42 (read as desk, about 1 m away), closet 36 (read as out, it is by the door). Any one board alone: 30 to 57%. All 12 views: 73%.
  • Then it broke. From 23:41, with Grayson typing, it said “out” in 13 to 20 of every 20 windows. The live path was not the cause: the offline pipeline gave the same answer. The walk trained every “out” block with the door shut and in-room blocks with it (probably) open; opening it helped only partly. One 14-minute walk memorises that walk.
  • Retrained on everything labelled (walk plus any 3 s window where HID idle stayed under 8 s, labelled desk), scored on held-out 10-minute time blocks: 60% overall, desk 81 to 82% (the old model: 31% on those windows), bed 73 to 82%. Keys, closet and out are weak (12 to 24%) because one walk is their only data.
  • Confound check, live: the new model could have learned “not the walk” instead of “desk”. Grayson lay on the bed: bed 80 of 80 readings. At the desk: desk 30 of 40.
  • Calibration: shrinkage LDA claims ~100% while right ~71% (ECE 0.29). One temperature (about 10 to 13) fixes it (ECE 0.03). Sticky smoothing on calibrated output: flicker 5.7 to 3.5 switches a minute for 1.6 points. Model variants (logistic, trees, PCA, 5 s windows, level and motion features) all landed within noise of the simple one, so the simple one stays.
  • Rejected: background subtraction scored 77%, but that was leakage (the held-out round’s own out windows made the reference); with a reference 5 minutes stale it fell to 53.8%.

What was built

  • Multi-illuminator firmware on all three boards (v0.8.4 build, ~/RuView/firmware/esp32-csi-node/main/csi_collector.c, uncommitted): classifies each frame by transmitter BEFORE the rate gate (the old order starved the wanted source), 25 Hz per transmitter, appends the 6-byte transmitter MAC to each UDP frame. Only the primary source feeds on-board presence. Each board now hears the mango, the house AP and the other two boards: 12 views. Flash backups: ~/RuView-backups/node{1,2,3}-flash-20260924.bin.
  • Presence thresholds pinned in NVS from the empty window (mean + 3 sd): node 1 25.20, node 2 27.45, node 3 27.02, so a reboot with someone present no longer poisons them.
  • Mango: back on channel 1, OFDM-only basic rates (WirelessMode=7, BasicRate=336), fully wireless since 25 Sep: its own AP ruview-tx on 192.168.8.1 with DHCP, and a Wi-Fi uplink (apcli0) to the house network at 192.168.1.204 that NATs node 2’s data to the Mac (192.168.1.134). SSH: root@192.168.1.204 (firewall rule mac_ssh allows only the Mac). Its LAN Ethernet port is removed from br-lan, so no cable is needed and its DHCP can never reach the Pi’s bridge again. /etc/ruview-tx.sh (started from /etc/rc.local via start-stop-daemon) pings every RuView board on it at 50/s so it transmits frames that carry CSI (beacons carry none). Node 2 is on ruview-tx at 192.168.8.150, target 192.168.1.134. Backups: /etc/*.bak-2026092*.
  • Server (~/RuView/v2, uncommitted): RUVIEW_UDP_TEE raw copy to :5006, RUVIEW_EDGE_PRESENCE_GATE (a board’s own empty verdict vetoes the stock classifier), --udp-allow now includes 192.168.2.0/24.
  • ~/cleetusd/bin/: ruview-raw.py (agent com.cleetus.ruview-raw, 1-minute npz in roomwatch/csi-raw/, about 650 MB a day, forwards to :5007), ruview-locate-session.py (spoken walk), ruview-locate-test.py (held-out-round scoring, controls, --synthetic plants, --score-saved for day-2 tests), ruview-locate-bench.py, ruview-locate-train.py (walk + HID desk + truth-empty, time-blocked CV), and ruview-locate-live.py + .html (agent com.cleetus.ruview-locate).
  • Live map: http://127.0.0.1:8793 (/state JSON), 4 Hz, heatmap, a moving dot and trail. The dot blends spot positions, so between spots it means uncertain.
  • Cleetus: room_sense now returns a ROOM LOCATOR section (spot, calibrated probability, held-out accuracy, limits) and /room carries location. The server’s person count, breathing and heart rate are still rejected.
  • Nightly retrain 04:40 (com.cleetus.ruview-locate-train), refuses to replace the live model if held-out accuracy drops more than 5 points. Log: ~/Library/Logs/ruview-locate-train.log.

Traps found

  • Pooling transmitters is fatal: each board heard two populations whose profiles differ by 0.5 log units, burying any body. The analyser caught it only because a planted 3% signal scored chance.
  • ESP-IDF 5.4.1 export.sh fails under Xcode Python 3.9 (ruamel_yaml metadata reads as missing). Reinstalled on Homebrew python@3.12; use . ~/esp/idf-env.sh.
  • plutil -replace ProgramArguments.N inserted instead of replacing; the server died on a stray argument. Always plutil -p after.
  • Both cameras (C920 and Brio) now hang with no frames, so roomwatch has no camera. Needs a replug.

Next (tomorrow’s pass)

  • Day-2 test first: one round of the walk, scored with ruview-locate-test.py <session> --score-saved against the model as it stands, no retraining. That is the honest number.
  • Then walk again with the door both open and shut inside each spot, more rounds, then retrain.
  • Leave the room for 10+ minutes with a truth window open, so “out” gets real data (the 17:41 empty window is still open and must be closed at the time Grayson came back).
  • Two-person walk. Raw CSI retention policy. Commit the RuView firmware and server changes.

The desk camera now teaches the RF locator; one command for the daily pass (25 Sep 2026)

Why. The RF locator (section 154) only learns spots it has labels for. HID labels only ever say “desk”, and walks are short. The C920 at the desk looks across the room: door and closet side, the middle, the bed. Those are exactly the spots the locator is weakest on (12 to 24%).

Cameras back

  • Both cameras hung with zero frames (roomwatch camera_unavailable since 23 Sep 00:39). After a replug the Brio worked; the C920 worked only once plugged directly into the Mac, not through the hub. AirPad restarted, frame.jpg 503 to 200, roomwatch heartbeats real again. Keep cameras off the hub.

How the camera labels (observe now, label later)

  • ~/cleetusd/bin/ruview-camlabel.py run (agent com.cleetus.ruview-camlabel, runs in the studio-locate venv for MediaPipe): every 2 s it reads AirPad’s frame.jpg (no second camera open), runs MediaPipe pose (~/cleetusd/models/pose/pose_landmarker_full.task), and appends numbers only, no images to roomwatch/camera-obs.jsonl: hip and shoulder position in the frame, lying or upright, visibility, brightness. Below brightness 12/255 it logs “dark” and detects nothing.
  • No hand-drawn image regions. ruview-camlabel.py calibrate <walk> fits the camera-position to spot map from a walk (the walk says where the body really was), scores it on held-out rounds, and saves roomwatch/camera-spotmap.json with that score.
  • ruview-locate-train.py uses camera labels only from a calibrated map, only at 80%+ confidence, only when exactly one body is seen, and never inside walks or HID-desk time. A body seen during an “out” block is never a label, and “nobody seen” is never turned into “empty” (the view does not cover the desk).
  • Tested on a synthetic walk with a known answer: calibrated to 100% on held-out rounds and labelled only bed, middle and closet. That test caught a real bug first: frames at block edges spilled into the next block, so “out” had learned two bodies and could have labelled a person as out. Fixed. Not yet run on a real walk; there is no calibrated map until the next one.

Walk changes

  • The first walk shut the door for every “out” block, and the model learned “door shut = out”. The walk now sets the door open in odd rounds, shut in even rounds, for every spot including out, and logs door per block. Held-out-round scoring then also tests the other door state.

The daily pass, one command

  • ~/cleetusd/bin/ruview-locate-day.sh [rounds=2] [block=45]: spoken walk, then score it against the saved model before anything learns from it (the honest day-over-day number), then camera calibration from the same walk, then the guarded retrain, then restart the live map (:8793).

156. Sleep watch started; Cleetus Security sentry designed and its core written (25 Sep, ~01:15-02:00)

Sleep watch (learns what "in bed" and "asleep" look like)

  • ~/cleetusd/bin/sleepwatch.py, LaunchAgent com.cleetus.sleepwatch (KeepAlive), a row every 10 s to roomwatch/sleepwatch/samples-DAY.jsonl: locator posterior, HID idle, Hue lamps on, latest roomwatch camera heartbeat. Raw CSI is already kept by ruview-raw.py, so stillness is computed after the fact.
  • sleepwatch mark bed|asleep|up (symlinked to /usr/local/bin) or sleepwatch truth 2026-09-24 bed=01:40 asleep=02:05 up=09:30. Labels come ONLY from Grayson, never from the sensors being scored.
  • bin/sleepwatch-report.py, agent com.cleetus.sleepwatch-report daily 14:05 with a push: per-minute timeline, class profiles, leave-one-NIGHT-out logistic model. READY rule (baseline.json): 5+ nights with truth, every held-out night in-bed accuracy ≥ 95%, bed and get-up times within 15 min, sleep onset within 20 min on 4 of 5. Until then it keeps watching.
  • CSI motion dropped about 5x at 23:29 on 24 Sep. That matches the multi-illuminator flash at 23:22, so nothing before the flash is comparable. The 0.1-0.5 Hz "breath" feature reads 0.3-0.4 all evening: experimental, probably noise.
  • Locator pinned: from at least 01:13 the :8793 model output 100% "keys" every window (raw keys 0.62, every other spot at the clip floor) with HID idle and a black camera. Out of distribution, not a location. Do not trust it as a bed signal until it moves again; the day pass (walk, then --score-saved) will say how far it has drifted.

Cleetus Security sentry

  • Plan, Q&A decisions and priced shopping list: Obsidian 30-Projects/Cleetus/cleetus-security.md. Living-area unit, multi-unit later; other people live here and visitors have keys; budget ~$250-400; no triggers yet, so the unit detects on its own.
  • RuView is NOT the trigger (bedroom only, presence measured useless). The sentry locates people itself with an HLK-LD2450 radar, confirms them with a camera/thermal, aims a pan/tilt night camera, and pushes a live stream. It never calls anyone.
  • Code ~/cleetus-security (own repo): LD2450 parser (format checked against the Hi-Link manual), one house frame + fit_pose from a 4-spot walk, Kalman tracker, policy (radar never alerts alone; known face = no push; stranger born at an entry at night = URGENT; faceless figure from the bedroom hall = CHECK), simulator. 11 tests pass; the pose-fit test caught a sign error first.
  • Prices: Pi 5 8 GB is $175 now (RAM price rises). v1 about $415, the ideal build about $576.

The sentry plan in full (source of truth: Obsidian 30-Projects/Cleetus/cleetus-security.md)

What it is. A stationary unit in the main living area. When something moves while the house is armed it points a night-vision camera at the target, keeps it in frame as it moves, works out housemate vs stranger, and for a stranger pushes a live stream to Grayson's phone. Grayson decides whether to call the police; the bot never calls anyone and never confronts anyone.

Decisions from the Q&A

QuestionAnswerWhat it changes
WhereMain living area, scalable to more unitsOne unit covering entry + living room; every unit reports in one shared house coordinate frame
Who elseOther people live here; regular visitorsIdentity is the core problem: faces, visitor time windows, and where a track was born
Budget~$250-400 first unitv1 list below lands ~$415 (Pi prices rose); drop thermal to get under $400
TriggersNothing yet: RuView + the bot itselfThe bot detects on its own; RuView is optional extra evidence later

Why RuView is not the trigger

  • The boards are in the bedroom; they see nothing in the living area.
  • Its presence/alert layer is measured not to work (motion AUC 0.60; edge-vitals presence is 95% false whether empty or occupied).
  • The fingerprint locator is 6-spot classification for one room, and tonight it was pinned at "keys". Not a coordinate feed.
  • So the sentry locates people itself with an HLK-LD2450 24 GHz radar (x/y in mm per person, in the dark). RuView joins later as one voter on the same bus (bedroom occupancy, "Grayson is asleep" from the sleep watch).

How it works

  1. Wake: radar track confirmed (4+ hits, survives 2 s dropouts, single-frame blips never confirm) while armed.
  2. Aim: the unit's pose in the house is known, so radar x/y becomes pan/tilt. The camera person detector then does fine aiming (closed loop), since radar is ~0.1-0.2 m and a face at 5 m needs better.
  3. Verify human: radar alone NEVER alerts (fans, curtains, HVAC). Camera person detector or thermal must agree within 6 s.
  4. Identify: face match against housemates + in-window visitors (existing YuNet+SFace gallery, re-enrolled in IR since night faces are grayscale).
  5. Decide: known face = logged, no push. Away mode, unknown = URGENT. Night mode, track born at an entry (front door, patio door, window) = URGENT. Night mode, born in the interior (bedroom hallway) with no face = CHECK (quiet push). A known face later downgrades the alert ("it's Alex").
  6. Stream: MediaMTX on the Pi serves WebRTC (<1 s latency) over Tailscale; the push opens it. 10 s pre-roll ring buffer so the clip starts before the trigger. Clips stay on the unit.

Arming: Away (no household phones on Wi-Fi, or manual); Night (schedule first, then auto when the sleep watch says Grayson is asleep, which is what the sleep baseline is for); Disarmed by default.

Multi-unit: each unit publishes tracks in house coordinates over MQTT cleetus/security/<unit>/tracks; mounting pose fitted once from a 4-spot calibration walk (fit_pose, which also resolves the radar x sign). The hub (cleetusd) fuses tracks so a person walking room to room is one incident handed between units.

Shopping list v1 (prices checked 25 Sep 2026, ~$415 + tax)

Upgrades later: Waveshare 2-Axis Pan-Tilt Camera Module $109.99 (2x ST3215 12-bit feedback servos); Arducam B0270 IMX477 auto IR-cut $64.99 (sharper faces, needs the Waveshare pan-tilt; no IMX708 board with auto IR-cut exists); Geekworm X1200 UPS $45 + 2x Molicel P28A $11.98; Slamtec RPLIDAR C1 $69 only if radar disappoints. Ideal build total ~$576.

Gotchas: Pi 5 camera cables differ from Pi 4. One PCIe lane, so AI HAT and NVMe don't share cleanly: clips on SD or USB 3 SSD. LD2450 = 5 V power, 3.3 V UART, 256000 baud, x/y sign-magnitude with top bit = POSITIVE (the csRon GitHub decoder gets X backwards; ours matches the Hi-Link manual's worked example).

Build phases

  • P0, done 25 Sep: parser, house frame + pose fit, tracker, policy, simulator, 11 tests. python -m sentry.sim intruder|housemate|fan: stranger via front door URGENT at 5.9 s; housemate KNOWN, no push; fan RADAR_ONLY, no alert.
  • P1, radar on the bench: LD2450 on USB-serial on the Mac; confirm frames and x sign; log a walk. Before the Pi arrives.
  • P2, the unit: Pi 5 + HAT + camera + servos; radar aim, then camera closed loop. Metric: target in the middle third of the frame ≥ 90% of a walk.
  • P3, identity: IR face enrolment, visitor windows, real entry/interior zones. Metric: a week armed at night, zero URGENT on housemates.
  • P4, phone: push with live WebRTC link, pre-roll clips, ack button, Night auto-arm from the sleep watch.
  • P5: unit two.

Waiting on Grayson

  • Housemates' names and consent to face enrolment; they must know the camera exists.
  • Buy the v1 list (or say drop thermal to stay under $400).

157. cleetus in the terminal: a Claude-Code-style coding agent on the local model (25 Sep)

  • ~/cleetusd/bin/cleetus.mjs, symlinked to /usr/local/bin/cleetus. Type cleetus in any directory; cleetus --self opens in ~/cleetusd so Cleetus works on its own code. -p "prompt" one-shot, -c continue, --yolo, --model, --think; in-session /clear /model /think /yolo /cwd /exit.
  • Tools: read_file, list_dir, grep, glob, edit_file, write_file, bash, all relative to the directory you are in. Reads are free; every edit/write shows a diff and every non-read-only shell command asks y/n/a(lways). Pre-edit copies go to ~/.cleetus/backups/, sessions to ~/.cleetus/sessions/. Loads CLEETUS.md / CLAUDE.md / AGENTS.md from the project (never ~/AGENTS.md).
  • Model: qwen3.8-27b-heretic:q8_0 (tools + thinking, 262k native, run at num_ctx 32768; override with CLEETUS_MODEL / CLEETUS_CTX). ~16 tok/s.
  • Verified: fixed a planted off-by-one on its own (ran the failing test, read both files, edited, re-ran: pass) in 48 s cold; declining a write left no file and it asked what to do instead.
  • Found: the daemon's configured model laguna-xs-2.1:q8_0 (LLM_MODEL in cleetus.env) is NOT installed in Ollama any more; only qwen3.8-27b-heretic, qwen2.5vl:7b and lfm2.5:8b are. cleetusd calls to its default model will fail until that is repointed or re-pulled.
  • Fixed ~/.zshrc line 1: the old cleetus alias (to a deleted ~/cleetus/cleetus.py) was glued to the brew shellenv eval on one line. Backup ~/.zshrc.bak-20260925.
